With the wisdom of thy good Spirit teach him to do thy will, and take not thy succour from him, but grant unto him, with me thine unprofitable servant, to become an inheritor of thine everlasting bliss, because thou art blessed and glorified forever, Amen.”

===Chinese===
说到这里，王的儿子痛楚悲愤，眼泪夺眶而出，受不住与这位慈父、这位至善的教师分离。「我的父啊，」他说，「谁能填补你的位置呢？我到哪里再寻得一个像你的人，作我灵魂救恩的牧人和引导者呢？我失去了你，还能得着什么安慰呢？看哪，我这恶而悖逆的仆人，你把我领回神面前，安放在儿子和后嗣的地位上！你寻找我这迷失流荡在山上、作了各样恶兽之掳食的羊，把我收拢在从未走迷的羊群中间。你将通往真理的直路指示我，领我出离黑暗和死荫之地，又把我脚步的行径从那滑跌、致命、弯曲而崎岖的路上改换过来，将伟大而奇妙的福分施行给我——那福分何等超绝，言语述说不尽。因着我这微小的人，愿你在神手中领受的恩赏何等浩大！又愿主补足我感谢中的缺欠——因为在恩赐的赏报上，惟独他所赐给爱他之人的，总过于他们所配得的！」

说到这里，巴拉阿姆截住了他的哀声，站起来祷告，举起双手，说：「神啊，我们主耶稣基督的父，你曾使那一度昏暗了的得着光照，又将这可见与不可见的造化从无有中领出，又使你这手所造的归回，不容我们随自己的愚妄而行：我们感谢你，又感谢你的智慧与大能、我们的主耶稣基督——你曾借着他创造诸世界，叫我们从跌倒处起来，赦免我们的过犯，从流迷中恢复我们，从被掳中赎出我们，借你儿子我们主的宝血叫我们从死里活过来。我呼求你，并呼求你的独生子，呼求圣灵。求你垂看你这属灵的羊——他借着我这不配的仆人，成了献与你的祭物——求你以你的大能和恩典使他的魂成圣。求你眷顾这棵葡萄树，就是你的圣灵所栽种的，赐它结果子，结仁义的果子。求你坚固他，在他身上坚定你的约，搭救他脱离魔鬼的诡诈。求你以你善灵的智慧指教他遵行你的旨意，不要将你的救助从他撤去；又赐他与我这无益的仆人一同作你永远福乐的承受者；因为你是可称颂、得荣耀，直到永远的。阿们。」

===Original===
When that he had ended his prayer, he turned him round and embraced Ioasaph, now a son of his heavenly father, wishing him eternal peace and salvation, and he departed out of the palace, and went his way, rejoicing and giving thanks to God, who had well ordered his steps for good.

===Chinese===
他祷告完了，就转过身来，拥抱约阿萨弗——他如今已是那位天父的儿子了——愿他得着永远的平安与救恩。随后巴拉阿姆出了宫殿，上路而去，一路欢欢喜喜地称谢神，因为神使他的脚步稳妥，引他行在美善之中。

## XXII / 二十二

===Original===
After Barlaam was gone forth, Ioasaph gave himself unto prayer and bitter tears, and said, "O God, haste thee to help me: O Lord, make speed to help me, because the poor hath committed himself unto thee; thou art the helper of the orphan. Look upon me, and have mercy upon me; thou who willest have all men to be saved and to come unto the knowledge of the truth, save me, and strengthen me, unworthy though I be, to walk the way of thy holy commandments, for I am weak and miserable, and not able to do the thing that is good. But thou art mighty to save me, who sustainest and holdest together all things visible and invisible. Suffer me not to walk after the evil will of the flesh, but teach me to do thy will, and preserve me unto thine eternal and blissful life. O Father, Son, and Holy Ghost, the consubstantial and undivided Godhead, I call upon thee and glorify thee. Thou art praised by all creation; thou art glorified by the intelligent powers of the Angels forever and ever. Amen."

From that time forth he kept himself with all vigilance, seeking to attain purity of soul and body, and living in continency and prayers and intercessions all night long. In the daytime he was often interrupted by the company of his fellows, and at times by a visit from the king, or a call to the king's presence, but the night would then make good the shortcomings of the day, whilst he stood, in prayer and weeping until daybreak, calling upon God. Whence in him was fulfilled the saying of the prophet, "In nights raise your hands unto holy things; and bless ye the Lord."

But Zardan observed Ioasaph's way of life, and was full of sorrow, and his soul was pierced with grievous anxieties; and he knew not what to do. At the last, worn down with pain, he withdrew to his own home, feigning sickness. When this had come to the knowledge of the king he appointed in his place another of his trusty men to minister unto his son, while he himself, being concerned for Zardan's health, sent a physician of reputation, and took great pains that he should be healed.

===Chinese===
巴拉阿姆出去之后，约阿萨弗便将自己献给祈祷与苦泪，说：「神啊，求你快快搭救我；耶和华啊，求你速速帮助我，因为无倚无靠的人将自己交托给你，你向来是孤儿的帮助。求你垂顾我，怜悯我；你愿意万人得救、明白真道，求你拯救我，坚固我——我虽不配——使我行走你圣诫命的道路，因为我是软弱可怜的，无力行那善的事。但你有大能拯救我；你扶持、维系着一切有形无形之物。求你不要容我随从肉体的恶欲而行，总要指教我遵行你的旨意，保守我进入你那永恒蒙福的生命。父啊，子啊，圣灵啊，同质而不可分的神性，我呼求你，荣耀你。你为一切受造之物所赞美，为众天使的明智权能所荣耀，直到永永远远。阿们。」

从那时起，他就以百般警醒保守自己，力求达到灵魂与身体的纯洁，过着节制的生活，整夜祈祷、代求。白昼之间，他常被同僚的相伴所搅扰，有时是王亲来看望，或是奉召来到王前；但到了夜间，他便补足白日的缺欠，站着祈祷流泪直到天明，呼求神。因此，先知的话便应验在他身上：「夜间要向圣所举起手来，称颂耶和华。」

扎尔丹却看出约阿萨弗的为人生活，满怀忧伤，魂被沉重的忧虑刺透，不知如何是好。末了，他被愁苦磨折得支持不住，便退回自己家中，诈称有病。这事传到王耳中，王便委派另一个亲信之人代替他伺候王子，自己则因挂念扎尔丹的病体，差遣一位有名望的医生，费心竭力要使他痊愈。

===Original===
The physician, seeing that Zardan was in favour with the king, attended him diligently, and, having right well judged his case, soon made this report to the king; "I have been unable to discover any root of disease in the man: wherefore I suppose that this weakness is to be ascribed to distress of spirit." But, on hearing his words, the king suspected that his son had been wroth with Zardan, and that this slight had caused his retirement. So, wishing to search the matter, he sent Zardan word, saying "Tomorrow I shall come to see thee, and judge of the malady that hath befallen thee."

But Zardan, on hearing this message, at daybreak wrapt his cloak around him and went to the king, and entered and fell in obeisance on the ground. The king spake unto him, "Why hast thou forced thyself to appear? I was minded to visit thee myself, and so make known to all my friendship for thee." He answered, "My sickness, O king, is no malady common to man; but pain of heart, arising from an anxious and careful mind, hath caused my body to suffer in sympathy. It had been folly in me, being as I am, not to attend as a slave before thy might, but to wait for thy Majesty to be troubled to come to me thy servant."

Then the king enquired after the cause of his despondency; Zardan answered and said, "Mighty is my peril, and mighty are the penalties that I deserve, and many deaths do I merit, for that I have been guilty of neglect of thy behests, and have brought on thee such sorrow as never before." Again said the king, "And of what neglect hast thou been guilty? And what is the dread that encompasseth thee?" "I have been guilty," said he, "of negligence in my close care of my lord thy son. There came an evil man and a sorcerer, and communicated to him the precepts of the Christian religion." Then he related to the king, point by point, the words which the old man spake with his son, and how gladly Ioasaph received his word, and how he had altogether become Christ's. Moreover he gave the old man's name, saying that it was Barlaam. Even before then the king had heard tell of Barlaam's ways and his extreme severity of life; but, when this came to the ears of the king, he was straightway astonied by the dismay that fell on him, and was filled with anger, and his blood well-nigh curdled at the tidings. Immediately he bade call one Araches, who held the second rank after the king, and was the chief in all his private councils: besides which the man was learned in star-lore. When he was come, with much despondency and dejection the king told him of that which had happened. He, seeing the king's trouble and confusion of mind, said, "O king, trouble and distress thyself no more. We are not without hope that the prince will yet change for the better: nay, I know for very certain that he will speedily renounce the teaching of this deceiver, and conform to thy will."

By these words then did Araches set the king in happier frame of mind; and they turned their thoughts to the thorough sifting of the matter. "This, O king," said Araches, "do we first of all. Make we haste to apprehend that infamous Barlaam. If we take him, I am assured that we shall not miss the mark, nor be cheated of our hope. Barlaam himself shall be persuaded, either by persuasion or by divers engines of torture, against his will to confess that he hath been talking falsely and at random, and shall persuade my lord, thy son, to cleave to his father's creed. But if we fail to take Barlaam, I know of an eremite, Nachor by name, in every way like unto him; it is impossible to distinguish the one from the other. He is of our opinion, and was my teacher in studies. I will give him the hint, and go by night, and tell him the full tale. Then will we blazon it abroad that Barlaam hath been caught; but we shall exhibit Nachor, who, calling himself Barlaam, shall feign that he is pleading the cause of the Christians and standing forth as their champion. Then, after much disputation, he shall be worsted and utterly discomfited. The prince, seeing Barlaam worsted, and our side victorious, will doubtless join the victors; the more so that he counteth it a great duty to reverence thy majesty, and do thy pleasure. Also the man who hath played the part of Barlaam shall be converted, and stoutly proclaim that he hath been in error."

===Chinese===
医生见扎尔丹正蒙王的恩宠，便殷勤照料他，把病情诊断得十分妥当，不久便向王如此回奏：「我在这人身上查不出任何病根，因此我推想，这软弱必是心灵愁苦所致。」王听了这话，却疑心是王子向扎尔丹动怒，这番冷遇叫他退居在家。于是王想要查究这事，便传话给扎尔丹说：「明天我要来看你，亲自察断你所患的病症。」

扎尔丹听了这传话，天一破晓便裹上外袍，去见王，进前俯伏在地叩拜。王对他说：「你何必勉强起身前来？我本想亲自探望你，好叫众人知道我对你的恩情。」他回答说：「王啊，我的病不是人间常患的病症，乃是出于忧惧焦思之心的心痛，累得身体也一同受苦。像我这样的人，不来在你大能跟前如奴仆伺候，反倒等我王屈尊枉驾到你仆人这里来，那就是愚妄了。」

于是王询问他何故如此沮丧；扎尔丹回答说：「我的危险何其大，我当受的刑罚何其重，我该死许多回，因为我犯了疏忽你命令的罪，给你带来了前所未有的忧伤。」王又说：「你犯了什么疏忽的罪？包围着你的恐惧又是什么？」他说：「我在悉心看顾我主你儿子的事上，有了疏忽。来了一个恶人、一个行邪术的，把基督徒信仰的规条传授给了他。」随后他便把老人与王子所谈的话，逐条述给王听，又讲约阿萨弗何等甘心领受他的话，如何已经完全属乎基督。他还说出那老人的名字，说名叫巴拉阿姆。其实在这以先，王早已听闻巴拉阿姆的行迹和他那极其严苛的生活；但这话传到王耳中，他立时被临到他的惊惶震住，满腔忿怒，听见这消息，血几乎凝结起来。他随即吩咐召一个名叫阿拉凯斯的人来——此人在王之下位居第二，是王一切机密议事的首臣，此外这人还通晓星象之学。他来到之后，王便满怀忧沮丧气，把所发生的事告诉他。他见王愁烦、心神迷乱，就说：「王啊，你不要再烦恼愁苦了。王子转好，我们并非没有指望；不但如此，我确切知道，他必快快弃绝这迷惑者的教训，顺从你的意愿。」

阿拉凯斯便用这些话使王的心境转为宽慰；二人又把心思转到对这事的彻底查究上。阿拉凯斯说：「王啊，我们要先这样行：赶紧去捉拿那声名狼藉的巴拉阿姆。我们若拿住他，我确信必不至失误，指望也不至落空。或是用劝诱，或是用种种刑具，总要打动巴拉阿姆本人，叫他违背己心，承认自己所说的话尽是虚假胡言，并劝我主你的儿子持守他父亲的信仰。但我们若捉不到巴拉阿姆，我认得一个隐修士，名叫纳霍尔，各面都与巴拉阿姆相像，两人简直无从分辨。他与我们见解相同，曾是我学业上的老师。我会给他暗中递话，夜里前去，把事情的原委全都告诉他。然后我们便四处张扬，说巴拉阿姆已被擒获；其实我们出示的是纳霍尔——他自称巴拉阿姆，装作是在为基督徒的事业辩护、挺身作他们的斗士。随后，经过多方争辩，他将被驳倒，彻底溃败。王子见巴拉阿姆败北、我方得胜，必无疑归从得胜的一方；何况他素来以敬畏你的威严、行你所喜悦的为大本分。再者，那扮演了巴拉阿姆的人随后也将回转过来，极力宣称自己从前是在错谬之中。」

===Original===
The king was delighted with his words, and rocked himself on idle hopes, and thought it excellent counsel. Thereupon, learning that Barlaam was but lately departed, he was zealous to take him prisoner. He therefore occupied most of the passes with troops and captains, and, himself, mounting his chariot, gave furious chase along the one road of which he was especially suspicious, being minded to surprise Barlaam at all costs. But though he toiled by the space of six full days, his labour was but spent in vain. Then he himself remained behind in one of his palaces situate in the country, but sent forward Araches, with horsemen not a few, as far as the wilderness of Senaar, in quest of Barlaam. When Araches arrived in that place, he threw all the neighbour folk into commotion: and when they constantly affirmed that they had never seen the man, he went forth into the desert places, for to hunt out the Faithful. When he had gone through a great tract of desert, and made the circuit of the fells around, and journeyed afoot over untrodden and pathless ravines, he and his hosts arrived at a plateau. Standing thereon, he descried at the foot of the mountain a company of hermits a-walking. Straightway at their governor's word of command all his men ran upon them in breathless haste, vying one with another, who should arrive first. When they arrived, they came about the monks like so many dogs, or evil beasts that plague mankind. And they seized these men of reverend mien and mind, that bare on their faces the hallmark of their hermit life, and haled them before the governor; but the monks showed no sign of alarm, no sign of meanness or sullenness, and spake never a word. Their leader and captain bore a wallet of hair, charged with the relics of some holy Fathers departed this life.

When Araches beheld them, but saw no Barlaam﻿—for he knew him by sight﻿—he was overwhelmed with grief, and said unto them, "Where is that deceiver who hath led the king's son astray?" The bearer of the wallet answered, "He is not amongst us, God forbid! For, driven forth by the grace of Christ, he avoideth us; but amongst you he hath his dwelling." The governor said, "Thou knowest him then?" "Yea," said the hermit, "I know him that is called the deceiver, which is the devil, who dwelleth in your midst and is worshipped and served by you." The governor said, "It is for Barlaam that I make search, and I asked thee of him, to learn where he is." The monk answered, "And wherefore then spakest thou in this ambiguous manner, asking about him that had deceived the king's son? If thou wast seeking Barlaam, thou shouldest certainly have said, 'Where is he that hath turned from error and saved the king's son?' Barlaam is our brother and fellow-monk. But now for many days past we have not seen his face." Said Araches, "Show me his abode." The monk answered, "Had he wished to see you, he would have come forth to meet you. As for us, it is not lawful to make known to you his hermitage."

Thereupon the governor waxed full of indignation, and, casting a haughty and savage glance upon him, said, "Ye shall die no ordinary death, except ye immediately bring Barlaam before me." "What," said the monk, "seest thou in our case that should by its attractions cause us to cling to life, and be afraid of death at thy hands? Whereas we should the rather feel grateful to thee for removing us from life in the close adherence to virtue. For we dread, not a little, the uncertainty of the end, knowing not in what state death shall overtake us, lest perchance a slip of the inclination, or some despiteful dealing of the devil, may alter the constancy of our choice, and mis-persuade us to think or do contrary to our covenants with God. Wherefore abandon all hope of gaining the knowledge that ye desire, and shrink not to work your will. We shall neither reveal the dwelling-place of our brother, whom God loveth, although we know it, nor shall we betray any other monasteries unbeknown to ye. We will not endure to escape death by such cowardice. Nay, liefer would we die honourably, and offer unto God, after the sweats of virtue, the lifeblood of courage."

That man of sin could not brook this boldness of speech, and was moved to the keenest passion against this high and noble spirit, and afflicted the monks with many stripes and tortures. Their courage and nobility won admiration even from that tyrant. But, when after many punishments he failed to persuade them, and none of them consented to discover Barlaam, he took and ordered them to be led to the king, bearing with them the wallet with the relics, and to be beaten and shamefully entreated as they went.

===Chinese===
王听了他的话，大为欢喜，在虚妄的指望中自我陶醉，认为这是极好的计谋。那时他得知巴拉阿姆才离去不久，便热心要擒拿他为囚。于是他派军兵将校占据了大部分关口，自己登上战车，沿着他格外起疑的那条路穷追不舍，一心要不惜任何代价出其不意地擒获巴拉阿姆。然而他劳苦了足足六天，辛劳尽付徒然。于是他本人留驻在乡间的一座宫中，却差阿拉凯斯带着为数不少的骑兵，径往示拿旷野，搜寻巴拉阿姆。阿拉凯斯到了那地方，把邻近的居民全都搅得骚动不安；他们既一口咬定从未见过那人，他便出动进入旷野之地，要搜捕信徒。他穿过了大片旷野，绕行了四周的山冈，又徒步跋涉过人迹未到、无路可循的沟壑，他和他的队伍便来到一处高台。他站在上面，望见山脚下有一群隐修士正在行走。随统领一声令下，他手下的众人立时气喘吁吁地扑向他们，彼此争竞，看谁先到。到了那里，他们就围着那些修士，如同一群狗，或一群扰害人类的恶兽。他们抓住这些仪表心志俱可敬、脸上带着隐修生活印记的人，把他们拖到统领面前；修士们却毫无惊惶之色，毫无卑劣愠怒之态，一句也不言语。他们的领袖和首领带着一只毛布行囊，里面盛着几位已经离世的圣教父的圣髑。

阿拉凯斯见了他们，却看不见巴拉阿姆——因为他认得巴拉阿姆的相貌——就忧伤不胜，对他们说：「那引诱王子走入迷途的骗子在哪里？」背行囊的人回答说：「断乎不然，他不在我们中间！因为他被基督的恩典赶逐，就躲避我们；他倒是在你们中间安身。」统领说：「那么你认得他？」隐修士说：「是的，我认得那称为迷惑者的，就是魔鬼，他住在你们中间，受你们的敬拜和事奉。」统领说：「我搜寻的正是巴拉阿姆，我问你的就是他，要知道他在哪里。」修士回答说：「那你为何说得这般含糊，问那迷惑了王子的人呢？你若寻找的是巴拉阿姆，就本当这样说：『那转离错谬、救了王子的人在何处？』巴拉阿姆是我们的弟兄、我们的同道修士。只是至今已有多日，我们不曾见他的面。」阿拉凯斯说：「把他的住处指给我看。」修士回答说：「他若愿意见你们，早就会出来迎见你们了。至于我们，把他的隐修之所告诉你们，是不合法的。」

统领就大起义愤，向他投以倨傲凶残的一瞥，说：「你们若不立即把巴拉阿姆带到我面前，就必不得好死。」修士说：「你在我身上看见有什么可恋慕的，叫我们贪恋性命、怕死在你手中呢？你若叫我们在紧紧依傍德行之中离开今世，我们倒应当感谢你。因为我们实在深深惧怕结局的不可确知，不知道死亡要在怎样的境况中追上我们，惟恐或因心志的一时滑跌，或因魔鬼的某种毒害，改变了我们抉择的坚贞，诱使我们思想或行出与我们对神所立之约相背的事。所以，要得着你们所想望知道的事，这指望你们尽管丢弃；要行你们的意愿，也不必缩手。我们弟兄——神所爱的那位的居所，我们虽然知道，也决不显露；你们所不知道的其他修院，我们也不出卖。我们决不甘心以这等怯懦逃脱死亡。宁要死得光荣，在德行的劳苦之后，将勇毅的生命之血献上给神。」

那大罪人容不下这等放胆直言，对这高尚尊贵的心志动了极烈的忿怒，便用许多鞭笞和酷刑苦害那些修士。他们的勇气与高贵，连那暴君也不禁心生钦佩。但他多方刑罚之后，既未能说服他们，也无一人肯供出巴拉阿姆，便下令将他们解送到王那里去，随身带着那盛圣髑的行囊，一路受鞭打、受凌辱。

## XXIII / 二十三

===Original===
After many days Araches brought them to the king, and declared their case. Then he set them before the bitterly incensed king: and he, when he saw them, boiled over with fury and was like to one mad. He ordered them to be beaten without mercy, and, when he saw them cruelly mangled with scourges, could scarcely restrain his madness, and order the tormentors to cease. Then said he unto them, “Why bear ye about these dead men’s bones? If ye carry these bones through affection for those men to whom they belong, this very hour I will set you in their company, that ye may meet your lost friends and be duly grateful to me.” The captain and leader of that godly band, setting at naught the king’s threats, showing no sign of the torment that he had undergone, with free voice and radiant countenance that signified the grace that dwelt in his soul, cried out, “We carry about these clean and holy bones, O king, because we attest in due form our love of those marvellous men to whom they belong: and because we would bring ourselves to remember their wrestlings and lovely conversation, to rouse up ourselves to the like zeal; and because we would catch some vision of the rest and felicity wherein they now live, and thus, as we call them blessed, and provoke one another to emulate them, strive to follow in their footsteps: because moreover, we find thereby that the thought of death, which is right profitable, lendeth wings of zeal to our religious exercises; and lastly, because we derive sanctification from their touch.”

Again said the king, “If the thought of death be profitable, as ye say, why should ye not reach that thought of death by the bones of the bodies that are now your own, and are soon to perish, rather than by the bones of other men which have already perished?”

The monk said, “Five reasons I gave thee, why we carry about these relics; and thou, making answer to one only, art like to be mocking us. But know thou well that the bones of them, that have already departed this life, bring the thought of death more vividly before us than do the bones of the living. But since thou judgest otherwise, and since the bones of thine own body are to thee a type of death, why dost thou not recollect thy latter end so shortly to come, and set thine house in order, instead of giving up thy soul to all kinds of iniquities, and violently and unmercifully murdering the servants of God and lovers of righteousness, who have done thee no wrong, and seek not to share with thee in present goods, nor are ambitious to rob thee of them?”

===Chinese===
过了许多日子，阿拉凯斯把他们带到王面前，陈明了他们的案情，将他们立在怒气满胸的王面前。王一看见他们，怒火沸腾，直如癫狂之人。他下令毫不留情地鞭打他们；待见他们被鞭笞得遍体鳞伤，才勉强遏住自己的狂怒，吩咐行刑的人住手。随后他对他们说：「你们为何随身带着这些死人的骸骨行走？你们若是出于对骸骨主人的情谊而携带它们，此时此刻我就叫你们与他们同列，好叫你们寻见你们失散的朋友，合宜地感谢我。」那支敬虔队伍的首领与领袖，将王的威吓视若无物，身上不显自己所受酷刑的痕迹，声音坦然，面容焕发，显出住在他灵魂里的恩典，就高声呼喊说：「王啊，我们随身带着这些洁净而圣洁的骸骨，是因为要以合宜的形式，见证我们对其主人——那些可奇之人——的爱；是因为要叫自己记念他们属灵的争战与美善的为人，激发自己生发同样的热心；是因为渴望略窥他们如今所享的安息与福乐，这样，一面称他们有福，一面彼此激发、争相效法，奋力跟随他们的脚踪；又因为我们由此体会到，那大有裨益的死亡之念，为我们的敬虔操练插上了热心的翅膀；末了，还因为我们从抚摸它们领受成圣之恩。」

王又说：「死亡的思念若真如你们所说是有益的，你们为何不借着那如今属你们自己、不久也必朽坏之身体的骨头，来得着这死亡的思念，反倒借着别人那已经朽坏了的骸骨呢？」

修士说：「我已给了你五个理由，说明我们为何携带这些圣髑；你却只回答了其中一条，倒像是有意戏弄我们。但你要确切知道：那已经离世之人的骸骨，比活人的骨头更鲜活地把死亡的思念摆在我们眼前。既然你另有判断，既然你自己身体的骨头于你既是死亡的预像，你为何不思念你自己那转瞬即至的结局，为你的家留遗命，反将你的灵魂交给诸般不义，去残暴无情地杀害神的众仆人、那些爱公义的人——他们未曾加害于你，不求与你分占今世的财物，也没有夺你所有的野心呢？」

===Original===
Said the king, “I do well to punish you, ye clever misleaders of the folk, because ye deceive all men, counselling them to abstain from the enjoyments of life; and because, instead of the sweets of life and the allures of appetite and pleasure, ye constrain them to choose the rough, filthy and squalid way, and preach that they should render to Jesus the honour due unto the gods. Accordingly, in order that the people may not follow your deceits and leave the land desolate, and, forsaking the gods of their fathers, serve another, I think it just to subject you to punishment and death.”

The monk answered, “If thou art eager that all should partake of the good things of life, why dost thou not distribute dainties and riches equally amongst all? And why is it that the common herd are pinched with poverty, while thou addest ever to thy store by seizing for thyself the goods of others? Nay, thou carest not for the weal of the many, but fattenest thine own flesh, to be meat for the worms to feed on. Wherefore also thou hast denied the God of all, and called them gods that are not, the inventors of all wickedness, in order that, by wantonness and wickedness after their example, thou mayest gain the title of imitator of the gods. For, as your gods have done, why should not also the men that follow them do? Great then is the error that thou hast erred, O king. Thou fearest that we should persuade certain of the people to join with us, and revolt from thy hand, and place themselves in that hand that holdeth all things, for thou willest the ministers of thy covetousness to be many, that they may be miserable while thou reapest profit from their toil; just as a man, who keepeth hounds or falcons tamed for hunting, before the hunt may be seen to pet them, but, when they have once seized the quarry, taketh the game with violence out of their mouths. So also thou, willing that there should be many to pay thee tribute and toll from land and water, pretendest to care for their welfare, but in truth bringest on them and above all on thyself eternal ruin; and simply to pile up gold, more worthless than dung or rottenness, thou hast been deluded into taking darkness for light. But recover thy wits from this earthly sleep: open thy sealed eyes, and behold the glory of God that shineth round about us all; and come at length to thyself. For saith the prophet, ‘Take heed, ye unwise among the people, and, O ye fools, understand at last.’ Understand thou that there is no God except our God, and no salvation except in him.”

But the king said, “Cease this foolish babbling, and anon discover to me Barlaam: else shalt thou taste instruments of torture such as thou hast never tasted before.” That noble-minded, greathearted monk, that lover of the heavenly philosophy, was not moved by the king’s threats, but stood unflinching, and said, “We are not commanded to fulfil thy hest, O king, but the orders of our Lord and God who teacheth us temperance, that we should be lords over all pleasures and passions, and practise fortitude, so as to endure all toil and all ill-treatment for righteousness’ sake. The more perils that thou subjectest us to for the sake of our religion, the more shalt thou be our benefactor. Do therefore as thou wilt: for we shall not consent to do aught outside our duty, nor shall we surrender ourselves to sin. Deem not that it is a slight sin to betray a fellow-combatant and fellow-soldier into thy hands. Nay, but thou shalt not have that scoff to make at us; no, not if thou put us to ten thousand deaths. We be not such cowards as to betray our religion through dread of thy torments, or to disgrace the law divine. So then, if such be thy purpose, make ready every weapon to defend thy claim; for to us to live is Christ, and to die for him is the best gain.”

===Chinese===
王说：「我惩罚你们是合乎理的，你们这些诡巧迷惑百姓的人；因为你们欺哄众人，劝他们戒绝人生的享乐；又叫他们撇下人生的甘甜与口腹情欲的种种诱惑，强逼他们拣选那粗粝、污秽、鄙陋的道路，又宣讲说，他们当将诸神所当得的尊荣归给耶稣。所以，为免百姓随从你们的欺哄，置此地于荒凉，离弃列祖的神去事奉别神，我认为将你们交给刑罚与死亡，乃是正当的。」

修士回答说：「你若切望人人都得分享人生的美物，为何不把美味与财富均分给众人？为何黎民困于贫穷，你却攫夺他人之物，不住增添自己的积蓄？不然；你并不顾念众人的福祉，只是养肥自己的肉体，作虫子吞吃的食物。因此你否认了万有的神，称那些并非神的为神——那一切邪恶的发明者——好叫你效法他们肆意放荡、并行诸恶，赚得『效法诸神者』的名号。因为你们的神既这样行了，跟随他们的人为何不可照样行呢？王啊，你所陷入的错谬何其大。你惧怕我们说服百姓中的一些人与我们联合，脱离你的权下，把自己置于那掌握万有的手中；因为你要贪婪的差役越多越好，叫他们在困苦中劳碌，你却从他们的劳苦里坐收其利；正如人豢养驯于田猎的鹰犬，出猎之前或许看似抚爱它们，及至它们一旦攫住猎物，便强暴地从它们口中夺走猎获。你也是如此：你既愿多人从陆路水路向你缴纳贡赋捐税，便假装眷顾他们的福祉，实则给他们、首先是给你自己招来永远的沉沦；你只为堆积那比粪土朽烂更不值钱的黄金，竟受迷惑，以暗为光。你要从这属地的沉睡中清醒过来，睁开你那封闭的眼睛，观看那四面照临我们众人的神的荣耀，至终醒悟过来吧。因为先知说：『你们民中的愚昧人哪，要留意；愚顽人哪，你们终久要明白。』你要知道：除我们的神以外，并无别神；除他以外，也别无拯救。」

王却说：「停止这愚昧的絮聒吧；快快把巴拉阿姆指给我看；不然，你们要尝到你们从未尝过的刑具。」那位心志高贵、胸怀宏阔的修士，那天上哲学的爱慕者，不为王的威吓所动，屹立不摇，说：「王啊，我们奉命要成全的，不是你的吩咐，乃是我们主、我们神的诫命；他教导我们节制，使我们作一切享乐与情欲的主宰；又教导我们坚忍，使我们为义的缘故忍受一切劳苦、一切苛待。你为我们的信仰叫我们经受的危难越多，你就越发成为我们的恩人。所以，随你的意思行吧；因为我们决不允诺去行任何违背本分的事，也决不把自己交给罪。你不要以为，把同场争战的战友、同营当兵的弟兄交在你手中，是一桩小罪。不；你决不能那样讥诮我们——不能，就是你叫我们死一万次，也不能。我们并非那样的懦夫，会因惧怕你的酷刑出卖信仰，或玷辱神圣的律法。既然如此，你若定意这样行，就备齐各样的兵器，来维护你的主张吧；因为在我们，活着就是基督，为他而死就是最大的益处。」

===Original===
Incensed with anger thereat, the monarch ordered the tongues of these Confessors to be rooted out, and their eyes digged out, and likewise their hands and feet lopped off. Sentence passed, the henchmen and guards surrounded and mutilated them, without pity and without ruth. And they plucked out their tongues from their mouths with prongs, and severed them with brutal severity, and they digged out their eyes with iron claws, and stretched their arms and legs on the rack, and lopped them off. But those blessed, shamefast, noble-hearted men went bravely to torture like guests to a banquet, exhorting one another to meet death for Christ his sake undaunted.

In such divers tortures did these holy monks lay down their lives for the Lord. They were in all seventeen. By common consent, the pious mind is superior to sufferings, as hath been said by one, but not of us, when narrating the martydom of the aged priest, and of the seven sons with their equally brave mother when contending for the law of their fathers: whose bravery and lofty spirit, however, was equalled by these marvellous fathers and citizens and heirs of Hierusalem that is above.

===Chinese===
王闻言怒火中烧，下令将这些认信者的舌头连根拔去，眼睛挖出，手脚也一并斩断。判决既下，侍从与卫兵便将他们围住，毫无怜悯、毫无仁慈地加以残害：用铁钩将他们的舌头从口中扯出，以凶残的手段割断；用铁爪挖出他们的眼睛；又将他们的臂膀与腿拉张在刑架上，一一斩断。而那些蒙福、端庄、心志高贵的人，却勇敢地走上酷刑，如同宾客之赴筵席，彼此劝勉，为基督的缘故无所畏惧地迎向死亡。

这些圣洁的修士，就在这样种种的酷刑之下为主舍了性命。他们共有十七人。人所公认，敬虔的心志胜过诸般苦难，正如一位作者——虽非我们中间的人——叙述那年迈的祭司、并那为列祖的律法争战的七个儿子与他们同样勇敢之母亲的殉道时所说的；然而，这些可奇的众教父——那在上的耶路撒冷的公民与后嗣——的英勇与高尚心志，与之正不相上下。

## XXIV / 二十四

===Original===
After the monks had made this godly end, the king bade Araches, his chief councillor, now that they had failed of their first plan, to look to the second and summon the man Nachor. At dead of night Araches repaired to his cave (he dwelt in the desert practising the arts of divination), and told him of their plans, and returned to the king at daybreak. Again he demanded horsemen, and made as though he went in quest of Barlaam. When he was gone forth, and was walking the desert, a man was seen to issue from a ravine. Araches gave command to his men to pursue him. They took and brought him before their master. When asked who he was, what his religion and what his name, the man declared himself a Christian and gave his name as Barlaam, even as he had been instructed. Araches made great show of joy, apprehended him and returned quickly to the king, and told his tale and produced his man. Then said the king in the hearing of all present, “Art thou the devil’s workman, Barlaam?” But he denied it, saying, “I am God’s workman, not the devil’s. Revile me not; for I am thy debtor to render me much thanks, because I have taught thy son to serve God, and have turned him from error to the true God, and have schooled him in all manner of virtue.” Feigning anger, again spake the king, “Though I ought to allow thee never a word, and give thee no room for defence, but rather do thee to death without question, yet such is my humanity that I will bear with thine effrontery until on a set day I try thy cause. If thou be persuaded by me, thou shalt receive pardon: if not, thou shalt die the death.” With these words he delivered him to Araches, commanding that he should be most strictly guarded.

On the morrow the king removed thence, and came back to his own palace, and it was blazoned abroad that Barlaam was captured, so that the king’s son heard thereof and was exceeding sad at heart, and could in no wise refrain from weeping. With groans and lamentations he importuned God, and called upon him to succour the aged man. Nor did the good God despise his complaint, for he is loving with them that abide him in the day of trouble, and knoweth them that fear him. Wherefore in a night-vision he made known the whole plot to the young prince, and strengthened and cheered him for the trial of his righteousness. So, when the prince awoke from sleep, he found that his heart, erstwhile so sore and heavy, was now full of joyance, courage and pleasant light. But the king rejoiced at that which he had done and planned, imagining that he was well advised, and showering thanks on Araches. But wickedness lied to itself, to use the words of holy David, and righteousness overcame iniquity, completely overthrowing it, and causing the memorial thereof to perish with sound, as shall be proven by our tale.

===Chinese===
修士们这样敬虔地殉道之后，王见第一计既败，便吩咐首席谋臣阿拉凯斯着手第二条计策，去召那纳霍尔来。夜阑人静，阿拉凯斯去到他的洞穴（他住在旷野，修习占卜之术），把他们的计谋告诉了他，天一破晓就回到王那里。他又索要了骑兵，装作出去寻找巴拉阿姆的样子。他出城在旷野行走时，见有一人从峡谷中出来。阿拉凯斯吩咐手下人追赶。他们拿住他，带到主人面前。及至问他是什么人、信奉什么教、名叫什么，那人便自称是基督徒，报名叫巴拉阿姆，正如先前所授意的一样。阿拉凯斯装出极大的欢喜，拿住他，急忙回到王那里，禀明原委，献上他寻得的人。于是王当着在场众人说：「巴拉阿姆，你是魔鬼的工人么？」他却否认说：「我是神的工人，不是魔鬼的工人。不要辱骂我；因为我教你儿子事奉神，领他离弃错谬归向真神，又在各样德行上教训了他，你倒欠我许多的感谢才是。」王佯装忿怒，又说：「我本不该容你说一句话，不给你分辩的余地，只该不问情由将你处死；但我如此仁慈，且容忍你的放肆，直到定期之日审问你的案子。你若听我的劝，必得赦免；若不听，必被处死。」说完这话，他就把他交付阿拉凯斯，吩咐要极其严紧地看守。

次日，王从那里起行，回到自己的宫殿；又有话传扬开来，说巴拉阿姆被擒了。王的儿子听见这话，心里极其忧伤，怎么也忍不住流泪。他呻吟哀叹，恳切求告神，呼求他救助那位老人。良善的神也不轻看他的哀诉，因为在患难的日子，他以恩慈待投靠他的人，且认得敬畏他的人。于是神在夜间的异象中，将这全部计谋指示了年轻的王子，为着他义的试炼刚强他、安慰他。王子从睡中醒来，觉得自己的心——先前那样伤痛沉重——如今满了喜乐、勇气与悦人的光。王却为自己所行所谋的欢喜，自以为计虑得当，向阿拉凯斯频频称谢。然而，用可称颂的大卫的话说，恶孽欺哄了它自己；公义胜过了不义，将它完全倾覆，叫它的纪念随一声响灭没净尽，正如我们的故事所要证明的。

===Original===
After two days the king visited his son’s palace. When his son came forth for to meet him, instead of kissing him, as was his wont, the father put on a show of distress and anger, and entered the royal chamber, and there sat down frowning. Then calling to his son, he said, “Child, what is this report that soundeth in mine ears, and weareth away my soul with despondency? Never, I ween, was man more filled with gladness of heart at the birth of a son than was I at thine; and, I trow, never was man so distressed and cruelly treated by child as I have now been by thee. Thou hast dishonoured my grey hairs, and taken away the light of mine eyes, and loosed the strength of my sinews; ‘for the thing which I greatly feared concerning thee is come upon me, and that which I was afraid of hath come unto me.’ Thou art become a joy to mine enemies, and a laughingstock to mine adversaries. With untutored mind and childish judgement thou hast followed the teaching of the deceivers and esteemed the counsel of the malicious above mine; thou hast forsaken the worship of our gods and become the servant of a strange God. Child, wherefore hast thou done this? I hoped to bring thee up in all safety, and have thee for the staff and support of mine old age, and leave thee, as is most meet, to succeed me in my kingdom, but thou wast not ashamed to play against me the part of a relentless foe. And shouldst thou not rather have listened to me, and followed my injunctions, than have obeyed the idle and foolish pratings of that crafty old knave, who taught thee to choose a sour life instead of a sweet, and abandon the charms of dalliance, to tread the hard and rough road, which the Son of Mary ordereth men to go? Dost thou not fear the displeasure of the most puissant gods, lest they strike thee with lightning, or quell thee with thunderbolt, or overwhelm thee in the yawning earth, because thou hast rejected and scorned those deities that have so richly blessed us, and adorned our brow with the kingly diadem, and made populous nations to be our servants, that, beyond my hope, in answer to my prayer and supplication, allowed thee to be born, and see the sweet life of day, and hast joined thyself unto the Crucified, duped by the hopes of his servants who tell thee fables of worlds to come, and drivel about the resurrection of dead bodies, and bring in a thousand more absurdities to catch fools? But now, dearest son, if thou hast any regard for me thy father, bid a long farewell to these longwinded follies, and come sacrifice to the gracious gods, and let us propitiate them with hecatombs and drink-offerings, that they may grant thee pardon for thy fall; for they be able and strong to bless and to punish. And wouldst thou have an example of that which I say? Behold us, who by them have been advanced to this honour, repaying them for their kindness by honouring their worshippers and chastising the runagates.”

===Chinese===
过了两天，王到他儿子的宫里去。儿子出来迎接，他非但不像惯常那样与他亲嘴，反装出忧愁忿怒的模样，进入王室内室，皱着眉坐下。然后唤儿子来，说：「孩子啊，这响在我耳中、使我魂里消沉的，是什么风声呢？我料想，人得子之时心里的欢乐，从来没有一人像我得你时那样满足；我也深信，人被儿子苦待得这样悲惨的，从来没有一人像我现在被你所待。你辱没了我的白发，夺去了我眼中的光，松解了我筋骨的力量；『因我所恐惧的临到我身，我所惧怕的迎我而来。』你成了我仇敌的喜乐，成了攻击我之人的笑柄。你以未受教的心思、孩童的判断，随从了那些欺哄者的教训，看重恶意之人的计谋过于我的；你离弃了对我们诸神的敬拜，作了生疏之神的仆人。孩子啊，你为什么行这事呢？我本指望平平安安地将你养大，使你作我老年的杖与扶持，又照最合宜的安排，留你继承我的国；你却不知羞耻，对我扮演了无情仇敌的角色。你岂不本该听我的话、遵我的禁令，而不该听从那奸猾老贼闲散愚妄的絮聒么？——他教你拣选苦涩的生活而舍弃甘甜，离弃温存嬉戏的媚惑，去踏那马利亚之子吩咐人行的又艰苦又崎岖的路。你难道不惧怕至能诸神的不悦，惟恐他们用闪电击打你，用雷轰扑灭你，将你吞灭在裂开的地中么？——因为你已弃绝并轻慢了那些厚厚赐福我们的神明：他们以帝王的冠冕装饰我们的额，叫人民众多的列邦作我们的仆役，又因着我的祷告恳求，超过我所盼望的，容你得以出生，得见白昼甘美的生活；你却受他仆人们所存之指望的欺哄，将自己联于那被钉十字架者——那些仆人向你讲述来世的虚谈，胡诌什么死人身体的复活，又引进千般别的荒谬来网罗愚人。但如今，最亲爱的儿子啊，你若对我你这为父的还有一点顾念，就同这些冗长的愚妄永远道别吧，来向恩慈的诸神献祭；让我们以百牛大祭与奠祭平息他们的怒气，好叫他们赦免你的堕落；因为无论祝福还是刑罚，他们都有能力大力施行。你要我所说的事要个例证么？看我们吧：我们蒙他们升到这尊荣，就报答他们的恩慈——敬重敬拜他们的人，惩治那些叛逃者。」

===Original===
Now when the king had ended all this idle parleying, gainsaying and slandering of our religion, and belauding and praising of his idolatry, the saintly young prince saw that the matter needed no further to be hid in a corner, but to be lighted and made plain to the eyes of all; and, full of boldness and courage, he said:

“That which I have done, sir, I will not deny. I have fled from darkness and run to the light: I have left error and joined the household of truth: I have deserted the service of devils, and joined the service of Christ, the Son and Word of God the Father, at whose decree the world was brought out of nothing; who, after forming man out of clay, breathed into him the breath of life, and set him to live in a paradise of delight, and, when he had broken his commandment and was become subject unto death, and had fallen into the power of the dread ruler of this world, did not fail him, but wrought diligently to bring him back to his former honour. Wherefore he, the framer of all Creation and maker of our race, became man for our sake, and, coming from a holy Virgin’s womb; on earth conversed with men: for us ungrateful servants did the master endure death, even the death of the Cross, that the tyranny of sin might be destroyed, that the former condemnation might be abolished, that the gates of heaven might be open to us again. Thither he hath exalted our nature, and set it on the throne of glory, and granted to them that love him an everlasting kingdom and joys beyond all that tongue can tell, or ear can hear. He is the mighty and only potentate, King of kings and Lord of lords, whose might is invincible, and whose lordship is beyond compare, who only is holy and dwelleth in holiness, who with the Father and with the Holy Ghost is glorified; into this faith I have been baptized. And I acknowledge and glorify and worship One God in Three persons, of one substance, and not to be confounded, increate and immortal, eternal, infinite, boundless, without body, without passions, immutable, unchangeable, undefinable, the fountain of goodness, righteousness and everlasting light, maker of all things visible and invisible, containing and sustaining all things, provident for all, ruler and King of all. Without him was there nothing made, nor without his providence can aught subsist. He is the life of all, the support of all, the light of all, being wholly sweetness and insatiable desire, the summit of aspiration. To leave God, then, who is so good, so wise, so mighty, and to serve impure devils, makers of all sinful lusts, and to assign worship to deaf and dumb images, that are not, and never shall be, were not that the extreme of folly and madness? When was there ever heard utterance or language from their lips? When have they given even the smallest answer to their bedesmen? When have they walked, or received any impression of sense? Those of them that stand have never thought of sitting down; and those that sit have never been seen to rise. From an holy man have I learned the ugliness, ill savour and insensibility of these idols, and, moreover, the rottenness and weakness of the devils that operate in them and by them deceive you; and I loathe their wickednesses and, hating them with a perfect hatred, have joined myself to the living and true God, and him will I serve until my latest breath, that my spirit also may return into his hands. When these unspeakable blessings came in my path I rejoiced to be freed from the bondage of evil devils, and to be reclaimed from dire captivity and to be illumined with the light of the countenance of the Lord. But my soul was distressed and divided asunder, that thou, my lord and father, didst not share in my blessings. Yet I feared the stubbornness of thy mind, and kept my grief to myself, not wishing to anger thee; but, without ceasing, I prayed God to draw thee to himself, and call thee back from the long exile that thou hast imposed upon thyself, a runagate alas! from righteousness, and a servant of all sin and wickedness. But sith thou thyself, O my father, hast brought mine affairs to light, hear the sum of my resolve: I will not be false to my covenant with Christ; no, I swear it by him that bought me out of slavery with his own precious blood; even if I must needs die a thousand deaths for his sake, die I will. Knowing then how matters now stand with me, prithee, no longer trouble thyself in endeavouring to persuade me to change my good confession. For as it were a thankless and never ending task for thee to try to grasp the heavens with thy hand, or to dry up the waters of the sea, so hard were it for thee to change me. Either then now listen to my counsel, and join the household of Christ, and so thou shalt gain blessings past man’s understanding, and we shall be fellows with one another by faith, even as by nature; or else, be well assured, I shall depart thy sonship, and serve my God with a clear conscience.”

===Chinese===
王这样把一切空言的支吾、对我们信仰的抗拒与毁谤、并对他偶像崇拜的夸赞颂扬说完之后，圣洁的少年王子看出，这事不必再藏在角落里，乃当点起灯来，显明在众人眼前；他就满有胆量与勇气，说：

「主啊，我所行的事，我不否认。我逃避了黑暗，奔向了光明；我离弃了错谬，归入了真理的家；我脱离了事奉鬼魔，加入了事奉基督——就是父神的子和道：万有是凭他的命从无有中被领出来的。他用尘土造了人之后，将生气吹在他里面，安置他住在悦乐的乐园；及至人违背了他的诫命，服在死权之下，落在今世可畏掌权者的权势里，他也没有丢弃他，倒殷勤劳作，要领他归回先前的尊荣。所以，他——万有的塑造者、我们族类的造作者——为我们成了人，自圣童女的胎中出来，在地上与人往来同处：为我们这些忘恩的仆人，主忍受了死，且死在十字架上，好叫罪的暴虐得以毁灭，从前的定罪得以废去，诸天的门得以重新向我们敞开。他已将我们的本性高举到那里，安置在荣耀的宝座上，又赐给爱他的人一个永远的国，并那舌头不能尽述、耳朵不能听闻的喜乐。他是大能者，独一的主宰，万王之王、万主之主；他的权能无可敌挡，他的主权无可比拟；他独一为圣，住在圣洁之中，与父与圣灵同受荣耀：我已受洗归入这信仰。我又承认、荣耀并敬拜独一的神，在三个位格中，同质而不可混滥，非受造而不死，永恒、无限、无涯，无形躯、无情欲，不变、不易、不可界说，是良善、公义与永远之光的泉源，是一切可见与不可见之物的造作者，包含万有、托住万有，眷顾万有，统辖万有、作万有之王。没有他，就没有一物造成；没有他的护理，就没有一物能以存立。他是万有的生命、万有的扶持、万有的光，全然是甘甜，是不能餍足的愿欲，是众望所归的极峰。这样，离开如此良善、如此智慧、如此大能的神，去事奉那些污秽的鬼魔——一切罪恶私欲的制造者——又将敬拜归于那些又聋又哑的像——它们不是存有的，也永不会存有——这岂不是愚妄与癫狂的极点么？何曾有人听见他们口中发出言语或乡谈？他们几曾向他们的祈求者给过哪怕最小的回答？他们几曾行走，或领受过任何官觉的印象？那些站着的，从未想过坐下；那些坐着的，从未被人看见起立。我从一位圣洁的人身上，学知了这些偶像的丑陋、恶味与无知觉，又学知了在其中运行、并借它们欺哄你们的鬼魔的朽坏与软弱；我憎恶它们的诸般邪恶，用完全的恨恨它们，已将自己联于那又真又活的神，并且我要事奉他，直到我末了一口气，好叫我的灵也归还他手。当这些不可言喻的福分临到我路途上时，我欢喜自己得脱离恶鬼的捆绑，从惨酷的俘掳中被赎回，蒙主面上的光照亮。但我的魂忧愁、被撕为两半，因为我主我的父啊，你未与我同得这些福分。只是我怕你心思的顽梗，便将忧伤藏在自己心里，不愿激怒你；却不住地求神吸引你归他自己，召你脱离你加在自己身上的长久流亡——哀哉，你这离弃了公义的逃亡者，作了一切罪恶不义的仆役！但父啊，你既亲自叫我如今的事显露出来，就请听我决志的总意：我必不背弃我与基督所立的约；不，我指着他起誓——他曾用自己的宝血将我从奴役中买来——哪怕我必须为他死上一千次的死，我也甘愿死。你既知道我如今的光景，就请你不要再劳心，想劝我背弃我美好的认信了。因为你若想以手握住诸天，或使海中的水枯干，那是无功而无尽的劳苦；你要改变我，也正是同样艰难。所以，你如今或是听从我的劝告，加入基督的家，这样你必得着那过于人所能领会的福分，我们在信仰上也必彼此为伴，正如在天性上为伴一样；不然，你要深信，我必退出你儿子的名分，存着无亏的良心事奉我的神。」

===Original===
Now when the king heard all these words, he was furiously enraged: and, seized with ungovernable anger, he cried out wrathfully against him, and gnashed his teeth fiercely, like any madman. “And who,” said he, “is blameable for all my misfortunes but myself, who have dealt with thee so kindly, and cared for thee as no father before? Hence the perversity and contrariness of thy mind, gathering strength by the licence that I gave thee, hath made thy madness to fall upon mine own pate. Rightly prophesied the astrologers in thy nativity that thou shouldest prove a knave and villain, an impostor and rebellious son. But now, if thou wilt make void my counsel, and cease to be my son, I will become thine enemy, and entreat thee worse than ever man yet entreated his foes.”

Again said Ioasaph, “Why, O king, hast thou been kindled to wrath? Art thou grieved that I have gained such bliss? Why, what father was ever seen to be sorrowful in the prosperity of his son? Would not such an one be called an enemy rather than a father? Therefore will I no more call thee my father, but will withdraw from thee, as a man fleeth from a snake, if I know that thou grudgest me my salvation, and with violent hand forcest me to destruction. If thou wilt force me, and play the tyrant, as thou hast threatened, be assured that thou shalt gain nought thereby save to exchange the name of father for that of tyrant and murderer. It were easier for thee to attain to the ways of the eagle, and, like him, cleave the air, than to alter my loyalty to Christ, and that good confession that I have confessed in him. But be wise, O my father, and shake off the rheum and mist from the eyes of thy mind, lift them aloft and look upward to view the light of my God that enlighteneth all around, and be thyself, at last, enlightened with this light most sweet. Why art thou wholly given up to the passions and desires of the flesh, and why is there no looking upward? Know thou that all flesh is grass and all the glory of man as the flower of grass. The grass withereth, the flower thereof falleth away; but the word of my Lord, which by the gospel is preached unto all, shall endure forever. Why then dost thou thus madly cling to and embrace that glory, which, like spring flowers, fadeth and perisheth, and to beastly unsavoury wantonness, and to the abominable passions of the belly and the members thereunder, which for a season please the senses of fools, but afterwards make returns more bitter than gall, when the shadows and dreams of this vain life are passed away, and the lovers thereof, and workers of iniquity are imprisoned in the perpetual pain of dark and unquenchable fire, where the worm that sleepeth not gnaweth forever, and where the fire burneth without ceasing and without quenching through endless ages? And with these sinners alas! thou too shalt be imprisoned and grievously tormented, and shalt bitterly rue thy wicked counsels, and bitterly regret thy days that now are, and think upon my words, but there shall be no advantage in repentance; for in death there is no confession and repentance. But the present is the set time for work: the future for reward. Even if the pleasures of the present world were not evanescent and fleeting, but were to endure forever with their owners, not even thus should any man choose them before the gifts of Christ, and the good things that pass man’s understanding. Soothly, as the sun surpasseth in radiance and brightness the dead of night, even so, and much more so, doth the happiness promised to those that love God excel in glory and magnificence all earthly kinship and glory; and there is utter need for a man to choose the more excellent before the more worthless. And forasmuch as everything here is fleeting and subject to decay, and passeth and vanisheth as a dream, and as a shadow and vision of sleep; and as one may sooner trust the unstable breezes, or the tracks of a ship passing over the waves, than the prosperity of men, what simplicity, nay, what folly and madness it is to choose the corruptible and perishable, the weak things of no worth, rather than the incorruptible and everlasting, the imperishable and endless, and, by the temporal enjoyment of these things, to forfeit the eternal fruition of the happiness to come! Wilt thou not understand this, my father? Wilt thou not haste past the things which haste pass thee, and attach thyself to that which endureth? Wilt thou not prefer a home land to a foreign land, light to darkness, the spirit to the flesh, eternal life to the shadow of death, the indestructible to the fleeting? Wilt thou not escape from the grievous bondage of the cruel prince of this world, I mean the evil one, the devil, and become the servant of the good, tenderhearted, and all merciful Lord? Wilt thou not break away from serving thy many gods, falsely so called, and serve the one, true and living God? Though thou hast sinned against him often times by blaspheming him, and often times by slaying his servants with dread torments, yet, I know well, that if thou turn again, he shall in his kindness receive thee, and no more remember thine offences: because he willeth not the death of a sinner but rather that he may turn and live﻿—he, who came down from the unspeakable heights, to seek us that had gone astray: who endured for us Cross, scourge and death; who bought with his precious blood us who had been sold in bondage under sin. Unto him be glory and praise forever and ever! Amen.”

===Chinese===
王听见这一切话，就狂烈地动怒：他被不能遏制的忿怒抓住，向他怒声呵斥，咬牙切齿，如同疯子一般。他说：「我这一切不幸，除了我自己，还能怪谁呢？我这样恩待你，照看得比从前任何为父的都周全。这样，你心思的乖僻与悖戾，仗着我所给的宽容渐渐蓄起势来，就叫你的癫狂落在我自己的头颅上。占星家论你的降生所预言的，果然不错：你必显为奸恶之徒与无赖，一个欺世盗名之人和悖逆之子。但如今，你若要使我的计谋落空，不再作我的儿子，我就要作你的仇敌，苦待你比历来任何人苦待仇敌更甚。」

约阿萨弗又说：「王啊，你为什么燃起了忿怒？你忧伤，是因我得着了这样的福分么？唉，何曾有父亲见儿子昌盛而忧愁的呢？这样的人，岂不当称为仇敌，过于称为父亲么？所以我若知道你妒忌我的救恩，以强暴的手逼我入于灭亡，我就必不再称你为父，必远离你，如同人逃避毒蛇一样。你若定要强迫我，如你所威吓的施行暴虐，你要确知，你必一无所获，不过把父亲的名换作暴君与凶手的名而已。你想达到鹰的路径、像鹰那样翱翔剖开空气，倒比改变我对基督的忠贞、改变我在他里面所认信的那美好认信更容易些。但我的父啊，你要有智慧，抖落你心思眼目上的眵翳与迷雾，将它们高举，向上仰望我神的光——那光照亮四围的一切——也愿你终于亲自蒙这至甜的光所照亮。你为什么全然沉溺于肉体的情欲和私欲？为什么不向上仰望呢？你要知道，凡有血气的尽都如草，他的美荣都像草上的花。草必枯干，花必凋谢；惟有我主的道——就是借福音传给众人的——必存到永远。那么，你为什么这样癫狂地恋慕、抱住那如春花般凋谢朽坏的荣耀，抱住那兽性无味的放荡，并那肚腹与其以下肢体的可憎情欲呢？这些事物一时之间悦媚愚人的官觉，过后却回报比苦胆更苦的味道——到那时，这虚妄人生的影子和梦都已过去，恋慕它的人和作孽的人都被囚在黑暗不灭之火的永刑里；在那里，不睡的虫永远咬啮；在那里，火焚烧不息、不得熄灭，直烧到无尽的岁月。哀哉，连你也要与这些罪人一同被囚，受重重的苦刑；你要痛悔你邪恶的计谋，痛惜你如今的日子，想起我的话来，只是悔改再无益处；因为在死亡里，没有认信，也没有悔改。现今是作工的定候，将来是得赏的定候。即使今世的种种逸乐不是转瞬即逝、而是与其主人永存的，也没有人该因此就在基督的恩赐、并那过于人所领会的美物之先，拣选它们。诚然，正如太阳在光辉明亮上胜过死寂的黑夜，照样，且更是如此，那应许给爱神之人的幸福，在荣耀华美上远胜地上一切亲谊与荣华；人实在必须拣选那更美的，而弃绝那更无价值的。况且，这里的一切都流变易朽，如梦之消逝，如睡中的影儿与幻象；人信无定的风、信船行波浪上留下的轨迹，还快过信人的顺遂；那么，拣选那能朽坏、必败亡、软弱无价值之物，而不拣选那不能朽坏、永远长存、不朽无尽之物，又因这些事物暂时的享受，丧失将来福乐永远的享受——这是何等的天真，不，是何等的愚妄与癫狂！我的父啊，你不明白这事么？你岂不当快快越过那快快越过你的事物，将自己联结于那常存的么？你岂不当拣选本乡过于异乡、光过于暗、灵过于肉体、永生过于死之荫影、不灭者过于易逝者么？你岂不当逃脱这今世残酷君王的沉重捆绑——就是那恶者魔鬼——作那良善、心慈、满有怜悯之主的仆人么？你岂不当断绝事奉你那许多枉称为神的神明，去事奉那独一又真又活的神么？虽然你屡次因谤渎他而得罪了他，屡次以可怕的酷刑杀戮他的仆人，然而我知道，你若回转，他必以恩慈收纳你，不再记念你的过犯：因为他不喜悦罪人之死，惟喜悦他回转而存活——他，就是那从说不出的高天降下、寻找我们这些迷失之人的；他为我们忍受了十字架、鞭笞和死亡；他以自己的宝血，买了我们这些被卖在罪下为奴的人。愿荣耀颂赞都归给他，直到永永远远。阿们。」

===Original===
The king was overwhelmed with astonishment and anger: with astonishment, at his son’s wisdom and unanswerable words; with anger, at the persistence with which he denounced his father’s gods, and mocked and ridiculed the whole tenor of his life. He could not admit the glory of his discourse because of the grossness of the darkness within, but natural affection forbad him to punish his son, or evilly to entreat him, and he utterly despaired of moving him by threats. Fearing then that, if he argued further with him, his son’s boldness and bitter satire of the gods might kindle him to hotter anger, and lead him to do him a mischief, he arose in wrath and withdrew. “Would that thou hadst never been born,” he cried, “nor hadst come to the light of day, destined as thou wert to be such an one, a blasphemer of the gods, and a renegade from thy father’s love and admonition! But thou shalt not alway mock the invincible gods, nor shall their enemies rejoice for long, nor shall these knavish sorceries prevail. For except thou become obedient unto me, and right-minded toward the gods, I will first deliver thee to sundry tortures, and then put thee to the cruellest death, dealing with thee not as with a son, but as with an enemy and rebel.”

===Chinese===
王被惊异和忿怒压倒了：惊异，是因儿子的智慧与无从置答的言语；忿怒，是因他坚持指斥他父亲的神明，讥诮嘲弄他一生的全般行径。因着里面黑暗的粗浊，他不能领会他讲论的荣美；但天然的亲情又拦阻他惩罚儿子或苦待他，他也全然绝望于用威吓摇动他。他又惧怕，若再与儿子争辩下去，儿子的放胆和他对诸神的辛辣讥诮，恐怕要激动他更炽热的怒气，以致下手害他；便在忿怒中起身退去了。他喊着说：「巴不得你从来没有出生，从来没有来到白日光中，因为你命中注定要作这样一个人——谤渎诸神的人、离弃父亲的爱与训诲的叛徒！但你不得永远嘲弄那无敌的诸神，他们的仇敌也不会长久欢跃，这些奸猾的邪术也终不得逞。因为你若不顺服我、不向诸神存正意，我必先叫你受种种酷刑，然后将你处最残忍的死，待你不像待儿子，倒像待仇敌和叛贼。」

## XXV / 二十五

===Original===
In such wise did the father threaten and wrathfully retire. But the son entered his own bedchamber, and lifted up his eyes to the proper judge of his cause, and cried out of the depth of his heart, “O Lord my God, my sweet hope and unerring promise, the sure refuge of them that are wholly given up to thee, with gracious and kindly eye look upon the contrition of my heart, and leave me not, neither forsake me. But, according to thine unerring pledge, be thou with me, thine unworthy and sorry servant. Thee I acknowledge and confess, the maker and provider of all creation. Therefore do thou thyself enable me to continue in this good confession, until my dying breath: look upon me, and pity me; and stand by and keep me unhurt by any working of Satan. Look upon me, O King: for my heart is enkindled with longing after thee, and is parched as with burning thirst in the desert, desiring thee, the well of immortality. Deliver not to the wild beasts my soul that confesseth thee: forget not the soul of the poor forever; but grant me that am a sinner throughout my length of days to suffer all things for thy name’s sake and in the confession of thee, and to sacrifice my whole self unto thee. For, with thy might working in them, even the feeble shall wax exceeding strong; for thou only art the unconquerable ally and merciful God, whom all creation blesseth, glorified forever and ever. Amen.”
When he had thus prayed, he felt divine comfort stealing over his heart, and, fulfilled with courage, he spent the whole night in prayer. Meanwhile the king communed with Araches, his friend, as touching his son’s matters, and signified to him his son’s sheer audacity and unchangeable resolution. Araches gave counsel that he should, in his dealings with him, show the utmost kindness and courtesy, in the hope, perchance, of alluring him by flattering attentions. The day following, the king came to his son, and sat down, and called him to his side. He embraced and kissed him affectionately, coaxing him gently and tenderly, and said, “O my darling and well-beloved son, honour thou thy father’s grey hairs: listen to my entreaty, and come, do sacrifice to the gods; thus shalt thou win their favour, and receive at their hands length of days, and the enjoyment of all glory and of an undisputed kingdom, and happiness of every sort. Thus shalt thou be well pleasing to me thy father throughout life and be honoured and lauded of all men. It is a great count in the score of praise to be obedient to thy father, especially in a good cause, and to gain the goodwill of the gods. What thinkest thou, my son? Is it that I have willingly declined from the right, and chosen to travel on the wrong road: or that, from ignorance and inexperience of the good, I have given myself to destruction? Well, if thou thinkest that I willingly prefer the evil to the profitable, and choose death before life, thou seemest to me, son, completely to have missed the goal in judging. Dost thou not see to what discomfort and trouble I often expose myself in mine expeditions against my foes, or when I am engaged in divers other business for the public good, not sparing myself even hunger and thirst, if need be, the march on foot, or the couch on the ground? As for riches and money, such is my contempt and scorn thereof, that I have at times ungrudgingly lavished all the stores of my palace, to build mighty temples for the gods, and to adorn them with all manner of splendour, or else to distribute liberal largess to my soldiers. Possessing then, as I also do, this contempt of pleasure and this courage in danger, what zeal would I not have devoted to contemning all else, and winning my salvation, had I only found that the religion of the Galileans were better than mine own? But, if thou condemnest me for ignorance and inexperience of the good, consider how many sleepless nights I have spent, with some problem before me, ofttimes no very important one, giving myself no rest until I had found the clear and most apt solution.
===Chinese===
父亲这样威吓了一场，怒气冲冲地退了出去。儿子却进了自己的寝宫，举目仰望那有权审断他案情的主，从心底深处呼喊说：「主我的神啊，我甘甜的盼望、无误的应许、完全归服你的人所确赖的避难所，求你以恩慈和悦的眼垂顾我心中的忧伤痛悔，不要丢弃我，也不要离弃我。求你照你无误的誓言，与我——你这不配而可怜的仆人——同在。我承认你，认信你，你是万有的创造者与养育者。所以求你亲自使我能在这美好的认信上恒忍到底，直到我临终的气息；看顾我，怜恤我；站在我身旁，保守我不受撒但任何作为的损伤。求你看顾我，王啊：因我的心切慕你，为你焚烧，如在旷野中被焦渴烧灼，切望你这不朽的井泉。求你不要将我这承认你的灵魂交给野兽；求你不要永远忘掉穷苦人的灵魂；又求你赐我这罪人，在世的全部年日里，为你的名、并在对你的认信中受一切苦难，将自己全人当作祭物献上给你。因为你的大能运行在人里面，软弱的也必变得极其刚强；因为惟有你是不可战胜的援助者、施怜悯的神，万有都称颂你，你得荣耀，直到永永远远。阿们。」

他这样祷告完了，就觉有属天的安慰悄然漫过心头，于是满得勇气，通宵恒切祷告。其间，王与他的朋友阿拉凯斯商议儿子的事，向他述说儿子何等执拗放胆、心志坚定不移。阿拉凯斯献策说，王与他周旋时当显出极度的仁慈与礼遇，盼望或可靠谄媚殷勤的眷顾诱他回转。次日，王来到儿子那里，坐下，召他到身旁；亲热地拥抱亲吻他，温柔体贴地哄劝，说：「我疼爱的宝贝、深蒙钟爱的儿子啊，你要敬重你父亲的白发：听我的恳求，来吧，向诸神献祭；这样你必赢得他们的恩宠，从他们手中得享长寿、享受一切的荣耀与无可争议的王国，并各样的幸福。这样，你一生必使你父亲喜悦，也受众人的尊崇称扬。顺从父亲——尤其是在善事上——又得诸神的善意，这在当称赞的事中乃是一大项。我儿，你意下如何？你以为我是甘心偏离正路，拣选了行在错谬的道上呢？还是因不识善、无历于善，而自取灭亡呢？唉，我儿，你若以为我甘心择恶舍益、宁死不生，那么在我看来，你在判断上就完全失了准的。你岂不见我出征讨伐仇敌、或为公益办理种种别的事务时，常常使自己经受多少辛苦烦劳？若有必要，我连饥饿干渴、徒步行军、露宿地面，也一概不惜。至于财富金钱，我对它们的轻蔑鄙视如此之深，以致有时毫不吝惜地倾尽宫中府库的一切积储，为诸神修建宏伟的庙宇，以各样的华美装饰它们，或向我军兵广施丰厚的赏赐。那么，我既同样怀有这轻看享乐之心、这临危不惧之勇，倘若我查明确是加利利人的宗教胜过我所信的，还有什么热心我不肯献上，为要轻看万事、赢得救恩呢？但你若定我的罪，说我不识善、无历于善，就请想一想：我曾有多少个不眠之夜，面前摆着某个难题——往往并非什么要紧的难题——不容自己歇息，直到寻出那清楚而最恰当的解答。」

===Original===
“Seeing then that I reckon that not even the least of these temporal concerns is unworthy of thought until all be fitly completed for the advantage of all and seeing that all (I ween) bear me witness that no man under the sun can search out secrets with more diligence than I, how then could I have considered divine things, that call for worship and serious consideration, unworthy of thought, and not rather have devoted all my zeal and might, all my mind and soul to the investigation thereof, to find out the right and the true? Aye, and I have laboriously sought thereafter. Many nights and days have I spent thus: many wise and learned men have I called to my council; and with many of them that are called Christians have I conversed. By untiring enquiry and ardent search I have discovered the pathway of truth, witnessed by wise men honoured for their intelligence and wit﻿—that there is none other faith than ours. This is the path that we tread today, worshipping the most puissant gods, and holding fast to that sweet and delightsome life, given by them to all men, fulfilled with all manner of pleasure and gladness of heart, which the leaders and priests of the Galileans have in their folly rejected; so that, in hope of some other uncertain life, they have readily cast away this sweet light, and all those pleasures which the gods have bestowed on us for enjoyment, and all the while know not what they say, nor whereof they confidently affirm.
“But thou, dearest son, obey thy father, who, by diligent and honest search, hath found the real good. Lo, I have shown thee that, neither willingly, no, nor by way of ignorance, have I failed of the good, but rather that I have found and laid hold thereon. And I earnestly desire that thou too shouldest not wander as a fool, but shouldest follow me. Have respect then unto thy father. Dost thou not know how lovely a thing it is to obey one’s father, and please him in all ways? Contrariwise, how deadly and cursed a thing it is to provoke a father and despise his commands? As many as have done so, have come to a miserable end. But be not thou, my son, one of their number. Rather do that which is well pleasing to thy sire, and so mayest thou obtain all happiness and inherit my blessing and my kingdom!”
===Chinese===
「既然我认为，这些属世的事务，哪怕顶小的一件，在妥善办成、使众人得益之前，也不配加以思量；又料想众人都可为我作证：普天之下，无人探究隐秘的事比我更勤勉——那么，对于那当受敬拜、当郑重思考的属神之事，我怎能倒以为不配思量，而不倾尽我一切的热心与力量、一切的心思与性命去考究它，以求辨明何为正、何为真呢？实在的，我也曾劳苦地寻求过。为此我度过了许多昼夜；召了许多智慧博学之士到我议事之廷；也与许多称为基督徒的人交谈过。借着不倦的询问与热切的搜寻，我发现了真理的路径——有以聪明睿智见尊的智者为证——除我们的信仰以外，别无他信。这就是我们今日所行的路：敬拜最有权能的诸神，牢牢持守那由他们赐给众人、充满各样享乐与心中欢畅的甘美生活；加利利人的首领与祭司们却在愚妄中弃绝了它，以致为了盼望另一种渺茫的生命，就轻易丢弃了这甘甜的光，丢弃了诸神赐给我们享受的一切逸乐，自己却始终不知道所说的是什么，也不晓得所满口断言的是什么。

「但最亲爱的儿子啊，你要听从你的父亲——他已借着勤勉诚实的寻求，寻得了真正的善。看哪，我已指示你：我未曾出于甘心、也未曾出于无知而错失了善，倒是寻见了善，并且牢牢地把握住它。我恳切盼望你也不作愚妄人四处游荡，而是跟随我。所以要顾念你的父亲。你岂不知道顺从父亲、凡事讨他喜悦，是何等可亲爱的事？反过来说，触怒父亲、藐视他的命令，又是何等致命、何等可咒诅的事？凡这样行的人，都落得悲惨的结局。我儿啊，你不要列在他们中间。倒要行那叫你父亲喜悦的事，这样，你就必得着一切幸福，承受我的祝福和我的国！」

===Original===
The high-minded and noble youth listened to his father’s windy discourse and foolish opposition, and recognized therein the devices of the crooked serpent, and how standing at his right hand he had prepared a snare for his feet, and was scheming how to overthrow his righteous soul, and hinder him of the prize laid up in store. Therefore the prince set before his eyes the commandment of the Lord, which saith, “I came not to send peace, but strife and a sword. For I am come to set a man at variance against his father, and a daughter against her mother,” and so forth; and “He that loveth father or mother more than me is not worthy of me”; and “Whosoever shall deny me before men, him will I also deny before my Father which is in heaven.” When he had considered these things, and fettered his soul with divine fear, and strengthened it with longing desire and love, right opportunely he remembered the saying of Solomon, “There is a time to love, and a time to hate; a time of war, and a time of peace.” First of all he prayed in silence, and said, “Have mercy of me, Lord God, have mercy of me; for my soul trusteth in thee; and under the shadow of thy wings I shall hope till wickedness overpass. I shall cry to the highest God; to God that did well to me,” and the rest of the psalm.
Then said Ioasaph to the king, “To honour one’s father, and to obey his commands, and to serve him with good will and affection is taught us by the Lord of us all, who hath implanted in our hearts this natural affection. But, when loving devotion to our parents bringeth our soul into peril, and separateth her from her Maker, then we are commanded, at all costs, to cut it out, and, on no account, to yield to them that would depart us from God, but to hate and avoid them, even if it be our father that issueth the abominable command, or our mother, or our king, or the master of our very life. Wherefore it is impossible for me, out of devotion to my father, to forfeit God. So, prithee, trouble not thyself, nor me: but be persuaded, and let us both serve the true and living God, for the objects of thy present worship are idols, the works of men’s hands, devoid of breath, and deaf, and give nought but destruction and eternal punishment to their worshippers.
===Chinese===
那心志高超、品性尊贵的少年听了父亲虚浮的长论与愚妄的抗拒，就认出其中那曲行的蛇的诡计：它如何站在他右边，为他的脚设下网罗，图谋倾覆他义人的灵魂，拦阻他得那为他存留的奖赏。于是王子把主的诫命摆在眼前，那诫命说：「我来并不是叫地上太平，乃是叫纷争与刀兵；因为我来是叫人与父亲生疏，女儿与母亲生疏」等等；又说：「爱父母过于爱我的，不配作我的门徒」；又说：「凡在人面前不认我的，我在我天上的父面前也必不认他。」他思想了这些事，用神圣的敬畏捆锁自己的灵魂，又以切慕与爱加以坚固，便正合其时地想起所罗门的话：「喜爱有时，恨恶有时；争战有时，和好有时。」他首先默祷，说：「主神啊，求你怜悯我，怜悯我；因为我的心投靠你；我要投靠在你翅膀的荫下，直到灾害过去。我要求告至高的神，就是施恩于我的神」——并诵念那篇诗的其余部分。

于是约阿萨弗对王说：「当敬重父亲、顺从他的命令、以善意与亲情事奉他，这是我们的主、我们众人的主所教训我们的；他将这天然的亲情栽种在我们心里。但当对父母的孺慕之情使我们的灵魂陷于危殆、使她与造她的主隔绝时，我们就受了吩咐：务要将这情连根铲除，决不可向那些使我们离开神的人让步，倒要恨恶他们、躲避他们——即使颁布这可憎命令的是我们的父亲，或我们的母亲，或我们的君王，或掌管我们性命的主。所以，要我因孝敬父亲的缘故而丧失神，这是不可能的。我求你，不要烦扰你自己，也不要烦扰我；倒要听劝，让我们二人都事奉那又真又活的神；因为你如今所敬拜的对象乃是偶像，是人手所造之物，没有气息，没有听觉，只能将灭亡与永远的刑罚报给敬拜它们的人。」

===Original===
“But if this be not thy pleasure, deal with me even as thou wilt: for I am a servant of Christ, and neither flatteries nor torments shall separate me from his love, as I told thee yesterday, swearing it by my Master’s name, and confirming the word with surest oath. But, whereas thou saidest that thou didst neither wilfully do wrong, nor didst fail of the mark through ignorance, but after much laborious enquiry hadst ascertained that it was truly a good thing to worship idols and to be riveted to the pleasures of the passions﻿—that thou art wilfully a wrong doer, I may not say. But this I know full well, and would have thee know, O my father, that thou art surrounded with a dense mist of ignorance, and, walking in darkness that may be felt, seest not even one small glimmer of light. Wherefore thou hast lost the right pathway, and wanderest over terrible cliffs and chasms. Holding darkness for light, and clinging to death as it were life, thou deemest that thou art well advised, and hast reflected to good effect: but it is not so, not so. The objects of thy veneration are not gods but statues of devils, charged with all their filthy power; nor is the life, which thou pronouncest sweet and pleasant, and thinkest to be full of delight and gladness of heart, such in kind: but the same is abominable, according to the word of truth, and to be abhorred. For for a time it sweeteneth and tickleth the gullet, but afterwards it maketh the risings more bitter than gall (as said my teacher), and is sharper than any two-edged sword.
“How shall I describe to thee the evils of this life? I will tell them, and they shall be more in number than the sand. For such life is the fishhook of the devil, baited with beastly pleasure, whereby he deceiveth and draggeth his prey into the depth of hell. Whereas the good things, promised by my Master, which thou callest ‘the hope of some other uncertain life,’ are true and unchangeable; they know no end, and are not subject to decay. There is no language that can declare the greatness of yonder glory and delight, of the joy unspeakable, and the everlasting gladness. As thou thyself seest, we all die; and there is no man that shall live and not see death. But one day we shall all rise again, when our Lord Jesus Christ shall come, the Son of God, in unspeakable glory and dread power, the only King of kings, and Lord of lords; to whom every knee shall bow, of things in heaven, and things in earth, and things under the earth. Such terror shall he then inspire that the very powers of heaven shall be shaken: and before him there shall stand in fear thousand thousands, and ten thousand times ten thousand of Angels and Archangels, and the whole world shall be full of fear and terror. For one of the Archangels shall sound with the trump of God, and immediately the heavens shall be rolled together as a scroll; and the earth shall be rent, and shall give up the dead bodies of all men that ever were since the first man Adam until that day. And then shall all men that have died since the beginning of the world in the twinkling of an eye stand alive before the judgement seat of the immortal Lord, and every man shall give account of his deeds. Then shall the righteous shine forth as the sun; they that believed in the Father, Son and Holy Ghost, and ended this present life in good works. And how can I describe to thee the glory that shall receive them at that day? For though I compare their brightness and beauty to the light of the sun or to the brightest lightning flash, yet should I fail to do justice to their brightness. Eye hath not seen, nor ear heard, neither have entered into the heart of man, the things which God hath prepared for them that love him, in the kingdom of heaven, in the light which no man can approach unto, in his unspeakable and unending glory.
“Such joys and such bliss shall the righteous obtain, but they that have denied the only true God and not known their Maker and Creator, but have worshipped foul devils, and rendered homage to dumb idols, and loved the pleasures of this vain world, and, like swine, wallowed in the mire of sinful lusts, and made their lives a headquarters for all wickedness, shall stand naked and laid bare, downright ashamed and downcast, pitiable in appearance and in fact, set forth for a reproach to all creation. All their life in word, deed and thought shall come before their faces. Then, after this bitter disgrace and unbearable reproach, shall they be sentenced to the unquenchable and lightless fire of Gehenna, unto the outer darkness, the gnashing of teeth and the venomous worm. This is their portion, this their lot, in the which they shall dwell together in punishment for endless ages, because they rejected the good things offered them in promise, and, for the sake of the pleasure of sin for a season, made choice of eternal punishment. For these reasons﻿—to obtain that unspeakable bliss, to enjoy that ineffable glory, to equal the Angels in splendour, and to stand with boldness before the good and most sweetest Lord, to escape those bitter and unending punishments and that galling shame﻿—time after time, were it not worth men’s while to sacrifice their riches and bodies, nay, even their very lives? Who is so cowardly, who so foolish, as not to endure a thousand temporal deaths, to escape eternal and everlasting death, and to inherit life, blissful and imperishable, and to shine in the light of the blessed and life-giving Trinity?”
===Chinese===
「但你若不以为然，就随你的意思待我吧：因为我是基督的仆人，谄媚也好，酷刑也好，都不能叫我与他的爱隔绝——正如我昨日告诉你的，我曾奉我主人的名起誓，用最确凿的誓言坚立了这话。至于你说，你既非故意作恶，也非因无知而失了标的，倒是经过多方劳苦的查究，确知敬拜偶像、钉牢在情欲的享乐上确是美事——说你故意作恶，这话我不敢说。但这一点我深知，也愿你得知，我的父亲啊：你正陷在无知的浓雾包围之中，行在摸得着的黑暗里，连一线微光也看不见。因此你已迷失了正路，在可怕的悬崖深壑之间流离。你以黑暗为光，抱住死亡如同生命，还自以为深谋远虑、思虑得当：然而不是这样，断乎不是这样。你所敬奉的对象并不是神，乃是浸满了鬼魔一切污秽权能的鬼魔之像；那被你称为甘甜愉悦、你以为满有欢愉与心中喜乐的生活，实质也并非如此：按着真理的道，它乃是可憎的，当受厌弃。因为它片时之间发甜，酥痒人的喉咙，过后却使上涌的滋味比胆汁更苦（正如我的教师所说），又比一切两刃的剑更快。

「我怎能向你述说这今生的种种祸患呢？我若一一述说，它们必比海沙更多。因为这样的生活就是魔鬼的鱼钩，挂着兽性享乐的饵，魔鬼借它行骗，将猎物拖进地狱的深处。而我主所应许的那些美事——就是你所称『另一种渺茫生命的盼望』——却是真实而不能更改的；它们没有终局，也不服在朽坏之下。那世荣耀与福乐之大、那说不出来的喜乐与永远的欢欣，没有任何言语能够述说。如你自己所见的，我们人人都死；没有一个活着的人能不见死。但有一日我们都要复活，那时我们的主耶稣基督——神的儿子——要带着说不出的荣耀与可畏的权能降临，就是那独一的万王之王、万主之主；一切在天上的、地上的、地底下的，都必向他屈膝。他所显的威严如此可畏，连天势都必震动；千千万万的天使与天使长，都必战兢侍立在他面前；全世界都必充满惧怕惊惶。因为有一位天使长要吹响神的号，诸天便立时如书卷被卷起来；地也必裂开，交出自第一人亚当直到那日、一切曾存活之人的尸身。那时，自创世以来一切死了的人，都要在一霎时、眨眼之间活活站在那不朽之主的审判座前，各人都要为自己的行为交账。那时，义人必要发出光来，如同太阳——就是那些信了父、子、圣灵，又在善工里结束了今生的人。那日他们所要领受的荣耀，我怎能向你形容呢？因为纵然我把他们的光辉华美比作日光，或比作最耀眼的闪电，仍不足以描摹他们的明亮。『神为爱他的人所预备的，是眼睛未曾看见，耳朵未曾听见，人心也未曾想到的』——在那国度里，在那不能靠近的光里，在他那不可言喻、永无穷尽的荣耀里。

「这样的喜乐、这样的福气，义人必得着。至于那些否认了独一真神、不认识造他们的主与创造者的人——他们敬拜污秽的鬼魔，向哑巴偶像叩拜，贪爱这虚妄世界的逸乐，像猪一样在罪恶情欲的泥泞里打滚，又使自己的一生作了万恶的巢穴——他们必赤身露体、一无遮蔽地站立，全然羞愧、垂头丧气，外貌与实情俱皆可怜，被陈列出来，作一切受造之物的羞辱。他们一生的言语、行为与意念，都必摆在他们眼前。然后，经过这苦涩的耻辱与难忍的凌辱，他们必被判投于地狱那不灭而无光的火里，落到外边的黑暗、哀哭切齿与不死毒虫之处。这就是他们的分，这就是他们的业；他们要在其中一同受罚，直到无穷无尽的世代，因为他们弃绝了应许中赐给他们的美事，为了暂时的罪中之乐，拣选了永远的刑罚。为了这些缘故——为要得着那说不出的福气、享受那莫可名状的荣耀、在荣光上媲美天使、坦然无惧站在那良善至甘甜的主面前、逃脱那些苦楚无尽的刑罚与那难堪的羞辱——人就是一次又一次舍掉财富与身体，甚至舍掉性命本身，岂不也是值得的吗？谁是这样怯懦、谁是这样愚妄，竟不肯忍受千次暂时的死，以逃脱那永永远远的死，承受那有福而不朽的生命，在那可称颂而赐生命的三一神的光中发光呢？」

## XXVI / 二十六

===Original===
When the king heard these words, and saw the steadfastness, and unbuxomness of his son, who yielded neither to flattery, nor persuasion, nor threat, he marvelled indeed at the persuasiveness of his speech and his irrefutable answers, and was convicted by his own conscience secretly assuring him that Ioasaph spake truly and aright. But he was dragged back by his evil habit and passions, which, from long use, had taken firm grip on him, and held him in as with bit and bridle, and suffered him not to behold the light of truth. So he left no stone unturned, as the saying is, and adhered to his old purpose, determining to put into action the plot which he and Araches had between them devised. Said he to his son, “Although, child, thou oughtest in all points simply to give in to my commands, yet, because thou art stubborn and disobedient, and hast thus stiffly opposed me, insisting that thine own opinion should prevail over all, bid we now farewell to vain insistance, and let persuasion be now our policy. And, forasmuch as Barlaam, thy deceiver, is here, my prisoner in iron chains, I will make a great assembly, and summon all our people and your Galileans, to one place; and I will charge heralds to proclaim expressly that none of the Christians shall fear, but that all shall muster without dread; and we will hold debate together. If your side win, then shall ye and your Barlaam gain your desires; but if ye lose, then shall ye with right good will yield yourselves to my commands.”
But this truly wise and prudent youth, forewarned, by the heavenly vision sent him, of his father’s mischief, replied, “The Lord’s will be done! Be it according to thy command! May our good God and Lord himself vouchsafe that we wander not from the right way, for my soul trusteth in him, and he shall be merciful unto me.” There and then did the king command all, whether idolaters or Christians, to assemble. Letters were despatched in all quarters: heralds proclaimed it in every village town that no Christian need fear any secret surprise, but all might come together without fear, as friends and kindred, for the honest and unrestrained enquiry that should be held with their chief and captain, Barlaam. In like manner also he summoned the initiate and the temple-keepers of his idols, and wise men of the Chaldeans and Indians that were in all his kingdom, beside certain augurs, sorcerers and seers, that they might get the better of the Christians.
Then were there gathered together multitudes that held his loathly religion; but of the Christians was there found one only that came to the help of the supposed Barlaam. His name was Barachias. For of the Faithful, some were dead, having fallen victims to the fury of the governors of the cities; and some were hiding in mountains and dens, in dread of the terrors hanging over them; while others had feared the threats of the king, and durst not adventure themselves into the light of day, but were worshippers by night, serving Christ in secret, and in no wise boldly confessing him. So noble-hearted Barachias came alone to the contest, to help and champion the truth.

===Chinese===
王听见这些话，又见儿子如此坚定、如此倔强不屈——谄媚不能使他屈从，劝诱不能，威吓也不能——就实在诧异他言语的说服力，和他那些无可辩驳的回答；他更被自己的良心所定罪，因为良心暗暗向他作证：约阿萨弗所讲的真实而正当。但他却被自己的恶习和情欲拽了回去：这些恶习与情欲因着长久的惯行，已把他牢牢攫住，如嚼环辔头一般笼住他，不容他看见真理之光。于是他如俗语所说，连一块石头也不留着不翻，仍旧固守他素来的主意，决意要把他与阿拉凯斯二人共同设计的诡计付诸实行。他对儿子说：「孩子啊，论理你在凡事上都该单纯地顺从我的命令；但你既顽梗又不顺服，这样强硬地顶撞我，执意要你自己的主张压倒一切，如今我们就向虚妄的强辩道别，以劝服为我们今日的方策吧。况且，那迷惑你的巴拉阿姆既在这里，戴着铁链作我的囚徒，我就必聚集一个大会，将我们全体百姓和你们这些加利利人都召到一处；我必吩咐传令官明明宣告：基督徒都不要惧怕，只管人人坦然聚合；我们要一同辩论。你们这一方若得胜，你和你的巴拉阿姆就可以如愿以偿；你们若失败，就当甘心乐意地顺服我的命令。」
但这位真正有智慧而明达的少年，既蒙所赐天上的异象预先警示，知道他父亲的恶谋，就回答说：「愿主的旨意成就！就照你的命令行吧！愿我们良善的神、我们的主亲自施恩，叫我们不偏离正路，因为我的心倚靠他，他必施怜悯与我。」王当即吩咐众人，无论拜偶像的还是基督徒，一概聚集。文书发往四方；传令官在各城各村宣告：基督徒不必惧怕有什么暗中的埋伏，人人都可以坦然无惧地聚到一处，如同朋友亲属一样，为要与他们的首领和统帅巴拉阿姆，进行诚实而无拘束的质询。他又照样召聚他诸偶像的入教之众与守庙人，并他全国之中迦勒底人与印度人的智慧人，还有若干占卜的、行邪术的和观兆的，要叫他们胜过基督徒。
于是，有许多信奉他那可憎宗教的人聚集了来；而在基督徒中间，前来帮助那被当作巴拉阿姆之人的，只寻得一位，名叫巴拉加（Barachias）。因为在信徒之中，有的已经死了，作了众城统领怒火的祭物；有的藏在山岭与洞穴里，惧怕那悬在他们头上的威吓；又有的惧怕王的恐吓，不敢冒险现身于白日光中，只作夜间的敬拜者，暗中事奉基督，决不敢放胆认他。这样，心志高贵的巴拉加便独自前来赴这场较量，为要扶助真理，作真理的辩护者。

===Original===
The king sat down before all on a doom-stool high and exalted, and bade his son sit beside him. He, in reverence and awe of his father, consented not thereto, but sat near him on the ground. There stood the learned in the wisdom which God hath made foolish, whose unwise hearts had gone astray, as saith the Apostle; for, “professing themselves to be wise, they became fools, and changed the glory of the uncorruptible God into an image made like to corruptible man, and four-footed beasts, and creeping things.” These were assembled for to join argument with the king’s son and his fellows, and on them was fulfilled the proverb, “Gazelle against lion.” The one made the Most High his house of defence, and his hope was under the shadow of his wings; while the others trusted in the princes of this world, who are made of none effect, and in the ruler of the darkness of this world, to whom they have subjected themselves miserably and wretchedly.
Now came on Nachor, in the disguise of Barlaam; and the king’s side were like to reach their goal; but, once again, very different was the ordering of the wise providence of God. When all the company was come, thus spake the king to his orators and philosophers, or rather to the deceivers of his people, and fools at heart, “Behold now, there lieth before you a contest, even the mightiest of contests; for one of two things shall befall you. If ye establish our cause, and prove Barlaam and his friends to be in error, ye shall have your fill of glory and honour from us and all the senate, and shall be crowned with crowns of victory. But if ye be worsted, in all ignominy ye shall pitiably perish, and all your goods shall be given to the people, that your memorial may be clean blotted out from off the earth. Your bodies will I give to be devoured by wild beasts and your children will I deliver to perpetual slavery.”
When the king had thus spoken, his son said, “A righteous doom hast thou judged this day, O king. The Lord establish this thy mind! I too have the same bidding for my teacher.” And, turning round to Nachor, who was supposed to be Barlaam, he said, “Thou knowest, Barlaam, in what splendour and luxury thou foundest me. With many a speech thou persuadedst me to leave my father’s laws and customs, and to serve an unknown God, drawn by the promise of some unspeakable and eternal blessings, to follow thy doctrines and to provoke to anger my father and lord. Now therefore consider that thou art weighed in the balance. If thou overcome in the wrestling, and prove that the doctrines, which thou hast taught me, be true, and show that they, that try a fall with us, be in error, thou shalt be magnified as no man heretofore, and shalt be entitled ‘herald of truth’; and I will abide in thy doctrine and serve Christ, even as thou didst preach, until my dying breath. But if thou be worsted, by foul play or fair, and thus bring shame on me today, speedily will I avenge me of mine injury; with mine own hands will I quickly tear out thy heart and thy tongue, and throw them with the residue of thy carcase to be meat for the dogs, that others may be lessoned by thee not to cozen the sons of kings.”

===Chinese===
王在众人面前坐上那高高在上、极其尊崇的审判座，吩咐儿子坐在他旁边。王子出于对父亲的敬畏尊崇，不肯依从，只就近坐在他身旁的地上。那里站着众博学之士，通晓那「神已使变成愚拙」的智慧；他们无知的心走迷了，如使徒所说；因为「他们自称为聪明，反成了愚拙，将不能朽坏之神的荣耀变为偶像的样式，仿佛必朽坏的人和走兽、昆虫」。这些人聚集，要与王的儿子和他的同伴辩驳；在他们身上正应了「瞪羚敌狮子」的谚语：一方以至高者为保障的居所，他的指望就在他翅膀的荫下；另一方却倚靠那将要归于无有的世上众掌权者，倚靠那管辖这幽暗世界的，悲惨而可怜地自服于他。
这时，纳霍尔（Nachor）扮作巴拉阿姆进前来；王的一党眼看就要达到目的；然而神智慧的护理所安排的，再一次截然不同。众人既已到齐，王便对众演说家和哲学家——不如说，对他百姓的迷惑者、心中的愚妄人——这样说：「看哪，如今摆在你们面前的，是一场较量，且是诸般较量中至大的一场；因为两件事中必有一件临到你们。你们若坚立我们的事业，证明巴拉阿姆和他的同伙陷于错谬，就必从我们和全体元老得着满盈的荣耀尊贵，并戴上得胜的冠冕。你们若被击败，就必在极大的羞辱中可怜地灭亡，你们的一切财物必分给百姓，使你们的纪念从地上尽行涂抹。你们的身体，我要给野兽吞吃；你们的儿女，我要交付永远的奴役。」
王说了这话，他儿子就说：「王啊，你今日所断的判决是公义的。愿主坚立你这样的心意！对我的教师，我也下同样的吩咐。」于是他转过身来，对那被众人当作巴拉阿姆的纳霍尔说：「巴拉阿姆，你知道你遇见我的时候，我正处在怎样的荣华奢靡之中。你用许多话劝我离弃我父亲的律法习俗，去事奉一位未识之神，被那不可言喻、永恒的福分应许所吸引，跟从了你的教训，惹动我父亲我主的怒气。所以如今你要思想，你是被称在天平里的。你若在这场角力中得胜，证明你所教我的教训是真实的，又显明那些与我们较力的人陷于错谬，你必被高举，为历来任何人所不及，并要得称『真理的传令官』；我也必持守你的教训，照你所传讲的事奉基督，直到我断气的一刻。但你若被击败——无论凭正当还是卑鄙的手段——今日这样使我蒙羞，我必速速报复我的羞辱；我必亲手剜出你的心和你的舌头，把它们同你尸身的其余部分一起丢给狗作食物，叫后人从你得着鉴戒，不敢再欺哄王的众子。」

===Original===
When Nachor heard these words, he was exceeding sorrowful and downcast, seeing himself falling into the destruction that he had made for other, and being drawn into the net that he had laid privily, and feeling the sword entering into his own soul. So he took counsel with himself, and determined rather to take the side of the king’s son, and make it to prevail, that he might avoid the danger hanging over him, because the prince was doubtless able to requite him, should he be found to provoke him. But this was all the work of divine providence that was wisely establishing our cause by the mouth of our adversaries. For when these idol-priests and Nachor crossed words, like another Barlaam, who, of old in the time of Balak, when purposing to curse Israel, loaded him with manifold blessings, so did Nachor mightily resist these unwise and unlearned wise men.
There sat the king upon his throne, his son beside him, as we have said. There beside him stood these unwise orators who had whetted their tongues like a sharp sword, to destroy truth, and who (as saith Esay) conceive mischief and bring forth iniquity. There were gathered innumerable multitudes, come to view the contest and see which side should carry oft the victory. Then one of the orators, the most eminent of all his fellows, said unto Nachor, “Art thou that Barlaam which hath so shamelessly and audaciously blasphemed our gods, and hath enmeshed our king’s well beloved son in the net of error, and taught him to serve the Crucified?” Nachor answered, “I am he, I am Barlaam, that, as thou sayest, doth set your gods at nought: but the king’s son have I not enmeshed in error; but rather from error have I delivered him, and brought him to the true God.” The orator replied, “When the great and marvellous men, who have discovered all knowledge of wisdom, do call them high and immortal gods, and when all the kings and honourable men upon earth do worship and adore them, how waggest thou tongue against them, and, in brief, how durst thou be so mighty brazen-faced? What is the manner of thy proof that the Crucified is God, and these be none?” Then replied Nachor, disdaining even to answer the speaker. He beckoned with his hand to the multitude to keep silence, and opening his mouth, like Balaam’s ass, spake that which he had not purposed to say, and thus addressed the king.

===Chinese===
纳霍尔听见这些话，就极其忧愁沮丧：他看见自己正掉进他为别人所掘的毁灭坑里，被拉进自己暗设的网罗，又觉得有刀剑刺入自己的心。他就在心里自商自议，定意不如站到王子的一边，助他得胜，好避开那临到自己头上的危险；因为倘若他被查出激怒了王子，王子无疑有能力报应他。然而这全是神护理的作为——他藉着我们仇敌的口，智慧地坚立我们的事业。因为当这些偶像祭司与纳霍尔交锋的时候，他就像古时的另一位巴兰：那巴兰在巴勒（Balak）的日子，本存意要咒诅以色列，反倒给以色列加上诸般的祝福；纳霍尔也这样，大力抵挡这些无智无学的「智慧人」。
王坐在宝座上，儿子在他旁边，如我们前面所说。他身旁站着这些无知的演说家，他们把舌头磨得如快利的剑，要败坏真理，（如以赛亚所说）「所怀的是毒害，所生的是罪孽」。那里聚集了数不尽的群众，来观看这场较量，要看哪一方夺得胜利。于是众演说家中有一位，是他同辈之中最杰出的，对纳霍尔说：「你就是那巴拉阿姆么？就是那如此无耻放肆地亵渎我们诸神，将我们王所钟爱的儿子缠在错谬的网罗里，又教他事奉那被钉十字架者的？」纳霍尔回答说：「我就是，我就是巴拉阿姆；正如你所说，我实在把你们的诸神看为无有；但王的儿子，我并没有把他缠在错谬里，倒是把他从错谬里救出来，领他到了真神面前。」演说家回答说：「当那些寻得一切智慧知识的大而可畏之人，尚且称他们为崇高不朽的诸神，地上众王与一切尊贵之人也都敬拜尊崇他们，你竟怎敢向他们摇动口舌？简而言之，你怎敢如此厚颜大胆？你有何凭据说那被钉十字架者是神，而这些不是？」纳霍尔这才回答，却全然不屑回答那发问的人。他挥手示意众人安静，开口——像巴兰的驴一样——说出他本没有打算说的话，向王这样说。

## XXVII / 二十七

===Original===
“By the providence of God, O king, came I into the world; and when I contemplated heaven and earth and sea, the sun and moon, and the other heavenly bodies, I was led to marvel at their fair order. And, when I beheld the world and all that therein is, how it is moved by law, I understood that he who moveth and sustaineth it is God. That which moveth is ever stronger than that which is moved, and that which sustaineth is stronger than that which is sustained. Him therefore I call God, who constructed all things and sustaineth them, without beginning, without end, immortal, without want, above all passions, and failings, such as anger, forgetfulness, ignorance, and the like. By him all things consist. He hath no need of sacrifice, or drink-offering, or of any of the things that we see, but all men have need of him.

“Now that I have said thus much concerning God, according as he hath granted me to speak concerning himself, come we now to the human race, that we may know which of them partake of truth, and which of error. It is manifiest to us, O king, that there are three races of men in this world: those that are worshippers of them whom ye call gods, and Jews, and Christians. And again those who serve many gods are divided into three races, Chaldeans, Greeks and Egyptians, for these are to the other nations the leaders and teachers of the service and worship of the gods whose name is legion. Let us therefore see which of these hold the truth, and which error.

“The Chaldeans, which knew not God, went astray after the elements and began to worship the creature rather than their Creator, and they made figures of these creatures and called them likenesses of heaven, and earth and sea, of sun and moon, and of the other elements or luminaries. And they enclose them in temples, and worship them under the title of gods, and guard them in safety lest they be stolen by robbers. They have not understood how that which guardeth is ever greater than that which is guarded, and that the maker is greater than the thing that is made; for, if the gods be unable to take care of themselves, how can they take care of others? Great then is the error that the Chaldeans have erred in worshipping lifeless and useless images. And I am moved to wonder, O king, how they, who are called philosophers among them, fail to understand that even the very elements are corruptible.
