But if the elements are corruptible and subject to necessity, how are they gods? And if the elements are not gods, how are the images, created to their honour, gods?

“Come we then, O king, to the elements themselves, that we may prove concerning them, that they are not gods, but corruptible and changeable things, brought out of nonexistence by the command of him who is God indeed, who is incorruptible, and unchangeable, and invisible, but yet himself seeth all things, and, as he willeth, changeth and altereth the same. What then must I say about the elements?

===Chinese===
「王啊，我是凭着神的护理来到世上的。当我静观天地海洋、日月并其余诸天体时，便不禁惊叹它们美好的次序；又观看世界和其中的万物，见它们都循规律而运转，我便明白：那推动并托住它的，乃是神。凡推动的，总强于被推动的；凡托住的，总强于被托住的。所以，我称那构造万物、又托住万有的为神：他无始无终，不朽不坏，无所匮乏，超乎一切情欲与软弱——诸如忿怒、健忘、无知之类。万有都靠他而立。他不需要祭牲，不需要奠祭，也不需要我们所见的任何事物；倒是万人都需要他。

「论到神，我已照他所赐、论到他自己的话说了这许多；现在让我们来到人类，好知道他们中间谁是有分于真理的，谁是有分于错谬的。王啊，我们明知，世上的人分为三类：敬拜你们所谓之神的人、犹太人，并基督徒。而事奉多神的人又分为三族：迦勒底人、希腊人和埃及人；因为对其余各族而言，他们乃是事奉并敬拜那名为『群』之诸神的首领与师傅。那么，让我们看看这三族中谁是持守真理的，谁是持守错谬的。

「迦勒底人不认识神，随从众元素走入迷途，开始敬拜受造之物，而不敬奉那造物的主；又为这些受造之物造像，称之为天、地、海，日月并其余元素或光体的形像。他们将它们禁闭在庙宇中，奉以神的名号来敬拜，又妥善守卫，惟恐被盗贼偷去。他们不明白：凡看守的，总大于受看守的；凡造作的，总大于所造作的。神明若无力看顾自己，怎能看顾别物呢？这样，迦勒底人敬拜无生命、无用处的形像，实在是错谬甚大。王啊，我又不禁诧异，他们中称为哲学家的，怎么竟不明白连众元素本身也是必朽坏的。元素若会朽坏、又服在必然之下，怎能是神呢？元素若不是神，为尊荣它们而造的像，又怎能是神呢？

「王啊，那么让我们来到众元素本身，好证明它们并非神明，乃是朽坏流变之物，是奉那位真神——他不能朽坏、不能改变、不能看见，自己却鉴察万物，又随己意更改变化万有——之命，从无有中被领出来的。论到众元素，我该说什么呢？

===Original===
“They, who ween that the Heaven is a god, are in error. For we see it turning and moving by law, and consisting of many parts, whence also it is called Cosmos!9 Now a ‘Cosmos’ is the handiwork of some artificer; and that which is wrought by handiwork hath beginning and end. And the firmament is moved by law together with its luminaries. The stars are borne from Sign to Sign, each in his order and place: some rise, while others set: and they run their journey according to fixed seasons, to fulfil summer and winter, as it hath been ordained for them by God, nor do they transgress their proper bounds, according to the inexorable law of nature, in common with the heavenly firmament. Whence it is evident that the heaven is not a god, but only a work of God.

“They again that think that the Earth is a goddess have gone astray. We behold it dishonoured, mastered, defiled and rendered useless by mankind. If it be baked by the sun, it becometh dead, for nothing groweth from a potsherd. And again, if it be soaked overmuch, it rotteth, fruit and all. It is trodden under foot of men and the residue of the beasts: it is polluted with the blood of the murdered, it is digged and made a grave for dead bodies. This being so, Earth can in no wise be a goddess, but only the work of God for the use of men.

“They that think that Water is a god have gone astray. It also hath been made for the use of men. It is under their lordship: it is polluted, and perisheth: it is altered by boiling, by dyeing, by congealment, or by being brought to the cleansing of all defilements. Wherefore Water cannot be a god, but only the work of God.

“They that think that Fire is a god are in error. It too was made for the use of men. It is subject to their lordship, being carried about from place to place, for the seething and roasting of all manner of meats, yea, and for the burning of dead corpses. Moreover, it perisheth in divers ways, when it is quenched by mankind. Wherefore Fire cannot be a god, but only the work of God.

“They that think that the breath of the Winds is a god are in error. This, as is evident, is subject to another, and hath been prepared by God, for the sake of mankind, for the carriage of ships, and the conveyance of victuals, and for other uses of men, it riseth and falleth according to the ordinance of God. Wherefore it is not to be supposed that the breath of the Winds is a god, but only the work of God.

===Chinese===
「那些以为天是神的人，实在错了。因为我们看见它循规律转动运行，又由许多部分构成——故此它也称为科斯摩斯（Cosmos）9！而『科斯摩斯』乃是某位匠人的手工；凡出于手工的，必有始有终。穹苍与其光体一同循规律运转。众星从一宫运到一宫，各按自己的等次与本位：有的升起，有的沉落；它们按一定的节期奔跑路程，成就夏冬，正如神为他们所命定的；他们也与天上穹苍一同循自然那不可抗拒的律，不越过自己的界限。由此可见，天并不是神，只是神的工作。

「那些以为地是女神的人，也走迷了。我们眼见它受人羞辱、制伏、污秽，变为无用。它若被日头烤硬，就成了死的，因为碎瓦上长不出什么；它若浸水过度，便连果实带根全都腐烂。它被人脚和其余走兽践踏在脚下；它被凶杀的血玷污，被掘开作死人的坟墓。事既如此，地决不能是女神，只是神为供人使用而造的工作。

「那些以为水是神的人走迷了。水也是为人使用而造的。它服在人的权下：被污秽、会败亡；煮沸、染色、凝结，或被取来洗除各样的污秽时，它就改变了。所以水不能是神，只是神的工作。

「那些以为火是神的人错了。火也是为人使用而造的。它服在人的权下，被人从一处搬到另一处，用来煮烤各样的食物，甚至用来焚烧死人的尸首。何况，它被人扑灭时，还以种种方式消灭。所以火不能是神，只是神的工作。

「那些以为风的气息是神的人错了。风，正如显而易见的，是服在另一位之下的，且是神为人预备的：为着船只的航行、粮糈的运转，并为人的其他用处；它按神的定例兴起又平息。所以不可设想风的气息是神，它只是神的工作。

===Original===
“They that think that the Sun is a god are in error. We see him moving and turning by law, and passing from Sign to Sign, setting and rising, to warm herbs and trees for the use of men, sharing power with the other stars, being much less than the heaven, and falling into eclipse and possessed of no sovranty of his own. Wherefore we may not consider that the Sun is a god, but only the work of God.

“They that think that the Moon is a goddess are in error. We behold her moving and turning by law, and passing from Sign to Sign, setting and rising for the use of men, lesser than the sun, waxing and waning, suffering eclipse. Wherefore we do not consider that the Moon is a goddess, but only the work of God.

“They that think that Man is a god are in error. We see man moving by law, growing up, and waxing old, even against his will. Now he rejoiceth, now he grieveth, requiring meat and drink and raiment. Besides he is passionate, envious, lustful, fickle, and full of failings: and he perisheth in many a way, by the elements, by wild beasts, and by the death that ever awaiteth him. So Man cannot be a god, but only the work of God. Great then is the error that the Chaldeans have erred in following their own lusts; for they worship corruptible elements and dead images, neither do they perceive that they are making gods of these.

===Chinese===
「那些以为太阳是神的人错了。我们看见它循规律运转，从一宫行到一宫，落下又升起，为供人使用而温暖草木；它与别的星辰同分权能，远小于天，会遭掩蚀，自己并无主权。所以我们不可认为太阳是神，它只是神的工作。

「那些以为月亮是女神的人错了。我们看见她循规律运转，从一宫行到一宫，落下又升起，为人所用；她小于太阳，有盈有亏，也遭掩蚀。所以我们不认为月亮是女神，她只是神的工作。

「那些以为人是神的人错了。我们看见人循规律而动，长大而衰老，连违背自己的心愿也是如此。他时而喜乐，时而忧伤，需要饮食和衣裳。此外，他又易怒、嫉妒、纵欲、善变，满有缺欠，且在种种途径中灭亡：或因众元素，或因野兽，或因那永远候着他的死。所以人不能是神，只是神的工作。这样，迦勒底人随从自己的私欲，实在是错谬甚大；因为他们敬拜必朽坏的元素和死的形像，还不觉察自己正在以这些为神。

===Original===
“Now come we to the Greeks that we may see whether they have any understanding concerning God. The Greeks, then, professing themselves to be wise, fell into greater folly than the Chaldeans, alleging the existence of many gods, some male, others female, creators of all passions and sins of every kind. Wherefore the Greeks, O king, introduced an absurd, foolish and ungodly fashion of talk, calling them gods that were not, according to their own evil passions; that, having these gods for advocates of their wickedness, they might commit adultery, theft, murder and all manner of iniquity. For if their gods did so, how should they not themselves do the like? Therefore from these practices of error it came to pass that men suffered frequent wars and slaughters and cruel captivities. But if now we choose to pass in review each one of these gods, what a strange sight shalt thou see!

“First and foremost they introduce the god whom they call Kronos, and to him they sacrifice their own children, to him who had many sons by Rhea, and in a fit of madness ate his own children. And they say that Zeus cut off his privy parts, and cast them into the sea, whence, as fable telleth, was born Aphrodite. So Zeus bound his own father, and cast him into Tartarus. Dost thou mark the delusion and lasciviousness that they allege against their gods? Is it possible then that one who was prisoner and mutilated should be a god? What folly? What man in his senses could admit it?

“Next they introduce Zeus, who, they say, became king of the gods, and would take the shape of animals, that he might defile mortal women. They show him transformed into a bull, for Europa; into gold, for Danae; into a swan, for Leda; into a satyr, for Antiope; and into a thunderbolt, for Semele. Then of these were born many children, Dionysus, Zethus, Amphion, Herakles, Apollo, Artemis, Perseus, Castor, Helen, Polydeukes, Minos, Rhadamanthos, Sarpedon, and the nine daughters whom they call the Muses.

===Chinese===
「现在让我们来到希腊人，看看他们对神可有任何认识。希腊人自称为聪明，反陷入了比迦勒底人更深的愚拙，宣称有许多神存在，有男有女，是一切情欲和各样罪孽的创造者。这样，王啊，希腊人便引入了一种荒谬、愚昧而不敬虔的谈论方式，按着自己邪恶的情欲，把那不存在的称为神；好得着这些神作他们恶行的辩护者，使他们得以犯奸淫、偷盗、凶杀并各样的不义。他们的神尚且这样行，他们自己岂不也照样行么？因此，出于这些错谬的行径，人就屡遭战祸、屠杀和残酷的掳掠。如今我们若把这些神明一一检视，王啊，你要看见的是一幅何等怪异的景象！

「他们首先引进那位称为克洛诺斯（Kronos）的神，并把自己的儿女献给他为祭——这一位与瑞亚（Rhea）生了许多儿子，却在癫狂发作时吞吃自己儿女的。他们又说，宙斯（Zeus）割下他的下体，抛在海里，据传说所讲，阿佛洛狄忒（Aphrodite）便由此而生。宙斯又捆绑自己的父亲，把他丢进塔尔塔罗斯（Tartarus）。你可看出他们加在自己神明身上的迷乱与淫乱么？这样，一个作过囚虏、受过残害的，可能是神么？何等的愚妄？头脑清醒的人谁能承认呢？

「接着他们引进宙斯。据说他作了众神之王，又变作兽类的形状，好玷辱必朽的妇人。他们描绘他变作公牛，为着欧罗巴（Europa）；变作金雨，为着达那厄（Danae）；变作天鹅，为着勒达（Leda）；变作萨提洛斯（satyr），为着安提俄佩（Antiope）；又变作雷霆，为着塞墨勒（Semele）。从这些便生下许多儿女：狄俄尼索斯（Dionysus）、泽托斯（Zethus）、安菲翁（Amphion）、赫拉克勒斯（Herakles）、阿波罗（Apollo）、阿尔忒弥斯（Artemis）、珀耳修斯（Perseus）、卡斯托耳（Castor）、海伦（Helen）、波吕丢刻斯（Polydeukes）、弥诺斯（Minos）、拉达曼提斯（Rhadamanthos）、萨耳佩冬（Sarpedon），并他们称为缪斯（Muses）的九个女儿。

===Original===
“In like manner they introduce the story of Ganymede. And so befell it, O king, that men imitated all these things, and became adulterers, and defilers of themselves with mankind, and doers of other monstrous deeds, in imitation of their god. How then can an adulterer, one that defileth himself by unnatural lust, a slayer of his father be a god?

“With Zeus also they represent one Hephaestus as a god, and him lame, holding hammer and fire-tongs, and working as a coppersmith for hire. So it appeareth that he is needy. But it is impossible for one who is lame and wanteth men’s aid to be a God.

“After him, they represent as a god Hermes, a lusty fellow, a thief, and a covetous, a sorcerer, bowlegged, and an interpreter of speech. It is impossible for such an one to be a God.

“They also exhibit Asklepius as god, a physician, a maker of medicines, a compounder of plasters for his livelihood (for he is a needy wight), and in the end, they say, he was struck by Zeus with a thunderbolt, because of Tyndareus, son of Lakedaemon, and thus perished. Now if Asklepius, though a god, when struck by a thunderbolt, could not help himself, how can he help others?

===Chinese===
「他们又照样引进伽倪墨得斯（Ganymede）的故事。王啊，于是就有了这样的事：人效法这一切，成了奸淫的、与男人苟合玷辱己身的，并效法他们的神行出别样骇人之事的。这样，一个奸淫的、以逆性情欲玷辱己身的、弑父的人，怎能是神呢？

「他们又把赫菲斯托斯（Hephaestus）与宙斯同表为神：他是个瘸腿的，拿着锤子和火钳，作雇工铜匠挣钱度日。这样看来，他是穷乏的。但一个瘸腿的、需要人帮助的，不可能是神。

「在他之后，他们又把赫尔墨斯（Hermes）表为神：一个纵欲之徒、贼、贪财的、行邪术的、罗圈腿的、传话通译的。这样的人不可能是神。

「他们又把阿斯克勒庇俄斯（Asklepius）展示为神：一位医生、制药的、为糊口而调制膏药的（因为他是个穷乏人）；末了，据说他因拉科代蒙（Lakedaemon）之子廷达瑞俄斯（Tyndareus）的缘故，被宙斯用雷霆击杀，就这样灭亡了。阿斯克勒庇俄斯既称为神，被雷霆击打时尚且不能自救，怎能救别人呢？

===Original===
“Ares is represented as a warlike god, emulous, and covetous of sheep and other things. But in the end they say he was taken in adultery with Aphrodite by the child Eros and Hephaestus and was bound by them. How then can the covetous, the warrior, the bondman and adulterer be a god?

“Dionysus they show as a god, who leadeth nightly orgies, and teacheth drunkenness, and carrieth off his neighbours’ wives, a madman and an exile, finally slain by the Titans. If then Dionysus was slain and unable to help himself, nay, further was a madman, a drunkard, and vagabond, how could he be a god?

“Herakles, too, is represented as drunken and mad, as slaying his own children, then consuming with fire and thus dying. How then could a drunkard and slayer of his own children, burnt to death by fire, be a god? Or how can he help others who could not help himself?

“Apollo they represent as an emulous god, holding bow and quiver, and, at times, harp and flute, and prophesying to men for pay. Soothly he is needy: but one that is needy and emulous and a minstrel cannot be a god.

===Chinese===
「阿瑞斯（Ares）被描绘成好战的神，争竞嫉妒，贪图羊群并别样财物。末了，据说他与阿佛洛狄忒行淫，被孩童厄洛斯（Eros）和赫菲斯托斯当场捉住，被他们捆绑了。这样，一个贪财的、好斗的、作囚奴的、奸淫的，怎能是神呢？

「他们又把狄俄尼索斯展示为神：他率领夜间的狂欢纵饮，教人酗酒，掳掠邻舍之妻，是个疯子和流亡者，末了被提坦众神（Titans）所杀。这样，狄俄尼索斯既被杀而无力自救，更是个疯子、酒徒、流浪汉，他怎能是神呢？

「赫拉克勒斯也被描绘成酗酒癫狂的：杀了自己的儿女，最后自投火中焚死。这样，一个酒徒、杀儿女的、被火焚死的人，怎能是神呢？连自己都不能救的，又怎能救别人呢？

「他们把阿波罗描绘成争竞嫉妒的神，拿着弓和箭袋，有时又拿琴和笛，为人占卜而受酬报。他实在是穷乏的：而一个穷乏的、争竞的、卖艺的乐师，不能是神。

===Original===
“Artemis, his sister, they represent as an huntress, with bow and quiver, ranging the mountains alone, with her hounds, in chase of stag or boar. How can such an one, that is an huntress and a ranger with hounds, be a goddess?

“Of Aphrodite, adulteress though she be, they say that she is herself a goddess. Once she had for leman Ares, once Anchises, once Adonis, whose death she lamenteth, seeking her lost lover. They say that she even descended into Hades to ransom Adonis from Persephone. Didst thou, O king, ever see madness greater than this? They represent this weeping and wailing adulteress as a goddess.

“Adonis they show as an hunter god, violently killed by a boar tusk, and unable to help his own distress. How then shall he take thought for mankind, he the adulterer, the hunter who died a violent death?

“All such tales, and many like them, and many wicked tales more shameful still, have the Greeks introduced, O king, concerning their gods; tales, whereof it is unlawful to speak, or even to have them in remembrance. Hence men, taking occasion from their gods, wrought all lawlessness, lasciviousness and ungodliness, polluting earth and air with their horrible deeds.

===Chinese===
「他的姊妹阿尔忒弥斯，被描绘成狩猎的女子，拿着弓和箭袋，带着猎犬，独自游荡山间，追逐牡鹿野猪。这样一个狩猎的、带着犬只奔走山野的，怎能是女神呢？

「至于阿佛洛狄忒，她虽是个淫妇，他们却说她是女神。她一度以阿瑞斯为情夫，一度以安基塞斯（Anchises），又一度以阿多尼斯（Adonis）；阿多尼斯死了，她哀哭他，寻找她失去的恋人。他们又说，她甚至下到阴间，要向珀耳塞福涅（Persephone）赎回阿多尼斯。王啊，你可曾见过比这更大的癫狂么？他们竟把这哭泣哀号的淫妇表为女神。

「他们又把阿多尼斯展示为狩猎的神：他被野猪的獠牙惨暴杀死，自己的危难尚且无力解脱。这奸淫的、横死的猎人，怎会眷念世人呢？

「王啊，关于自己的神明，希腊人编造了这一切故事，还有许多类似的，更有许多比这些还可耻的恶事——这些故事，述说不合律法，连存记在心也不宜。人既从他们的神明取得借口，便行出一切不法、淫荡和不敬虔，用他们可憎的恶行玷污了地和空气。

===Original===
“But the Egyptians, more fatuous and foolish than they, have erred worse than any other nation. They were not satisfied with the idols worshipped by the Chaldeans and Greeks, but further introduced as gods brute beasts of land and water, and herbs and trees, and were defiled in all madness and lasciviousness worse than all people upon earth. From the beginning they worshipped Isis, which had for her brother and husband that Osiris which was slain by his brother Typhon. And for this reason Isis fled with Horus her son to Byblos in Syria, seeking Osiris and bitterly wailing, until Horus was grown up and killed Typhon. Isis then was not able to help her own brother and husband; nor had Osiris, who was slain by Typhon, power to succour himself; nor had Typhon, who killed his brother and was himself destroyed by Horus and Isis, any resource to save himself from death. And yet, although famous for all these misadventures, these be they that were considered gods by the senseless Egyptians.

“The same people, not content therewith, nor with the rest of the idols of the heathen, also introduced brute beasts as gods. Some of them worshipped the sheep, some the goat, and others the calf and the hog; while certain of them worshipped the raven, the kite, the vulture, and the eagle. Others again worshipped the crocodile, and some the cat and dog, the wolf and ape, the dragon and serpent, and others the onion, garlic and thorns, and every other creature. And the poor fools do not perceive, concerning these things, that they have no power at all. Though they see their gods being devoured, burnt and killed by other men, and rotting away, they cannot grasp the fact that they are no gods.

“Great, then, is the error that the Egyptians, the Chaldeans, and the Greeks have erred in introducing such gods as these, and making images thereof, and deifying dumb and senseless idols. I marvel how, when they behold their gods being sawn and chiselled by workmen’s axes, growing old and dissolving through lapse of time, and molten in the pot, they never reflected concerning them that they are no gods. For when these skill not to work their own salvation, how can they take care of mankind? Nay, even the poets and philosophers among the Chaldeans, Greeks and Egyptians, although by their poems and histories they desired to glorify their people’s gods, yet they rather revealed and exposed their shame before all men. If the body of a man, consisting of many parts, loseth not any of its proper members, but, having an unbroken union with all its members, is in harmony with itself, how in the nature of God shall there be such warfare and discord? For if the nature of the gods were one, then ought not one god to persecute, slay or injure another. But if the gods were persecuted by other gods, and slain and plundered and killed with thunder-stones, then is their nature no longer one, but their wills are divided, and are all mischievous, so that not one among them is God. So it is manifest, O king, that all this history of the nature of the gods is error.

“Furthermore, how do the wise and eloquent among the Greeks fail to perceive that lawgivers themselves are judged by their own laws? For if their laws are just, then are their gods assuredly unjust, in that they have offended against law by murders, sorceries, adulteries, thefts and unnatural crimes. But, if they did well in so doing, then are their laws unjust, seeing that they have been framed in condemnation of the gods. But now the laws are good and just, because they encourage good and forbid evil; whereas the deeds of their gods offend against law. Their gods then are offenders against law; and all that introduce such gods as these are worthy of death and are ungodly. If the stories of the gods be myths, then are the gods mere words: but if the stories be natural, then are they that wrought or endured such things no longer gods: if the stories be allegorical, then are the gods myths and nothing else. Therefore it hath been proven, O king, that all these idols, belonging to many gods, are works of error and destruction. So it is not meet to call those gods that are seen, but cannot see: but it is right to worship as God him who is unseen and is the Maker of all mankind.

===Chinese===
「至于埃及人，比他们更昏昧愚拙，其错谬比任何别国都更甚。他们不以迦勒底人和希腊人所拜的偶像为足，更引进陆上水中的牲畜走兽、草本树木为神，在一切癫狂淫荡上，比地上万民更污秽。起初他们敬拜伊西斯（Isis）；她的兄弟兼丈夫，就是被自己兄弟提丰（Typhon）所杀的俄西里斯（Osiris）。为此，伊西斯带着儿子荷鲁斯（Horus）逃到叙利亚（Syria）的比布鲁斯（Byblos），寻找俄西里斯，哀哭甚恸，直到荷鲁斯长大，杀了提丰。这样，伊西斯救不了自己的兄弟兼丈夫；被提丰所杀的俄西里斯，无力救助自己；杀了兄弟、又被荷鲁斯和伊西斯除灭的提丰，也无从救自己脱离死亡。然而，尽管以这一切不幸闻名，这些正是无知的埃及人所视为神的。

「这族人仍不以此为足，也不以外邦其余的偶像为足，还引进牲畜走兽为神。他们中有人敬拜绵羊，有人敬拜山羊，有人敬拜牛犊和猪；又有人敬拜乌鸦、鸢、秃鹰和鹰；另有人敬拜鳄鱼，有人敬拜猫和狗、狼和猿、龙和蛇；还有人敬拜洋葱、大蒜和荆棘，并各样别的受造之物。这些可怜的愚人却不察觉，这些事物毫无权能。他们虽亲眼见自己的神明被别人吞吃、焚烧、杀害、朽烂，仍不能领会它们并不是神。

「这样，埃及人、迦勒底人和希腊人引进这般神明，为之造像，又将哑而无知觉的偶像奉为神明，实在是错谬甚大。我诧异的是：他们眼看自己的神明被工匠的斧锯锯开雕凿，因岁月迁流而老化消解，在锅里熔化，却从不对它们省察——它们并不是神。这些既无能成就自己的救恩，怎能看顾世人呢？不，连迦勒底人、希腊人和埃及人中的诗人和哲学家，虽想借诗歌和史传荣耀本族的神明，反倒把他们神明的羞耻在众人面前揭露彰显了。人的身体由许多部分构成，若不失丧任何固有的肢体，与全体肢体联合无间、和谐一致；那么，在神的本性里，怎会有这样的争战与不和呢？众神的本性若是一，就不该有这一位神逼迫、杀害或伤害另一位神。但众神若被别的神逼迫、杀害、掳掠、被雷石击死，那么他们的本性就不再是一，他们的意志乃是分裂的，并且尽都邪恶，以致他们中间没有一位是神。所以，王啊，这是显明的：这一切关于众神本性的记述，都是错谬。

「再者，希腊人中那些有智慧、有辩才的，怎么竟看不出：立法者自己正被自己的法律所审判呢？他们的法律若是公义的，他们的神明就必是不义的，因他们借凶杀、邪术、奸淫、偷盗并逆性的罪行干犯了法律。他们若干犯得有理，那他们的法律就是不义的，因为那些法律正是为定神明的罪而制定的。但如今法律乃是良善公义的，因为它们劝善禁恶；而他们神明的行为却干犯法律。这样，他们的神明就是犯法的；凡引进这般神明的人，都是该死的，都是不敬虔的。众神的故事若是神话，众神就只是空言罢了；故事若属实情，那么行这些事、受这些事的，就不再是神；故事若是寓意，众神就只是神话，别无其他。所以，王啊，事情已经证明：属乎众多神明的这一切偶像，都是错谬与败亡之工。所以，称那能被看见却不能看见的为神，是不相宜的；敬拜那不能看见、却是全人类之创造主的那位，才是正理。

===Original===
“Come we now, O king, to the Jews, that we may see what they also think concerning God. The Jews are the descendants of Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, and went once to sojourn in Egypt. From thence God brought them out with a mighty hand and stretched out arm by Moses their lawgiver; and with many miracles and signs made he known unto them his power. But, like the rest, these proved ungrateful and unprofitable, and often worshipped images of the heathen, and killed the prophets and righteous men that were sent unto them. Then, when it pleased the Son of God to come on earth, they did shamefully entreat him and deliver him to Pilate the Roman governor, and condemn him to the Cross, regardless of his benefits and the countless miracles that he had worked amongst them. Wherefore by their own lawlessness they perished. For though to this day they worship the One Omnipotent God, yet it is not according unto knowledge; for they deny Christ the Son of God, and are like the heathen, although they seem to approach the truth from which they have estranged themselves. So much for the Jews.

“As for the Christians, they trace their line from the Lord Jesus Christ. He is confessed to be the Son of the most high God, who came down from heaven, by the Holy Ghost, for the salvation of mankind, and was born of a pure Virgin, without seed of man, and without defilement, and took flesh, and appeared among men, that he might recall them from the error of worshipping many gods. When he had accomplished his marvellous dispensation, of his own free will by a mighty dispensation he tasted of death upon the Cross. But after three days he came to life again, and ascended into the heavens, the glory of whose coming thou mayest learn, O king, by the reading of the holy Scripture, which the Christians call the Gospel, shouldst thou meet therewith. This Jesus had twelve disciples, who, after his ascent into the heavens, went out into all the kingdoms of the world, telling of his greatness. Even so one of them visited our coasts, preaching the doctrine of truth; whence they who still serve the righteousness of his preaching are called Christians. And these are they who, above all the nations of the earth, have found the truth: for they acknowledge God the Creator and Maker of all things in the only begotten Son, and in the Holy Ghost, and other God than him they worship none. They have the commandments of the Lord Jesus Christ himself engraven on their hearts, and these they observe, looking for the resurrection of the dead and the life of the world to come. They neither commit adultery nor fornication; nor do they bear false witness, nor covet other men’s goods: they honour father and mother, and love their neighbours: they give right judgement. They do not unto other that which they would not have done unto themselves. They comfort such as wrong them, and make friends of them: they labour to do good to their enemies: they are meek and gentle. They refrain themselves from all unlawful intercourse and all uncleanness. They despise not the widow, and grieve not the orphan. He that hath distributeth liberally to him that hath not. If they see a stranger, they bring him under their roof, and rejoice over him, as it were their own brother: for they call themselves brethren, not after the flesh, but after the spirit. For Christ his sake they are ready to lay down their lives: they keep his commandments faithfully, living righteous and holy lives, as the Lord commanded them, giving him thanks every hour, for meat and drink and every blessing. Verily, then, this is the way of truth which leadeth its wayfarers unto the eternal kingdom promised by Christ in the life to come.

“And that thou mayest know, O king, that I speak nought of myself,10 look thou into the writings of the Christians, and thou shalt find that I speak nothing but the truth. Well, therefore, hath thy son understood it, and rightly hath he been taught to serve the living God, and to be saved for the world to come. Great and marvellous are the things spoken and wrought by the Christians, because they speak not the words of men but the words of God. But all other nations are deceived, and deceive themselves. Walking in darkness they stagger one against another like drunken men. This is the end of my speech spoken unto thee, O king, prompted by the truth that is in my mind. Wherefore let thy foolish wiseacres refrain from babbling idly against the Lord; for it is profitable to you to worship God the Creator, and hearken to his incorruptible sayings, in order that ye may escape judgement and punishment, and be found partakers of deathless life.”

===Chinese===
「王啊，现在让我们来到犹太人，看看他们对神又存什么观念。犹太人是亚伯拉罕、以撒、雅各的后裔，曾一度寄居埃及。神借他们的立法者摩西，用大能的手和伸出来的膀臂，将他们从那里领出来；又借许多神迹奇事，向他们显明他的大能。但他们也和其余的人一样，显为忘恩而无益，屡次敬拜外邦的形像，杀害那奉差遣到他们那里去的先知和义人。及至神之子乐意降临地上，他们竟凌辱他，把他交给罗马巡抚彼拉多（Pilate），定了他十字架的罪，全不顾念他的恩惠，和他先前在他们中间所行的无数神迹。所以他们因自己的不法，自取灭亡了。因为直到今日，他们虽敬拜独一全能的神，却不是按着真知识；他们否认基督是神的儿子，就与外邦人无异，虽然他们似乎接近真理，其实早已与之疏远了。犹太人的事，就说到这里。

「至于基督徒，他们的世系溯自主耶稣基督。他被承认为至高神的儿子，为救世人，借圣灵自天降临，由纯洁的童女所生，没有男人的种子，没有玷污，取了肉身，显现于人间，好叫世人从敬拜多神的错谬中转回。他成就了他奇妙的经纶之后，又凭自己的自由意志、借大能的经纶，在十字架上尝了死味。三天之后，他复活过来，升入诸天；他降临的荣耀，王啊，你若得读基督徒称为福音书的那圣经，便可知晓。这位耶稣有十二位门徒；他升天之后，他们出去往世界列国，传扬他的伟大。他们中间的一位也曾来到我们的地界，传讲真理之道；因此，凡至今仍遵行他所传之义的人，就称为基督徒。正是他们，在地上万国之中寻得了真理：因为他们借独生子并在圣灵里，承认神是万有的创造者和造成者，除他以外，不敬拜别神。他们心中铭刻着主耶稣基督自己的诫命，并且谨守遵行，仰望死人复活，并来世的生命。他们不奸淫，不苟合，不作假见证，不贪恋别人的财物；孝敬父母，爱邻舍，断案秉公。凡他们不愿人怎样待自己的，也决不那样待人。他们安慰亏负他们的人，与他们结为朋友；他们尽力向仇敌行善；他们温柔谦和。他们禁戒一切不法之交和一切污秽。他们不藐视寡妇，不使孤儿忧伤。凡有的，都慷慨分给那没有的。他们若看见客旅，就领他到自己的屋顶下，欢欢喜喜地接待他，如同接待自己的弟兄；因为他们彼此称为弟兄，不是按着血气，乃是按着圣灵。为基督的缘故，他们甘愿舍命；他们忠心守他的诫命，照主的吩咐，过公义圣洁的生活，为着饮食并各样的恩赐，时时向他谢恩。诚然，这就是那真理的道路，引领行路的人进入基督在来世所应许的永远国度。

「王啊，为叫你知道我所说的并非出于自己 10 ，你且查考基督徒的著作，就必见我所说的无非真理。这样看来，你的儿子实在明白得好，他所受的教导也实在正确——教他事奉又真又活的神，为来世得救。基督徒所说所行的事既大且奇，因为他们所说的不是人的言语，乃是神的言语。其余万国尽都受欺，又自欺。他们在黑暗中行走，东倒西歪，彼此碰撞，如同醉酒的人。王啊，这就是我在心中的真理催促之下，对你所说的话的终结。所以，愿你那些愚妄的自作聪明之辈，不要再向主空言呶呶；因为敬拜造物主神、听从他不能朽坏的话语，于你们乃是有益的，好叫你们逃脱审判与刑罚，得显为有分于不朽生命的人。」

## XXVIII / 二十八

===Original===
When Nachor had fully delivered this oration, the king changed countenance for very anger, but his orators and temple-keepers stood speechless, having nothing but a few weak and rotten shreds of argument in reply. But the king’s son rejoiced in spirit and with glad countenance magnified the Lord, who had made a path, where no path was, for them that trusted in him, who by the mouth of a foeman and enemy was establishing the truth; and the leader of error had proved a defender of the right cause.

But the king, although furiously enraged with Nachor, was nevertheless unable to do him any mischief, because of the proclamation already read before all, wherein he urged him to plead without fear in behalf of the Christians. So he himself made answer in many words, and by dark speeches hinted that Nachor should relax his resistance, and be worsted by the argument of the orators. But Nachor the more mightily prevailed, tearing to pieces all their propositions and conclusions and exposing the fallacy of their error. After the debate had been prolonged till well-nigh eventide, the king dismissed the assembly, making as though he would renew the discussion on the morrow.

Then said Ioasaph to the king his father, “As at the beginning, Sir, thou commandedst that the trial should be just, so too crown the end thereof with justice, by doing one or other of these two things. Either allow my teacher to tarry with me tonight, that we may take counsel together as touching those things which we must say unto our adversaries tomorrow: and do thou in turn take thine advisers unto thee, and duly practise yourselves as ye will. Or else deliver thy counsellors to me this night, and take mine to thyself. But if both sides be with thee, mine advocate in tribulation and fear, but thine in joy and refreshment, me thinketh it is not a fair trial, but a tyrannical misuse of power, and a breaking of the covenants.” The king, compelled to yield by the gracefulness of this speech took his wise men and priests to himself, and delivered Nachor to his son, still having hopes of him and thinking fit to keep his agreement.

===Chinese===
纳霍尔全然讲完了这篇演说，王因盛怒而变了脸色；他的众演说家与守庙人却哑口无言地站着，除了几片软弱朽败的论证残片，再无可作回答的。王的儿子却灵里喜乐，面带欢容，尊崇主——那位为倚靠他的人在无路之处开出路径的，那位借仇敌与对头之口坚立真理的；错谬的首领竟成了正义事业的辩护者。

王虽然向纳霍尔大发烈怒，却因着那已当众宣读的谕令——其中他曾劝勉纳霍尔放胆无惧地为基督徒辩护——而无从加害于他。于是王亲自以许多话作答，又用隐晦的言语暗示纳霍尔应当松弛抵挡，被演说家们的论证击败。但纳霍尔却愈加有力地得胜，把他们一切的命题与结论撕得粉碎，揭露出他们错谬的虚妄。辩论延长到将近黄昏，王才解散会众，装作明日要重新开论的样子。

于是约阿萨弗对王他父亲说：「主上，你既在起初吩咐这场审断应当公正，如今也当以公正为它作结，行这两件事中的一件：或是容我的教师今夜与我同住，使我们可以一同商议明天当向我们的对手说什么话；你也照样把你的谋士接到你那里，随你们的意思好好预演。或是今夜把你的谋士交给我，把我的接到你那里。但若两造都在你那里——我的辩护人处在患难惧怕之中，你的却处在喜乐宽舒之中——我看这就不是公正的审断，乃是专横地滥用权柄，是背弃盟约。」王被这番话的得体所折服，只得依从，便把自己的智慧人和祭司接到自己那里，把纳霍尔交给儿子，仍对他存着指望，也认为持守自己的约言是合宜的。


===Original===
The king’s son, therefore, departed unto his own palace, like a conqueror in the Olympic games, and with him went Nachor. When alone, the prince called him and said, “Think not that I am ignorant of thy tale, for I wot, of a surety, that thou art not saintly Barlaam, but Nachor the astrologer; and I marvel how it seemed thee good to act this play, and to think that thou couldst so dull my sight at midday, that I should mistake a wolf for a sheep. But well sung is the proverb, ‘The heart of a fool will conceive folly.’ So this your device and counsel was stale and utterly senseless; but the work that thou hast accomplished is full of wisdom. Wherefore, rejoice, Nachor, and be exceeding glad. I render thee many thanks, that thou hast been today advocate of the truth, and hast not polluted thy lips with foul words and crafty simulation, but hast rather cleansed them from many defilements, and thoroughly proven the error of the gods, as they be wrongly called, and hast established the truth of the Christian faith. I have been zealous to bring thee hither with me for two reasons; that the king might not privily seize and punish thee, because thou spakest not after his heart, and next that I might recompense thee for the favour that thou hast done me today. And what is my recompense for thee? To show thee how to turn from the evil and slippery road which thou hast trodden until now, and to journey along the straight and saving pathway which thou hast avoided, not in ignorance, but by wilful wrongdoing, throwing thyself into depths and precipices of iniquity. Understand then, Nachor, man of understanding as thou art, and be thou zealous to gain Christ only, and the life that is hid with him, and despise this fleeting and corruptible world. Thou shalt not live forever, but, being mortal, shalt depart hence ere long, even as all that have been before thee. And woe betide thee, if, with the heavy load of sin on thy shoulders, thou depart thither where there is righteous judgement and recompense for thy works, and cast it not off, while it is easy to rid thyself thereof!”

Pricked at heart by these words, spake Nachor, “Well said! Sir prince, well said! I do know the true and very God, by whom all things were made, and I wot of the judgement to come, having heard thereof from many texts of the Scriptures. But evil habit and the insolence of the ancient supplanter hath blinded the eyes of my heart, and shed a thick darkness over my reason. But now, at thy word, I will cast away the veil of gloom, and run unto the light of the countenance of the Lord. May be, he will have mercy on me, and will open a door of repentance to his wicked and rebellious servant, even if it seem impossible to me that my sins, which are heavier than the sand, be forgiven; sins, which, wittingly or unwittingly, I have sinned from childhood upwards to this my hoary age.”

When the king’s son heard these words, immediately he arose, and his heart waxed warm, and he began to try to raise Nachor’s courage which was drooping to despair, and to confirm it in the faith of Christ, saying, “Let no doubt about this, Nachor, find place in thy mind. For it is written, God is able of these very stones to raise up children unto Abraham. What meaneth this (as father Barlaam said) except that men beyond hope, stained with all manner of wickedness, can be saved, and become servants of Christ, who, in the exceeding greatness of his love toward mankind, hath opened the gates of heaven to all that turn, barring the way of salvation to none, and receiving with compassion them that repent? Wherefore to all that have entered the vineyard at the first, third, sixth, ninth or eleventh hour there is apportioned equal pay, as saith the holy Gospel: so that even if, until this present time, thou hast waxen old in thy sins, yet if thou draw nigh with a fervent heart, thou shalt gain the same rewards as they who have laboured from their youth upwards.”

===Chinese===
于是王子回到自己的宫里，如同在奥林匹亚竞技中夺得冠冕的得胜者一样，纳霍尔也与偕行。独处的时候，王子唤他来说：「你不要以为我不知道你的底细，因为我确实知道，你不是圣洁的巴拉阿姆，乃是占星家纳霍尔。我希奇你怎么看为合宜，要上演这一出戏，又以为你能这样叫我白昼的眼目昏蒙，把狼当作羊。然而俗谚唱得好：『愚昧人的心必怀愚妄。』所以你们这计谋与策划固然陈腐而全然无识；但你所成就的工作却满了智慧。所以，纳霍尔啊，你要喜乐，并且大大喜乐。我多多感谢你，因你今日作了真理的辩护人，没有用污秽的言语和诡诈的假冒玷污你的嘴唇，反倒把它们从许多玷污中洁净了，又彻底证明了那些被妄称为神者的错谬，坚立了基督信仰的真理。我热心带你到这里来与我同在，有两个缘故：一是免得王因你所说的话不合他的心意，而暗中捉拿你、刑罚你；二是要我报答你今日向我所施的恩惠。我拿什么报答你呢？就是指示你，当怎样转离你直到如今所行的又恶又滑的路，走上那正直而施救恩的路——你避开那路，不是出于无知，乃是出于故意的恶行，把自己抛进了不义的深渊与悬崖。所以，纳霍尔啊，你既是通达人，就当明白这事，当热心只求赢得基督，并那与他一同隐藏的生命，轻看这飞逝而必朽坏的世界。你不会永远活着；你既是必死的，不久就要离开这里，正如在你以先的一切人一样。你若负着罪的重担在肩头，往那有公义审判、照各人行为施报的地方去，却不趁着容易摆脱的时候把它甩脱——你就有祸了！」

纳霍尔听见这些话，觉得扎心，就说：「说得好，王子殿下，说得好！我实在认识那又真又实的神，万物都是借他造的；我也知道将来的审判，因为曾从圣经的许多经文听过这事。但恶习和那古时欺哄者的狂妄，使我心中的眼睛瞎了，在我的理性上罩下了厚厚的黑暗。但如今，因着你的话，我要丢弃幽暗的帕子，奔向主面上的光。或许他会怜悯我，向他这邪恶悖逆的仆人开一扇悔改的门；尽管在我看来，我那比海沙更重的罪能得着赦免，似乎是不能的事——这些罪，是我从幼年直到这皓首之年，或有意、或无意地犯下的。」

王子听见这些话，立刻起身，心就火热起来，便设法扶起纳霍尔那颓丧到绝望的勇气，使它在基督的信仰上得以坚固，说：「纳霍尔啊，关于这事，你不要容任何疑惑在你心里寻得立足之地。因为经上记着说：『神能从这些石头中给亚伯拉罕兴起子孙来。』这话是什么意思呢（正如属灵之父巴拉阿姆所说）？岂不是说，那些本已无望、染了各样恶行的人也能得救，成为基督的仆人么？他向世人的爱是何等浩大，已为一切转回的人敞开天门，不向任何人封锁救恩之路，且怀着怜悯接待悔改的人。所以，凡在清早、巳初、午正、申初或酉初进入葡萄园的，所分得的工钱都是一样的，如圣福音所说：这样，即使你直到今日都在罪中老去，只要存着火热的心近前来，就必得着与那些从幼年劳苦直到如今的人同样的赏赐。」


===Original===
With these and many other words did that saintly youth speak of repentance to that aged sinner Nachor, promising him that Christ was merciful, and pledging him forgiveness, and satisfying him that the good God is alway ready to receive the penitent, and with these words, as it were with ointments, did he mollify that ailing soul and give it perfect health. Nachor at once said unto him, “O prince, more noble in soul even than in outward show, well instructed in these marvellous mysteries, mayst thou continue in thy good confession until the end, and may neither time nor tide ever pluck it out of thine heart! For myself, I will depart straightway in search of my salvation, and will by penance pacify that God whom I have angered: for, except thou will it, I shall see the king’s face no more.” Then was the prince exceeding glad, and joyfully heard his saying. And he embraced and kissed him affectionately; and, when he had prayed earnestly to God, he sent him forth from the palace.

So Nachor stepped forth with a contrite heart, and went bounding into the depths of the desert, like as doth an hart, and came to a den belonging to a monk that had attained to the dignity of the priesthood, and was hiding there for fear of the pressing danger. With a right warm heart knelt Nachor down before him, and washed his feet with his tears, like the harlot of old, and craved holy Baptism. The priest, full of heavenly grace, was passing glad, and did at once begin to instruct him, as the custom is, and after many days, perfected him with baptism in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost. And Nachor abode with him, always repentant of his sins, and blessing that God who never willeth that any should perish, but receiveth all that turn again unto him, and lovingly accepteth the penitent.

Now on the morrow when the king heard what had befallen Nachor, he despaired of the hopes that he once had in him: and, seeing those wise and foolish orators of his mightily discomfited, he was at his wits’ end. Them he visited with terrible outrage and dishonour, scourging some severely with whips of oxhide, besmearing their eyes with soot, and casting them away from his presence. He himself began to condemn the impotence of the gods falsely so called, although as yet he refused to look fully at the light of Christ, for the dense cloud of darkness, that enveloped him, still bound the eyes of his heart. Howbeit he no longer honoured his temple-keepers, nor would he keep feasts, nor make drink offerings to his idols, but his mind was tossed between two opinions. On the one hand, he poured scorn on the impotence of his gods; on the other, he dreaded the strictness of the profession of the Gospel, and was hardly to be torn from his evil ways, being completely in slavery to the pleasures of the body, and like a captive drawn towards sinful lusts, and being drunken, as saith Esay, but not with wine, and led as it were with the bridle of evil habit.

===Chinese===
那圣洁的少年就用这些话和许多别的话，向那年老的罪人纳霍尔讲论悔改，向他应许基督是满有怜悯的，向他担保必得赦免，又使他满心确信那良善的神随时都预备接纳悔改的人；他用这些话仿佛用膏油，软化了那患病的心灵，使它得着完全的健康。纳霍尔立刻对他说：「王子啊，你灵魂的高贵更胜于你外表的荣美，你又在这些奇妙的奥迹上受了好教训；愿你持守你美好的认信直到末了，愿时移世易都永不能把它从你心中夺去！至于我自己，我要立刻动身去寻求我的救恩，要以悔罪的工夫平息那被我触怒的神：因为除非你愿意，我必不再见王的面。」王子就大大欢喜，快乐地听了他这话，又深情地拥抱他、与他亲嘴，并且恳切地向神祷告之后，就打发他出宫去了。

于是纳霍尔怀着痛悔的心出来，像鹿一样奔跃，进入旷野的深处，到了一个属于修士的洞窟；那修士已登上司祭的尊位，因迫近的危险躲藏在那里。纳霍尔怀着十分火热的心跪在他面前，像古时的妓女一样，用眼泪洗他的脚，恳求圣洗礼。那司祭满有天上的恩典，极其欢喜，便立即照着惯例开始教导他；过了许多日子，就奉父、子、圣灵的名，以洗礼使他得以成全。纳霍尔便与他同住，常常为自己的罪悔改，又称颂那神——他不愿有一人沉沦，乃接纳一切归向他的人，以慈爱悦纳悔改者。

次日，王听见临到纳霍尔的事，就对自己素来在他身上的指望绝望了；又看见他那些又智慧又愚拙的演说家惨遭击溃，他就智慧无法可施。他便以可怕的暴虐和羞辱加在他们身上：用牛皮鞭狠狠鞭打其中一些人，把煤烟涂抹在他们眼睛上，又将他们从自己面前驱逐出去。他自己也开始谴责那些被妄称为神者的无能；只是他还不肯完全仰望基督的光，因为那笼罩着他的浓黑云雾，仍旧捆绑着他心中的眼睛。然而他不再尊荣他的守庙人，也不再设摆筵席，不再向他的偶像献奠祭；他的心思却在两种意见之间被抛来抛去。一方面，他讥诮他那些神的无能；另一方面，他又惧怕福音宣认的严格，很难从他邪恶的路上被拉回来，因为他完全作了肉体逸乐的奴仆，像俘虏一样被牵向罪恶的情欲，又如以赛亚所说，醉酒却非因酒，仿佛被恶习的嚼环牵引着走。


===Original===
While the king was thus wrestling with two opinions, his noble and truly royal-hearted son dwelt at peace in his palace, proving to all men by his deeds the nobility, order and steadfastness of his nature. Theatres, horse-races, riding to hounds, and all the vain pleasures of youth, the baits that take foolish souls, were reckoned by him as nothing worth. But he hung wholly on the commands of Christ for whom he yearned, his heart being wounded with love divine. For him he longed, who alone is to be longed for, who is all sweetness and desire and aspiration insatiable.

Now, when he came to think upon his teacher Barlaam, and as in a mirror saw his life, his soul was enchanted with love, and he much occupied himself a-thinking how he might see him; and ever carrying his sayings in his heart, he was like the tree in the Psalms planted by the river side, unceasingly watered, and bringing forth unto the Lord his fruits in due season. Many were the souls that he delivered from the snares of the devil, and brought safely unto Christ; for many resorted unto him, and profited by his wholesome words. And not a few left the way of error, and ran toward the word of salvation; while others bade a long farewell to the concerns of the world, and came to the wrestling-school of the monastic life. He himself spent his time in prayers and fastings, and would often offer up this prayer, “O Lord, my Lord and King, in whom I have trusted, to whom I have fled and been delivered from my error, render thou due recompense to Barlaam thy servant, because when I was in error he pointed thee to me, who art the way of truth and life. Forbid me not to behold once more that angel in bodily shape, of whom the world is not worthy, but grant me in his company to finish the residue of my life, that, treading in the footsteps of his conversation, I may be well-pleasing to thee my God and Lord.”

===Chinese===
王这样在两种意见之间挣扎的时候，他那高贵而真具王者之心的儿子，却安然住在自己宫里，以自己的行为向众人显明他性情的尊贵、端方与坚定不移。戏园、赛马、纵犬行猎，和少年人一切虚妄的娱乐——那些捕捉愚妄灵魂的饵——在他都被算为不足介意。他却全然系于基督的诫命，切慕着基督，他的心为神圣之爱所伤。他所切慕的，是那位独一配受切慕的主——他就是全然的甘甜，就是愿望，就是那永难餍足的渴慕。

后来，当他思念起他的教师巴拉阿姆，如同在镜中观看他的生平，他的灵就为爱所陶醉，常常思量怎样才能见他一面；又将他的言语常存在心里，就像诗篇中那栽在溪水旁的树，不住地蒙浇灌，按时候向主结出自己的果子。他救拔了许多灵魂脱离魔鬼的网罗，平平安安地领他们归向基督；因为有许多人到他那里去，从他有益的言语中得益处。又有不少人离弃了错谬之路，奔向救恩之道；还有些人与属世的挂虑长久话别，来到修道生活的角力场。他自己则在祷告和禁食中度过光阴，又常常献上这样的祷告：「主啊，我的主、我的王，我所倚靠的，我所投奔、并蒙拯救脱离错谬的，求你将当得的报赏酬报你的仆人巴拉阿姆；因为我在错谬之中的时候，是他把你——真理与生命之道——指示了我。求你不要禁止我再看见那位具血肉之形的天使——本是世界不配有的人；求你恩准我在他的陪伴下度尽余下的年日，使我踏上他行谊的脚踪，得蒙你——我的神、我的主——的喜悦。」

## XXIX / 二十九

===Original===
Now about the same time there was in that city a public assembly in honour of the false gods, and the king must needs be present at the feast, and grace it with lavish sacrifices. But the temple-keepers, seeing that he was careless and lukewarm with regard to their worship, feared that he might neglect to be present in their temple, and that they might lose the royal largess, and the rest of their revenues. So they arose, and withdrew to a cavern situate in the depth of the desert, where dwelt a man who busied himself with magical arts, and was a fervent champion of the error of idolatry: Theudas was his name. Him the king honoured exceedingly, and counted him his friend and teacher, because, he said, it was by the guidance of his prophecies that his kingdom ever prospered. So these idol-priests, that were no priests, came to him, and appealed to him for help, and made known to him the evil opinion of their gods which was growing on their king, and all that the king’s son had done, and all the eloquent discourse that Nachor had held against them. And they said, “Except thou come thyself to our succour, gone is all hope! and lost is all the reverence of the gods. Thou only art left to be our comfort in this misfortune, and upon thee we fix our hopes.”

So forth marched Theudas, in company with his Satanic host; and he armed himself against the truth, invoking many of his evil spirits, who knew how to lend ready aid for evil ends, and whom he alway used for his ministers; and with these allies he came to the king.

When his arrival had been announced to the king, and he had entered in, with a palm-staff in his hand and a sheepskin girt about his loins, the king arose from his throne, and met and welcomed him; and, fetching a seat, he made him to sit down beside him. Then spake Theudas unto the king, “O king, live forever under the shelter of the favour of the most puissant gods! I have heard that thou hast foughten a mighty fight with the Galileans, and hast been crowned with right glorious diadems of victory. Wherefore I am come, that we may celebrate together a feast of thanksgiving, and sacrifice to the immortal gods young men in the bloom of youth and well-favoured damsels, and eke offer them an hecatomb of bullocks and herds of beasts, that we may have them from henceforth for our allies invincible, making plain our path of life before us.”

===Chinese===
约在那时候，那城里正有一次尊崇假神的公共大会，王理当出席这节筵，并以丰盛的祭祀为之增辉。但众守庙人见王对他们的敬拜疏忽怠慢、不冷不热，就恐怕他或不去他们庙中赴会，他们便要失去王的赏赐与其余的进项。于是他们起身，退往沙漠深处的一座洞穴；那里住着一个人，专务巫幻之术，是偶像崇拜错谬的一名热烈斗士：他的名字叫特乌达斯（Theudas）。王极其尊荣他，以他为友为师，因为王说，他的王国历久昌盛，正是凭他预言的引领。于是这些本非祭司的偶像祭司来到他那里，向他求援，把王对他们诸神日渐滋长的恶评、王儿子所行的一切、并纳霍尔（Nachor）向他们所发的一切雄辩言辞，都一一禀明。他们说：「你若不亲自来救助我们，一切指望就都完了！诸神所受的尊崇也尽都丧失。惟独你留下，作我们这场不幸中的安慰；我们把盼望全然寄托在你身上。」

于是特乌达斯偕同他那撒但的军众开步前进；他武装自己抵挡真理，召请了他的许多恶灵——这些灵惯会为邪恶的目的随时效力，他素常用它们作臣仆——便带着这些盟友来到王那里。

他的到来既已奏报于王，他就进前来，手中拿着棕榈杖，腰间束着羊皮。王从宝座起身，迎接他、欢迎他，又取过一张座席，请他坐在自己身旁。于是特乌达斯对王说：「王啊，愿你在至有权能的诸神恩宠之荫庇下万岁！我听说你已与加利利人大大争战，并戴上了极荣耀的得胜冠冕。为此我来了，要叫我们一同庆祝感恩的筵席，把青春方盛的少年和容貌秀美的少女献给不朽的诸神为祭，再向他们献上公牛的百牛大祭与成群的牲畜，好叫我们从此得着他们作无敌的盟友，在我们面前修平人生的道路。」

===Original===
Hereto the king made answer, “We have not conquered, aged sir, we have not conquered: nay, rather have we been defeated in open fight. They that were for us turned suddenly against us. They found our host a wild, half-drunken, feeble folk, and utterly overthrew it. But now, if there be with thee any power and strength to help our fallen religion and set it up again, declare it.”

Theudas replied in this wise, “Dread not, O king, the oppositions and vain babblings of the Galileans: for of what worth against reasonable and sensible men are the arguments that they use? These methinks shall be more easily overthrown than a leaf shaken with the wind. They shall not endure to face me, far less join argument, or come to propositions and oppositions with me. But, in order that the coming contest and all our wishes may prosper, and that our matters may run smoothly with the stream, adorn thou with thy presence this public festival, and gird on for thy strong sword the favour of the gods, and well befall thee!”

When the mighty in wickedness had thus boasted himself and thought of mischief all the day long (let David bear his part in our chorus), and when, as saith Esay,11 he had given his neighbour a drink of turbid dregs, by the help of the evil spirits his comrades he made the king utterly to forget the thoughts that inclined him to salvation, and caused him again to cleave to his wonted ways. Then the king despatched letters hither and thither, that all men should gather together to this loathsome assembly. Then mightest thou have seen multitudes streaming in, and bringing with them sheep and oxen and divers kinds of beasts.

===Chinese===
王回答他说：「老丈，我们没有得胜，我们没有得胜；反倒是在公开的交锋中败北了。那本在我们一边的人忽然倒戈攻我。他们见我们这一军原是粗野、半醉、软弱之众，就把它全然倾覆。但如今，你里面若有什么能力力量，能扶助我们这倾倒的宗教，将它重新立起，就请明说。」

特乌达斯这样回答：「王啊，不要惧怕加利利人的抗辩与虚妄的空谈：因为他们所用的论证，在明理而有识的人面前，还有什么分量呢？我想，这些人必比风里摇动的一片叶子更容易被推翻。他们必站立不住、不敢当我的面，更不必说与我辩驳，或与我立论对垒了。但为叫将临的较量和我们一切的愿望尽都顺遂，我们的事如顺流而行，还请你亲临这公共节庆，为之增辉，又束上诸神的恩宠作你有力的剑——愿你亨通！」

这恶中的强者既如此自夸，终日图谋奸恶（愿大卫也在我们的诗班中同唱他的一份），又如以赛亚（Esay）11 所说，使邻舍喝了浑浊的残滓，就借着他同伙众恶灵之力，使王全然忘记那引他倾向救恩的意念，又叫他再黏附素习的旧路。于是王把文书发往四方，叫众人都聚集到这可憎的大会来。那时你会看见成群的百姓川流涌入，带着羊、牛和各样的牲畜同来。

===Original===
So when all were assembled, the king arose, with that deceiver Theudas, and proceeded to the temple, bringing one hundred and twenty bullocks and many animals for sacrifice. And they celebrated their accursed feast till the city resounded with the cry of the brute beasts and the very air was polluted with the reek of sacrifice. This done, when the spirits of wickedness had greatly vaunted them over Theudas’ victory, and when the temple-keepers had rendered him thanks, the king went up again unto his palace, and said milo Theudas, “Behold now, as thou badest us, we have spared no pains over the splendour of this gathering and the lavishness of the sacrifice. Now, therefore, it is time for thee to fulfil thy promises, and to deliver from the error of the Christians my son that hath rebelled against our religion, and to reconcile him to our gracious gods. For, though I have left no device and deed untried, yet have I found no remedy for the mischief, but I perceive that his will is stronger than all. When I have dealt gently and kindly with him, I have found that he payeth me no regard whatsoever. When I have treated him harshly and severely, I have seen him driven the quicker to desperation. To thy wisdom for the future I leave the care of this calamity that hath befallen me. If then I be delivered from this trouble by thy means, and once more behold my son worshipping my gods with me, and enjoying the gratification of this life of pleasure, and this royal estate, I will set up unto thee a golden statue, and make thee to receive divine honours from all men for all time to come.”

Hereupon Theudas, bowing an attentive ear to the evil one, and learning from him the secret of his evil and deadly counsel, became himself the devil’s tongue and mouthpiece, and spake unto the king, “If thou wilt get the better of thy son, and make his opposition vain, I have discovered a plan, which he shall in no wise be able to resist, but his hard and obdurate mind shall melt quicker than wax before the hottest fire.” The king, seeing this foolish fellow swelling with empty pride, immediately grew merry and joyful, hoping that the unbridled and boastful tongue would get the mastery of that divinely instructed and philosophic soul. “And what is the plan?” he asked. Then began Theudas to weave his web. He made his villainy sharp as any razor and did cunningly prepare his drugs. Now behold this malicious device and suggestion of the evil one. “Remove, O king,” said he, “all thy son’s waiting men and servants far from him, and order that comely damsels, of exceeding beauty, and bedizened to be the more winsome, be continually with him and minister to him, and be his companions day and night. For myself, I will send him one of the spirits told off for such duties, and I will thus kindle all the more fiercely the coals of sensual desire. After that he hath once only had intercourse with but one of these women, if all go not as thou wilt, then disdain me forever, as unprofitable, and worthy not of honour but of dire punishment. For there is nothing like the sight of women to allure and enchant the minds of men. Listen to a story that beareth witness to my word.”

===Chinese===
众人既都聚齐，王就同那迷惑人的特乌达斯起来，前往庙宇，带着一百二十头公牛和许多献祭的牲畜。他们庆祝那可咒诅的节筵，直到全城回荡着牲畜的嘶鸣，连空气也被祭牲的腥烟所玷污。这事既毕，邪恶的众灵为特乌达斯的得胜大肆夸耀，守庙人也向他道谢；王便重新上到宫中，对特乌达斯说：「看哪，正如你所吩咐的，我们在这聚会的盛美与祭礼的丰盛上，已不遗余力。如今正是时候，你要履行你的应许，把我那背叛我们宗教的儿子从基督徒的错谬里救出来，使他与我们恩慈的诸神复和。因为一切计谋与手段，我虽无不试过，却寻不着医治这祸患之方，只看出他的意志强过一切。我以温良慈爱待他，见他竟全然不把我放在眼里；我以严苛厉待他，见他反倒更快被逼入绝境。此后，我将这临到我的灾祸，交托你的智慧料理。我若借着你脱离这困扰，再一次得见我儿子同我一道敬拜我的诸神，享受这宴乐人生的快慰与这王家的尊位，我就为你立一座金像，使你世世代代受众人神明般的敬奉。」

于是特乌达斯侧耳细听那恶者，从他学得了他那恶毒致命之计谋的奥秘，自己便成了魔鬼的舌头与喉舌，对王说：「你若要胜过你的儿子，使他的抗拒归于无效，我已寻得一个计策，是他断断抵挡不住的；他那刚硬顽梗的心，必比蜡在烈火面前熔化得更快。」王见这愚妄之辈鼓胀着虚空的骄傲，立时欢畅喜乐起来，指望那无羁而夸大的口舌，能制胜那受神教导、富于哲思的灵魂。「这计策是什么呢？」他问。于是特乌达斯开始编织他的网罗，把他的恶毒磨得快如剃刀，又狡狯地调制药剂。且看这恶者恶毒的计谋与挑唆吧。「王啊，」他说，「把你儿子一切的侍从和仆役，都从他身边远远撤去；吩咐那容色绝美、又妆扮得愈加动人的少女，常与他同在、伺候他，昼夜作他的陪伴。至于我，我要差一个派定司此职分的灵到他那里，就越发猛烈地扇起情欲的炭火。只要他与这些女子中的一位仅仅同房一次，此后若不凡事照你的心意成就，你就永远藐视我，看我毫无用处，不配受尊荣，反当受重刑。因为没有什么像看见妇人那样，能引诱并迷醉男人的心。且听一个故事，它可为我的话作见证。」

## XXX / 三十

===Original===
“A certain king was grieved and exceeding sad at heart, because that he had no male issue, deeming this no small misfortune. While he was in this condition, there was born to him a son, and the king’s soul was filled with joy thereat. Then they that were learned amongst his physicians told him that, if for the first twelve years the boy saw the sun or fire, he should entirely lose his sight, for this was proved by the condition of his eyes. Hearing this, the king, they say, caused a little house, full of dark chambers, to be hewn out of the rock, and therein enclosed his child together with the men that nursed him, and, until the twelve years were past, never suffered him to see the least ray of light. After the fulfilment of the twelve years, the king brought forth from his little house his son that had never seen a single object, and ordered his waiting men to show the boy everything after his kind; men in one place, women in another; elsewhere gold and silver; in another place, pearls and precious stones, fine and ornamental vestments, splendid chariots with horses from the royal stables, with golden bridles and purple caparisons, mounted by armed soldiers; also droves of oxen and flocks of sheep. In brief, row after row, they showed the boy everything. Now, as he asked what each of these was called, the king’s esquires and guards made known unto him each by name: but, when he desired to learn what women were called, the king’s spearman, they say, wittily replied that they were called, ‘Devils that deceive men.’ But the boy’s heart was smitten with the love of these above all the rest. So, when they had gone round everywhere and brought him again unto the king, the king asked, which of all these sights had pleased him most. ‘What,’ answered the boy, ‘but the Devils that deceive men? Nothing that I have seen today hath fired my heart with such love as these.’ The king was astonished at the saying of the boy, to think how masterful a thing the love of women is. Therefore think not to subdue thy son in any other way than this.”

The king heard this tale gladly; and there were brought before him some chosen damsels, young and exceeding beautiful. These he bedizened with dazzling ornaments and trained in all winsome ways: and then he turned out of the palace all his son’s squires and serving men, and set these women in their stead. These flocked around the prince, embraced him, and provoked him to filthy wantonness, by their walk and talk inviting him to dalliance. Besides these, he had no man at whom to look, or with whom to converse or break his fast, for these damsels were his all. Thus did the king. But Theudas went home to his evil den, and, dipping into his books that had virtue to work such magic, he called up one of his wicked spirits and sent him forth, for to battle with the soldier of the army of Christ. But the wretch little knew what laughter he should create against himself, and to what shame he should be put, with the whole devilish troop under him. So the evil spirit, taking to him other spirits more wicked than himself, entered the bedchamber of this noble youth, and attacked him by kindling right furiously the furnace of his flesh. The evil one plied the bellows from within, while the damsels, fair of face, but uncomely of soul, supplied the evil fuel from without.

===Chinese===
「从前有一位王，因为膝下没有儿子，心中忧伤、极其愁烦，认为这不是小小的灾祸。他正在这光景中的时候，得了一个儿子，王的心就因此满有喜乐。他众御医中有学问的人告诉他说，这孩子起初的十二年若看见太阳或火，就必完全失明，因为他眼睛的状况已经证明了这事。据说王听见这话，就在磐石中凿出一所满是幽暗内室的小屋，把孩子连同乳养他的人一并关在里面，十二年未满，总不容他看见最小的一线光。十二年既满，王把他那从未见过一件事物的儿子从小屋里领出来，吩咐侍从把各样东西按其类别指给少年看：男人在一处，女人在另一处；这里是金子银子；那里是珍珠宝石、精美华丽的衣袍，还有配上御厩骏马的华美车驾，带着金的辔头、紫的鞍帔，上面坐着持械的兵士；又有成群的牛和成群的羊。简而言之，他们把一切东西一排又一排地指给少年看。他问每一样叫什么名字，王的侍从和卫士就逐一告知；但当他想知道那些女人叫什么的时候，据说王的执戟卫士便妙语作答，说她们叫做『迷惑人的魔鬼』。不料这少年的心恋慕这些，胜过其余一切。及至他们各处走遍，把他再带到王面前，王问这一切景象中什么最使他喜悦。少年回答说：『还能是什么呢，不就是那迷惑人的魔鬼么？今天我所看见的，没有一样像她们这样，用这样的爱火点燃了我的心。』王听了少年的话，大为诧异，想到女人的爱情是何等强有力的一件事。所以，你不要想用别的方法制伏你的儿子，惟有这一途。」

王欢喜地听了这故事；有人把一些选出来的少女带到他面前，年轻而极其美丽。王给她们穿戴耀目的妆饰，教以种种媚态：随后把王子宫里一切侍从和仆役都赶出去，叫这些女子顶替其位。她们围绕王子，与他亲昵，挑动他行污秽的邪荡，行走言谈之间引诱他戏谑调情。在这些人以外，他再没有一个男子可以注目、可以交谈、可以一同用膳，因为这些少女就是他的一切。王所行的就是如此。特乌达斯却回他那邪恶的巢穴去，翻开那些能行这般法术的书卷，召起一个恶灵来，差他出去，与基督军队的兵士交战。但这恶徒全然不知，将有怎样的嗤笑落到自己身上，他与属下整个鬼魔的队伍将蒙受怎样的羞辱。于是那恶灵带上比自己更恶的别的灵，进入这高贵少年的寝宫，猛烈煽旺他肉体的炉火，以此攻击他。恶者在里面拉动风箱，众少女——面容秀美、灵魂却鄙陋——在外面供应邪恶的燃料。

===Original===
But Ioasaph’s pure soul was disturbed to feel the touch of evil, and to see the warlike host of strange thoughts that was charging down upon him. And he sought to find deliverance from this great mischief, and to present himself pure unto Christ, and not defile in the mire of sinful lust that holy apparel, wherein the grace of holy Baptism had clothed him. Immediately he set love against love, the divine against the lascivious; and he called to remembrance the beauty and unspeakable glory of Christ, the immortal bridegroom of virgin souls, and of that bride chamber and marriage, from whence they that have stained their wedding-garment are piteously cast out, bound hand and foot, into outer darkness. When he had thought thereon, and shed bitter tears, he smote upon his breast, driving out evil thoughts, as good-for-nothing drones from the hive. When he rose, and spread out his hands unto heaven, with fervent tears and groans calling upon God to help him, and he said, “Lord Almighty, who alone art powerful and merciful, the hope of the hopeless, and the help of the helpless, remember me thine unprofitable servant at this hour, and look upon me with a gracious countenance, and deliver my soul from the sword of the devil, and my darling from the paw of the dog: suffer me not to fall into the hands of mine enemies, and let not them that hate me triumph over me. Leave me not to be destroyed in iniquities, and to dishonour my body which I swore to present unto thee chaste. For for thee I yearn; thee I worship, the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Ghost, now and forevermore, and world without end.” When he had added the Amen, he felt heavenly comfort stealing over him from above, and the evil thoughts withdrew, and he continued in prayer until early morn. Being ware of the devices of the crafty foe, he began more and more to afflict his body by abstinence from meat and drink, and by other severities, standing in prayer all the night long, and reminding himself of his covenants made with God, and picturing in his mind the glory of the righteous yonder, and recounting to himself the full terrors of the Gehenna wherewith the wicked are threatened; all this, that the enemy might not find his soul lying fallow and untilled, and thus easily sow therein the seeds of evil thoughts, and befoul the cleanness of his mind. So, when the enemy was in great straits on every side, and altogether in despair of taking this noble youth, like a cunning knave, he proceeded to another more subtle device, he that is forever wicked, and never stinteth to contrive mischief and hurt. For he made furious endeavour to carry out the orders that Theudas had given him, and once more prepared his drugs, and on this wise.

The devil entered into the heart of one of the young damsels. Of all she was the most seemly, a king’s daughter, carried away captive from her own country, given to king Abenner as a great prize, and sent by him, being of ripe beauty, to his own son, for to cause him to slip or to trip. Of her the deceiver took possession, and whispered in her ear suggestions that plainly showed the wisdom and understanding of her mind; for the evil one easily pursueth all devices that make for wickedness. Then the evil spirit attacked the king’s son on the right hand, and gave him a potion to make him love the maiden, by reason﻿—so he pretended﻿—of her prudence and discretion and of her nobility and royal blood that yet had not saved her from banishment and loss of glory. Moreover the devil secretly sowed in Ioasaph’s heart thoughts that he might recover her from idolatry, and make her a Christian.

===Chinese===
但约阿萨弗纯洁的灵魂既感到恶的触摸，又看见异样的思想如军旅向他猛冲而下，就惊扰不安。他寻求解脱这大祸，要使自己贞洁地献给基督，不愿在罪欲的淤泥中玷污那件圣衣——圣洗礼的恩典曾给他穿上它。他立时以爱抵挡爱，以属天之爱抵挡淫欲之爱；他想起基督——童女之魂那不死的新郎——的美，与那说不出的荣耀，又想起那婚房与婚筵：凡玷污了自己礼服的，都要可悲地被捆起手脚，丢在外边的黑暗里。他这样思想，流下苦泪，捶着胸膛，把恶念像蜂房里无用的雄蜂一般赶出去。他起来，向天张开双手，怀着热泪与呻吟呼求神帮助他，说：「全能的主啊，惟独你有权能、有怜悯，你是无望之人的盼望、无助之人的帮助；求你在这时刻记念你这无用的仆人，以恩慈的容面垂顾我，救我的灵魂脱离魔鬼的刀剑，救我的生命脱离犬类的爪；不容我落在仇敌手中，不容恨我的人向我夸胜。不要任凭我毁灭在罪孽之中，玷污我曾起誓要贞洁献上给你的身体。因为我所切慕的是你，我所敬拜的是你——父、子、圣灵，从今直到永远，世世无尽。」他说了阿们，就觉得有天上的安慰从上面悄然覆庇他，恶念便退去了，他继续祷告直到清晨。他既识破那诡诈仇敌的种种计谋，就愈发借着禁戒饮食并别的严厉苦行刻苦己身，整夜站着祷告，提醒自己与神所立的约，心中描想彼岸义人的荣耀，向自己数算地狱的全然可怖——就是恶人所受的威吓；这一切，都是叫仇敌找不到他的灵魂闲荒未耕，轻易把恶念的种子撒在其中，污损他心思的洁净。仇敌既四面受窘，全然绝望于掳获这高贵的少年，就像一个奸猾的恶棍，转而行另一个更诡诈的计谋——他这永远是恶的、从不吝于谋算祸害损伤的。因为他猛烈地竭力要成全特乌达斯所给他的命令，再次调制药剂；情形如下。

魔鬼进了众少女中一个女子的心。她是她们全体中最端丽的，原是一位王的女儿，从本国被掳来，当作极重的战利品献给阿本内尔王；王见她美色已丰，便把她送到自己儿子那里，要叫他滑跌绊仆。那迷惑人的占据了这女子，在她耳边低声献上种种心思，那些话显然显出她心意的聪明识见；因为凡趋于邪恶的计谋，恶者行来都毫不费力。随后恶灵又从右边攻击王之子，给他一剂药，叫他爱上这少女——据他托称——是因她的谨慎贤明，并她那高贵的王室血统，只是这血统并未救她脱离放逐与失荣。魔鬼又暗中在约阿萨弗心里撒下意念，说他或许能把她从偶像崇拜中挽回，使她成为基督徒。

===Original===
But these were all stratagems of the wily serpent. For the king’s son, being in this frame of mind, could see in himself no unclean thought or passionate affection for the damsel, but only sympathy and pity for her misfortune, and the ruin of her soul, and knew not that this matter was a device of the devil; for verily he is darkness, and feigneth to be light. So he began to commune with the damsel, and talk with her over the oracles of the knowledge of God, and said, “Lady, be thou acquainted with the ever-living God, and perish not in the error of these idols; but know thy Lord, and the Maker of all this world, and thou shalt be happy, the bride of the immortal bridegroom.” While he exhorted her with many suchlike words, immediately the evil spirit whispered to the girl that she should spread under his feet the nets of deceit to drag his blessed soul into the pit of lust, as he once did to our first parent by means of Eve, thus miserably banishing him, alas! from Paradise and God, and making him to become subject to death in lieu of bliss and everlasting life.

When the damsel heard Ioasaph’s words fulfilled with all wisdom, being without understanding, she understood them not, but made answer thus, becoming the tongue and mouthpiece of the evil one: “If, sir, thou takest thought for my salvation, and desirest to bring me to thy God, and to save my poor soul, do thou also thyself grant me one request, and straightway I will bid goodbye to my fathers’ gods, and join thy God, serving him until my last breath; and thou shalt receive recompense for my salvation, and for my turning to God-ward.”

“Lady, and what is thy request?” said he. But she, setting her whole self, figure, look and voice in a fashion to charm him, answered, “Be thou joined with me in the bonds of wedlock, and I will joyfully follow out thy behests.”

“In vain, O Lady,” said he, “hast thou made this hard request. For though I earnestly care for thy salvation, and long to heave thee from the depth of perdition, yet to pollute my body through unclean union is grievous for me, and utterly impossible.”

===Chinese===
然而这些全是那狡诈之蛇的诡计。因为王之子存着这样的心，在自己里面看不出对这少女有什么不洁的意念或炽情的恋慕，只有对她的不幸、对她灵魂之灭亡所怀的怜恤与同情，却不知道这事原是魔鬼的计谋；因为他实在是黑暗，却假装为光。于是他开始与这少女倾谈，同她讲论认识神的圣言，说：「女士，愿你认识那永远活着的神，不要在诸偶像的错谬中灭亡；要认识你的主、这全世界的主宰，你就必成为有福，作那不死新郎的新妇。」他用许多这样的话劝勉她的时候，恶灵立时向这女子低语，叫她在他脚下铺开欺骗的网罗，把他有福的灵魂拖入情欲的坑——正如他从前借着夏娃（Eve）待我们的始祖，可怜哪！就这样悲惨地把始祖逐出乐园、逐出神之外，叫他在福祉与永远生命的地位上，反服在死亡之下。

这女子听见约阿萨弗满有智慧的话，却因没有悟性而不能明白，就这样作答，作了恶者的舌头和喉舌：「先生，你若顾念我的得救，愿意领我到你的神那里，救我这可怜的灵魂，那么你自己也应允我一个请求，我就立时向我列祖的神告别，归向你的神，事奉他直到我最后一口气；你也必因我的得救、因我归向神而得着赏报。」

他问：「女士，你要请求什么呢？」她却把全人——身段、眼色和声音——摆出媚惑他的样式，回答说：「你愿在婚配的纽带中与我连合，我就欢欢喜喜遵行你一切的吩咐。」

他说：「女士啊，你这艰难的请求是徒然的了。我固然切切顾念你的得救，渴望把你从灭亡的深处拉上来，但要借着不洁的连合玷污我的身体，于我是痛苦的事，且是断乎不能的。」

===Original===
She, seeking to make the way straight and smooth for him, cried, “Why dost thou, who are so wise, talk thus? Wherefore speakest thou of it as of defilement and shameful intercourse? I am not unacquainted with the Christian books: nay, I have met with many volumes in mine own country, and have heard the discourses of many Christians. What, is it not written in one of your books, ‘Marriage is honourable, and the bed undefiled’? and, ‘It is better to marry than to burn’? and again, ‘What God hath joined together, let not man put asunder’? Do not your Scriptures teach that all the righteous men of old, patriarchs and prophets, were wedded? Is it not written that the mighty Peter, whom ye call Prince of the Apostles, was a married man? Who, then, hath persuaded thee to call this defilement? Methink, sir, thou strayest utterly away from the truth of your doctrines.”

“Yea, Lady,” said he, “all this is even as thou sayest. It is permitted to all who will to live in wedlock, but not to them that have once made promise to Christ to be virgins. For myself, ever since I was cleansed in the laver of Holy Baptism from the sins of my youth and ignorance, I have resolved to present myself pure to Christ, and how shall I dare break my covenants with God?”

Again quoth the damsel, “Let this also be thy pleasure, as thou wilt. But fulfil me one other small and trivial desire of mine, if thou art in very truth minded for to save my soul. Keep company with me this one night only, and grant me to revel in thy beauty, and do thou in turn take thy fill of my comeliness. And I give thee my word, that, with daybreak, I will become a Christian, and forsake all the worship of my gods. Not only shalt thou be pardoned for this dealing, but thou shalt receive recompense from thy God because of my salvation, for thy Scripture saith, ‘There is joy in heaven over one sinner that repenteth.’ If, therefore, there is joy in heaven over the conversion of a sinner, shall not great recompense be due to the causer of that conversion? Yea, so it is: and dispute it not. Did not even the Apostles, the leaders of your religion, do many a thing by dispensation, at times transgressing a commandment on account of a greater one? Is not Paul said to have circumcised Timothy on account of a greater dispensation? And yet circumcision hath been reckoned by Christians as unlawful, but yet he did not decline so to do. And many other such things shalt thou find in thy Scriptures. If then in very sooth, as thou sayest, thou seekest to save my soul, fulfil me this my small desire. And although I seek to be joined with thee in the full estate of matrimony, yet, sith this is contrary to thy mind, I will never constrain thee again, but will do everything that liketh thee. For the rest, do not thou utterly abhor me; but hearken to me for the nonce, and thou shalt deliver me from superstitious error, and thou shalt do whatever seemeth thee good hereafter all the days of thy life.”

Thus spake she; for indeed she had, for her adviser, one to whom she lent a privy ear, and the pirate was well versed in Scripture, being verily the creator and teacher of iniquity. Thus then she spake with fawning words entangling him, right and left, around with her toils and meshes, and she began to shake the citadel of his soul, and to slacken his tension of purpose, and to soften the temper of his mind. Then the sower of these evil tares, and enemy of the righteous, when he saw the young man’s heart wavering, was full of joy, and straightway called to the evil spirits that were with him, crying, “Look you how yond damsel hasteth to bring to pass all that we were unable to accomplish! Hither! fall we now furiously upon him: for we shall find none other season so favourable to perform the will of him that sent us.” Thus spake this crafty spirit to his hounds: and straightway they lept on that soldier of Christ, disquieting all the powers of his soul, inspiring him with vehement love for the damsel, and kindling within him the fiercest fire of lust.

===Chinese===
她想要为他把路修得又直又平，就喊着说：「你这有智慧的人，怎么还这样说呢？你为何称这事为玷污、为可耻的交合呢？我并非不认识基督徒的书卷：不，我在本国见过许多册，也听过许多基督徒的讲论。怎么，你们书中岂不是有一处写着：『婚姻，人人都当尊重，床也不可污秽』么？又写着：『与其欲火攻心，倒不如嫁娶为妙』么？还有：『神所配合的，人不可分开』么？你们的圣经岂不是教导，古时一切义人，就是众族长和众先知，都曾婚娶么？岂不是记着，你们称为众使徒之首的大能者彼得，是个有妻室的人么？那么，是谁劝服你称这事为玷污呢？先生，我看你是全然偏离了你们教义的真理了。」

他说：「女士，诚然，这一切正像你所说的。凡愿意的，都蒙准许度婚娶的生活，只是那些既已向基督许愿守童身的人不可。至于我，自从我在圣洗礼的洗濯盆中，从幼年无知的罪里得蒙洁净，我就已定意将自己纯洁地献给基督，我怎敢背弃我与神所立的约呢？」

女子又说：「这事也凭你情愿吧。但你若真心要救我的灵魂，就再成全我一个小小而无足轻重的愿望。只这一夜，请你陪着我，容我饱享你的俊美，你也转而饱享我的秀色。我向你保证，一到天亮，我就作基督徒，弃绝我诸神的一切敬拜。你不但不因这事被定罪，反要因我的得救从你的神得着赏报，因为你的圣经说：『一个罪人悔改，在天上也要这样为他欢喜。』若一个罪人的归正使天上欢喜，那促成这归正的人，岂不更当得大赏报么？诚然是这样；你不要争辩了。你们宗教的领袖众使徒，岂不也多次借着通融行事，有时因一条更大的诫命而暂时干犯另一条么？不是有人说，保罗为着更大的通融，给提摩太（Timothy）行了割礼么？割礼虽已被基督徒算为不合法，他却不推辞这样行。还有许多这样的事，你要在自己的圣经中寻见。所以，你若真如你所说，切望救我的灵魂，就成全我这个小小的愿望吧。我固然寻求与你结为全备的婚姻，但这既违反你的心意，我就必不再勉强你，凡你所喜悦的，我都必行。此外，你不要全然厌弃我；这一次且听我，你就要救我脱离迷信的错谬；此后你一生的日子，凡你看为好的，都必随你行。」

她这样说；因为她确实有一位出谋的，她把私下的耳朵借给他——那海盗熟悉圣经，实在是不义的创始者与教师。她就这样用谄媚的话，左缠右绕，用她的罗网和圈套困住他，开始摇撼他灵魂的堡垒，松懈他意志的绷紧，软化他心志的刚毅。于是，这撒稗子的、义人的仇敌，见少年的心摇动，就满了喜乐，随即向与他同在的众恶灵呼喊说：「看哪，那女子何等急切地要成全我们所不能成全的一切！来吧！我们这就猛烈地扑上他去：因为我们再找不到这样有利的时机，来成全那差我们来者的旨意了。」这诡诈的灵向他的猎犬如此说了，他们就立时扑向基督的那位兵士，搅扰他灵魂的一切能力，向他灌输对那女子的炽烈爱恋，又在他里面燃起最猛烈的欲火。

===Original===
When Ioasaph saw that he was greatly inflamed, and was being led captive into sin, and perceived that his thoughts about the salvation of the damsel and her conversion to God had been set like bait on hook to hide the deed which she purposed, and were troubling him with the suggestion of the enemy, that, for the salvation of a soul, it was not sin for once to lie with a woman, then in the agony of his soul he drew a deep and lamentable groan, and nerved himself to pray, and, with streams of tears running down his cheeks, he cried aloud to him that is able to save them that trust in him, saying, “On thee, O Lord, have I set my trust: let me not be confounded forever; neither let mine enemies triumph over me, that hold by thy right hand. But stand thou by me at this hour, and according to thy will make straight my path, that thy glorious and dreadful name may be glorified in me thy servant, because thou art blessed forever. Amen.”

Now when he had prayed in tears for many hours, and often bent the knee, he sunk down upon the pavement. After he had slumbered awhile, he saw himself carried off by certain dread men, and passing through places which he had never heretofore beheld. He stood in a mighty plain, all abloom with fresh and fragrant flowers, where he descried all manner of plants of divers colours, charged with strange and marvellous fruits, pleasant to the eye and inviting to the touch. The leaves of the trees rustled clearly in a gentle breeze, and, as they shook, sent forth a gracious perfume that cloyed not the sense. Thrones were set there, fashioned of the purest gold and costly stones, throwing out never so bright a lustre, and radiant settles among wondrous couches too beautiful to be described. And beside them there were running waters exceeding clear, and delightful to the eye. When these dread men had led him through this great and wondrous plain, they brought him to a city that glistered with light unspeakable, whose walls were of dazzling gold, with high uprear’d parapets, built of gems such as man hath never seen. Ah! who could describe the beauty and brightness of that city? Light, ever shooting from above, filled all her streets with bright rays; and winged squadrons, each of them itself a light, dwelt in this city, making such melody as mortal ear ne’er heard. And Ioasaph heard a voice crying, “This is the rest of the righteous: this the gladness of them that have pleased the Lord.” When these dread men had carried him out from thence, they spake of taking him back to earth. But he, that had lost his heart to that scene of joyance and heartsease, exclaimed, “Reave me not, reave me not, I pray you, of this unspeakable joy, but grant me also to dwell in one corner of this mighty city.” But they said, “It is impossible for thee to be there now; but, with much toil and sweat, thou shalt come hither, if thou constrain thyself.”

===Chinese===
约阿萨弗见自己大大燃起欲火，正被掳往罪里去，又看出他论到那女子得救、归向神的意念，原像钩上的饵，为要掩饰她图谋的事，并且仇敌的暗示搅扰着他——暗示说，为救一个灵魂，与一个女人同寝一次并不算罪——就在灵魂的剧痛中发出一声深长可悲的叹息，振作精神祷告，泪如溪流顺着两颊淌下，向那能拯救一切信靠他者的高声呼喊说：「主啊，我素来倚靠你；求你永不叫我羞愧，也不叫我的仇敌向我夸胜——我这凭你右手得扶持的人。求你在这时刻站在我旁边，照你的旨意修直我的道路，使你荣耀而可畏的名，在你仆人我身上得着荣耀，因为你是永远可称颂的。阿们。」

他流泪祷告了许多时候，又屡次屈膝，随后瘫倒在石地上。他打盹片时，见自己被几个可畏的人带走，经过他从未见过的地方。他站在一片广大的平原上，遍地开着新鲜芬芳的花朵，他在那里看见各色各样的植物，结着奇异可畏的果实，悦人眼目，引人想摸。树叶在柔风中飒飒作响，摇动之间发出芬芳的香气，不使感官厌腻。那里安设着宝座，是用至纯的金子和宝石造的，射出无比明亮的光辉；奇妙的卧榻之间又有光华四射的座椅，华美得难以形容。旁边有流水，极其清澈，悦人眼目。这些可畏的人领他走过这广大奇妙的平原，把他带进一座城，城中闪烁着说不出的光，城墙是耀目的金子，城堞高耸，是用世人从未见过的宝石建造的。啊！谁能述说那城的美丽与光辉呢？光从上头不断放射，以明亮的光线充满她一切街道；有翅膀的队伍住在这城里，各个自身就是光，奏出必死者的耳朵从未听过的乐声。约阿萨弗听见有声音呼喊说：「这就是义人的安息；这是蒙主喜悦之人的喜乐。」这些可畏的人把他从那里带出来，说到要带他回地上。但他已把心迷于那喜乐安宁的景象，就喊道：「求你们不要夺去，不要夺去我这说不出的喜乐；也容我住在这大城的一个角落里吧。」他们却说：「你现在不能在这里；但你若约束自己，再经过许多劳苦流汗，就必来到这里。」

===Original===
Thus spake they; and again they crossed that mighty plain, and bare him to regions of darkness and utter woe, where sorrow matched the brightness which he had seen above. There was darkness without a ray of light, and utter gloom, and the whole place was full of tribulation and trouble. There blazed a glowing furnace of fire, and there crept the worm of torment. Revengeful powers were set over the furnace, and there were some that were burning piteously in the fire, and a voice was heard, saying, “This is the place of sinners; this the punishment for them that have defiled themselves by foul practices.” Hereupon Ioasaph was carried thence by his guides; and, when he came to himself, immediately he trembled from head to foot, and, like a river, his eyes dropped tears, and all the comeliness of that wanton damsel and her fellows was grown more loathsome to him than filth and rottenness. And as he mused in his heart on the memory of the visions, in longing for the good and in terror of the evil, he lay on his bed utterly unable to arise.

Then was the king informed of his son’s sickness; and he came and asked what ailed him. And Ioasaph told him his vision, and said, “Wherefore hast thou laid a net for my feet, and bowed down my soul? If the Lord had not helped me, my soul had well nigh dwelt in hell. But how loving is God unto Israel, even unto such as are of a true heart! He hath delivered me that am lowly from the midst of the dogs. For I was sore troubled and I fell on sleep: but God my Saviour from on high hath visited me, and showed me what joy they lose that provoke him and to what punishments they subject themselves. And now, O my father, since thou hast stopped thine ears not to hear the voice that will charm thee to good, at least forbid me not to walk the straight road. For this I desire, this I long for, to forsake all, and reach that place, where Barlaam the servant of Christ hath his dwelling, and with him to finish what remaineth of my life. But if thou keep me back by force, thou shalt quickly see me die of grief and despair, and thou shalt be no more called father, nor have me to thy son.”

===Chinese===
他们这样说了，又穿过那广大的平原，把他带到黑暗与彻底祸患之境，那里的愁苦，与他先前在上头所见的辉煌恰好相称。那里是没有一线光的黑暗，是完全的幽暗，全境满了患难与困苦。那里烧着炎炎的火炉，那里爬着刑罚的虫。复仇的权势被派在火炉之上，有人在火中凄惨地焚烧；有声音说：「这是罪人的地方；这是那些以污行玷污自己之人的刑罚。」此后，约阿萨弗被引路的从那里带出；他一醒过来，立时全身颤抖，眼泪如同江河滴落；那邪荡少女和她同伴们的一切妩媚，在他看已比污秽和腐臭更加可憎。他心中追想异象的记忆，一面切慕那善，一面畏惧那恶，躺在榻上，全然无力起身。

后来王得了儿子患病的消息，就来问他哪里不适。约阿萨弗把他的异象告诉他，说：「你为何给我的脚设下网罗，压下我的灵魂呢？若不是主帮助我，我的魂几乎就住在阴间了。神向以色列，向心里诚实的人，是何等有慈爱！他已将我这卑微的人从犬类中间救出来。我曾困苦忧伤，便沉睡下去；但救我的神自高天眷顾我，指给我看：那些惹动他的人要失去怎样的喜乐，又把自己陷在怎样的刑罚之下。我的父啊，你既塞住耳朵，不听那引你向善的声音，如今至少不要禁止我行那正直的路。我所愿的、我所切慕的，就是撇下一切，到达基督的仆人巴拉阿姆居住的地方，与他一同度尽我余生未完的日子。但你若强行拦阻我，你必快快看见我因忧愁绝望而死，你也必不再称为父，也不再得我为儿子。」

## XXXI / 三十一

===Original===
Again therefore the king was seized with despondency, and again he was like to abjure his whole way of life; and with strange thoughts he went again unto his own palace. But the evil spirits, that had been sent out by Theudas for to attack the young saint, returned to him, and, lovers of leasing though they were, confessed their shameful defeat, for they bare visible tokens of their defeat, upon their evil countenance. Said Theudas, “And be ye so weak and puny that ye cannot get the better of one young stripling?” Then did the evil spirits, constrained, to their sorrow, by the might of God, bring to light the truth, saying, “We cannot abide even the sight of the might of Christ, and the symbol of his Passion, which they call the Cross. For, when that sign is made, immediately all we, the princes of the air, and the rulers of the darkness of the world, are utterly routed and discomfited, even before the sign is completed. When we first fell upon this youth, we vexed him sore; but when he called on Christ for help, and armed him with the sign of the Cross, he routed us in angry wise, and stablished himself in safety. So incontinent we found a weapon, wherewith our chief did once confront the first-made man and prevailed against him. And verily we should have made this young man’s hope vain; but again Christ was called on for help, and he consumed us in the fire of his wrath from above, and put us to flight. We have determined to approach the prince no more.” Thus, then, did the evil spirits plainly make known unto Theudas all that was come to pass.

But the king, perplexed on every side, again summoned Theudas, and said, “Most wisest of men, all that seemed good to thee have we fulfilled, but have found no help therein. But now, if thou hast any device left, we will make trial thereof. Peradventure I shall find some escape from this evil.”

Then did Theudas ask for a meeting with his son; and on the morrow the king took him and went forth to visit the prince. The king sat down and provoked debate, upbraiding and chiding him for his disobedience and stubbornness of mind. When Ioasaph again maintained his ease, and loudly declared that he valued nothing so much as the love of Christ, Theudas came forward and said, “Wherefore, Ioasaph, dost thou despise our immortal gods, that thou hast departed from their worship, and, thus incensing thy father the king, art become hateful to all the people? Dost thou not owe thy life to the gods? And did they not present thee to the king in answer to his prayer, thus redeeming him from the bondage of childlessness?” While this Theudas, waxen old in wickedness, was putting forth these many vain arguments and useless propositions, and weaving words about the preaching of the Gospel, desiring to turn it into mockery, and magnify idolatry, Ioasaph, the son of the heavenly king, and citizen of that city which the Lord hath builded and not man, waited a while and then said unto him,

===Chinese===
王再次陷于沮丧，再次几乎要弃绝他全部的生活之道；他怀着异样的思绪，又回到自己的宫里。但特乌达斯先前差出去、要攻击那年轻圣徒的众鬼魔回到他那里；它们虽是惯于说谎之流，却承认了自己可耻的败绩，因为它们邪恶的面容上带着战败的明显痕迹。特乌达斯说：『你们竟这般软弱渺小，连一个少年人都胜不过么？』于是众鬼魔被神的大能所迫——它们引以为苦——只得将实情显露出来，说：『我们连基督的大能、并他受难的表号——就是他们所称的十字架——的景象都经受不起。因为那记号一画出来，我们这些空中的掌权者、这幽暗世界的众执政，不等记号画完，就立时全然溃败、狼狈不堪。我们初扑向这少年的时候，曾重重苦害他；但他一呼求基督救助，以十字架的记号披挂自身，就忿然将我们击溃，安然坚立。于是我们随即另寻得一件兵器，就是我们首领当初用以迎击那首先受造的人、并且胜过了他的那件。我们本当已使这少年的指望归于虚空；但基督再次被呼求救助，就从上头以忿怒之火吞灭我们，将我们赶散奔逃。我们已定意不再挨近那王子。』众鬼魔就这样把所发生的一切，明明地向特乌达斯述说了。

但王四面受窘，再次召了特乌达斯来，说：『至智至慧的人哪，凡你看为好的，我们都已遵行，却在其中寻不着帮助。如今你若还剩什么计谋，我们总要试它一试。或者我能从这恶事中寻着一条出路。』

于是特乌达斯求与王子会面；次日，王带他同去探访王子。王坐下，挑起辩论，责备叱责他的违命与心思的顽梗。约阿萨弗依旧从容自若，朗声宣告自己以基督的爱为至宝、胜于万物，特乌达斯便上前说：『约阿萨弗啊，你为何藐视我们不死的诸神，离弃了对他们的敬拜，又因此触怒你父王，使自己成了众民所憎恶的呢？你的性命岂不是亏欠于诸神么？他们岂不是应允王的祷告，将你赐给王，把他从不育的捆绑中赎了出来么？』这特乌达斯在恶事上已经老迈，正当他抛出这许多虚妄的论据、无用的提议，就着福音的传讲编织言词，想把它化为笑谈、把拜偶像之事抬举起来的时候，约阿萨弗——那天上之王的儿子、主所建造而非人手所建造之城邑的公民——静候片刻，然后对他说：

===Original===
“Give ear, thou abyss of error, blacker than the darkness that may be felt, thou seed of Babylon, child of the building of the tower of Chalané, whereby the world was confounded, foolish and pitiable dotard, whose sins outweigh the iniquity of the five cities that were destroyed by fire and brimstone. Why wouldest thou mock at the preaching of salvation, whereby darkness hath been made light, the wanderers have found the way, they that were lost in dire captivity have been recalled. Tell me whether is better? To worship God Almighty, with the only-begotten Son and the Holy Ghost, God increate and immortal, the beginning and wellspring of good, whose power is beyond compare, and his glory incomprehensible, before whom stand thousand thousands, and ten thousand times ten thousand of Angels and heavenly hosts, and heaven and earth are full of his glory, by whom all things were brought into being out of nothing, by whom everything is upheld and sustained and ordered by his providence; or to serve deadly devils and lifeless idols, whose glory and boast is in adultery and the corrupting of boys, and other works of iniquity that have been recorded concerning your gods in the books of your superstition? Have ye no modesty, ye miserable men, fuel for unquenchable fire, true copy of the Chaldean race, have ye no shame to worship dead images, the works of men’s hands? Ye have carved stone and graven wood and called it God. Next ye take the best bullock out of your folds, or (may be) some other of your fairest beasts, and in your folly make sacrifice to your dead divinity. Your sacrifice is of more value than your idol; for the image was fashioned by man, but the beast was created by God. How much wiser is the unreasonable beast than thou the reasonable man? For it knoweth the hand that feedeth it, but thou knowest not that God by whom thou wast created out of nothing, by whom thou livest, and art preserved; and thou callest God that which thou sawest, but now, smitten by steel, and burnt and moulded in the fire, and beaten with hammers, which thou hast covered around with silver and gold, and raised from the ground, and set on high. Then, falling upon the earth, thou liest baser than the base stone, worshipping not God but thine own dead and lifeless handiwork. Or rather, the idol hath no right to be called even dead, for how can that have died which never lived? Thou shouldest invent some new name worthy of such madness. Thy stone god is broken asunder; thy potsherd god shattered; thy brazen god rusteth; thy gold or silver god is melted down. Aye, and thy gods are sold, some for a paltry, others for a great price. Not their divinity but their material giveth them value. But who buyeth God? Who offereth God for sale? And how is that god that cannot move called God? Seest thou not that the god that standeth cannot sit, and the god that sitteth cannot stand?

===Chinese===
「你要侧耳而听，你这错谬的深渊，比那摸得着的黑暗更黑；你这巴比伦之种、甲尼（Chalané）筑塔之人的后裔——世界正是因那塔变乱的；你这愚妄可怜的老朽，你的罪比那被火与硫磺所毁五城的罪愆更重。你为何要讥诮救恩的传讲呢？借着它，黑暗得以成光，流离飘荡的人寻得道路，陷于可怕囚掳中失丧的人蒙了召回。你告诉我，哪一样更好？是敬拜全能的神——与独生子和圣灵同受敬拜的那位：他是非受造而不朽的神，是众善的起头与泉源，他的权能无可比拟，他的荣耀无可测度；众天使与天军千千事奉他、万万在他面前侍立；天与地都满了他的荣耀；万有本是借着他从无有中被造成，万有也借着他蒙托住、蒙保全、蒙他的护理所安排——还是服事那叫人死的鬼魔和无生命的偶像？那些神的荣耀与夸口，是在奸淫和狎玩男童，并你们那些迷信书卷里所记、关于你们诸神的别样恶行。你们这些可怜人哪——不灭之火的燃料、迦勒底族类的真副本——竟无一点廉耻么？竟不羞于敬拜死的形像、人手所造之物么？你们雕琢石头、刻画木头，就称它为神。接着，你们从圈中挑出最上等的公牛，或者（或许吧）别的最美好的牲畜，在愚妄中向你们那死了的神性献祭。你们的祭物比你们的偶像更有价值；因为形像是人制作的，牲畜却是神创造的。那无理性的牲畜，比你这有理性的人聪明了多少呢！因为它认识喂养它的手，你却不认识那位使你从无中受造、你借着他活着、蒙保守的神；你竟称那东西为神——就是你方才亲眼见它被钢铁劈凿、在火中焚烧熔铸、又被锤子击打，随后你用金银将它周身包裹，把它从地里抬起、安置在高处的。之后你俯伏在地，躺着比那卑贱的石头更卑贱，敬拜的不是神，乃是你自己死的、无生命的手工。不，更确切地说，偶像连称为死的名分也没有，因为从未活过的，怎会死呢？你倒该为这样的癫狂发明一个相称的新名字。你的石神破裂了；你的瓦神粉碎了；你的铜神生锈了；你的金银神熔化了。是啊，你们的神还被贩卖，有的卖得贱价，有的卖得重价。给它们定价值的，不是它们的神性，而是它们的材质。可是，谁买神呢？谁拿神出来卖呢？那不能动的，怎能称为神呢？你岂不看见：站立的神不能坐，坐着的神不能站么？

===Original===
