“Be ashamed, thou fool, and lay thine hand upon thy mouth, thou victim of folly, that commendest such things as these. Estranged from the truth, thou hast been led astray by false images, fashioning statues and attaching to the works of thine own hands the name of God. O wretched man, return to thy senses, and learn that thou art older than the god made by thee. This is downright madness. Being a man, thou hast persuaded thyself that thou canst make God. How can this be? Thou makest not God, but the likeness of a man, or of some beast, sans tongue, sans throat, sans brains, sans inwards, so that it is the similitude neither of a man, nor of a beast, but only a thing of no use and sheer vanity. Why therefore flatterest thou things that cannot feel? Why sittest thou at the feet of things that cannot move and help thee? But for the skill of the mason, or timber-wright, or hammer-smith, thou hadst not had a god. Had there been no warders nigh at hand, thou hadst lost thy god. He, to whom many a populous city of fools prayeth as God to guard it, the same hath suite of guards at hand to save him from being stolen. And if he be of silver or gold, he is carefully guarded; but if of stone or clay or any other less costly ware, he guardeth himself, for with you, no doubt, a god of clay is stronger than one of gold.”

===Chinese===
「愚妄人哪，你当抱愧，用手捂口；你这愚妄的牺牲品，竟称许这样的事。你既与真理隔绝，就被虚假的形像引入迷途，雕造像，又把神的名归于自己手所作的工。可怜的人哪，你醒悟过来吧，要知道你比你造出来的神更古老。这是全然的癫狂。你既是人，竟说服自己能造神。这怎么可能呢？你造出来的不是神，乃是人的形像，或某种走兽的形像，无舌、无喉、无脑、无脏腑，以致它既不是人的肖像，也不是兽的肖像，只是一件无用之物、纯粹的虚妄。那么，你为何谄媚那些没有知觉之物？为何坐在那些不能动弹、不能帮助你的东西脚下？若不是靠石匠、木匠、锤匠的手艺，你根本就不会有神。若没有看守的人在近旁，你的神早就丢失了。许多愚人聚居的城邑向他祷告，尊他为神，求他保卫；这位神身旁却得有一班卫士，救他免遭偷窃。他若是金银造的，就被小心看守；若是石头、泥土或别的贱料造的，他就自己看守自己——因为在你们中间，无疑泥造的神比金造的更刚强。」

===Original===
“Do we not, then, well to laugh you to scorn, or rather to weep over you, as men blind and without understanding? Your deeds are deeds of madness and not of piety. Your man of war maketh to himself an image after the similitude of a warrior, and calleth it Ares. And the lecher, making a symbol of his own soul, deifieth his vice and calleth it Aphrodite. Another, in honour of his own love of wine, fashioneth an idol which he calleth Dionysus. Likewise lovers of all other evil things set up idols of their own lusts; for they name their lusts their gods. And therefore, before their altars, there are lascivious dances, and strains of lewd songs and mad revelries. Who could recount in order their abominable doings? Who could endure to defile his lips by the repeating of their filthy communications? But these are manifest to all, even if we hold our peace. These be thine objects of worship, O Theudas, who art more senseless than thine idols. Before these thou biddest me fall down and worship. This verily is the counsel of thine iniquity and senseless mind. But thou thyself shalt be like unto them, and all such as put their trust in them.”

===Chinese===
「这样，我们讥笑你们岂不正对么？或不如说，看你们如瞎眼无知的人而为你哀哭，岂不更合宜么？你们的作为是癫狂的作为，不是敬虔的作为。你们的战士给自己造一个照着武士样式仿制的像，称它为阿瑞斯。好色之徒把自己灵魂的模样作成表号，把自己的恶行奉为神明，称它为阿佛洛狄忒。又有人为尊崇自己的嗜酒，造一个偶像，称它为狄俄尼索斯。照样，凡贪爱别样恶事的人，都为自己私欲立像；因为他们把自己的私欲唤作自己的神。因此，在他们的祭坛前，有淫荡的舞蹈、邪曲的歌调和癫狂的欢闹。他们可憎的作为，谁能按着次序述尽呢？他们污秽的言语，谁忍心复述而玷污自己的口唇呢？但这些事虽经我们缄默不言，也人人皆知。特乌达斯哪，这些就是你所敬拜的对象——你比你的偶像更无知。你竟吩咐我在这些面前俯伏下拜。这实在是你邪恶无知的心思所出的谋算。但你必变得与它们一样，凡倚靠它们的也是如此。」

===Original===
“As for me, I will serve my God, and to him will I wholly sacrifice myself, to God, the Creator and protector of all things through our Lord Jesus Christ, my hope, by whom we have access unto the Father of lights, in the Holy Ghost: by whom we have been redeemed from bitter slavery by his blood. For if he had not humbled himself so far as to take the form of a servant, we had not received the adoption of sons. But he humbled himself for our sake, not considering the Godhead a thing to be grasped, but he remained that which he was, and took on himself that which he was not, and conversed with men, and mounted the Cross in his flesh, and was laid in the sepulchre by the space of three days; he descended into hell, and brought out from thence them whom the fierce prince of this world held prisoners, sold into bondage by sin. What harm then befell him thereby that thou thinkest to make mock of him? Seest thou not yonder sun, into how many a barren and filthy place he darteth his rays? Upon how many a stinking corpse doth he cast his eye? Hath he therefore any stain of reproach? Doth he not dry and shrivel up filth and rottenness, and give light to dark places, himself the while unharmed and incapable of receiving any defilement? And what of fire? Doth it not take iron, which is black and cold in itself, and work it into white heat and harden it? Doth it receive any of the properties of the iron? When the iron is smitten and beaten with hammers is the fire any the worse, or doth it in any way suffer harm?”

===Chinese===
「至于我，我要事奉我的神，将自己全然当作祭物献给他——就是那万有的创造者与保护者，借着我们的主耶稣基督、我的盼望；我们借着他，在圣灵里得以进到众光之父面前；也借着他，我们靠他的宝血从那苦楚的奴役中被赎出来。他若不曾自卑到这般地步，取了仆人的形像，我们就不能得着儿子的名分。但他为我们的缘故自卑，不以神性为强夺的，仍旧是他所是的，又取了他所不是的，与人同居共语，在肉身中登上十字架，在坟墓里安放了三日之久；他下到阴间，从那里领出那些被这世界的凶暴君王所囚、被罪卖为奴仆的人。这样，他究竟受了什么损害，叫你想讥诮他呢？你看不见那太阳么？它把光线射进多少荒芜污秽之地？它把眼目垂顾多少发臭的尸首？它因此沾了什么羞辱的污点么？它岂不是晒干烤缩那污秽腐臭，光照黑暗之处，自己却毫无损伤、不能沾染任何污秽么？火又怎样呢？它岂不是把本身又黑又冷的铁烧成炽白、使它坚硬么？它岂从铁接受了什么性质么？当铁被击打、被锤子锤锻的时候，火是否因此受损，或在任何方面受伤害呢？

===Original===
“If, then, these created and corruptible things take no hurt from contact with things commoner than themselves, with what reason dost thou, O foolish and stony-hearted man, presume to mock at me for saying that the Son, the Word of God, never departing from the Father’s glory, but remaining the same God, for the salvation of men hath taken upon him the flesh of man, to the end that he may make men partakers of his divine and intelligent nature and may lead our substance out of the nether parts of hell, and honour it with heavenly glory; to the end that by taking of our flesh he may ensnare and defeat the ruler of the darkness of this world, and free our race from his tyranny. Wherefore, I tell thee, without suffering he met the suffering of the Cross, presenting therein his two natures. For, as man, he was crucified; but, as God, he darkened the sun, shook the earth, and raised from their graves many bodies that had fallen asleep. Again, as man, he died; but, as God, after that he had harried hell, he rose again. Wherefore also the prophet cried, Hell is in bitterness at having met thee below: for it was put to bitter derision, supposing that it had received a mere man, but finding God, and being made suddenly empty and led captive. Therefore, as God, he rose again, and ascended into heaven, from whence he was never parted. And our nature, so worthless and senseless beyond everything, so graceless and dishonoured, hath he made higher than all things, and established it upon a throne of honour, with immortal honour shining round. What harm therefore came to God, the Word, that thou blasphemest without a blush? Go to! Better were it to make this confession, and to worship such a God, who is good and a lover of mankind, who commandeth righteousness, enjoineth continency, ordaineth chastity, teacheth mercy, giveth faith, preacheth peace; who is called and is himself the very truth, the very love, the very goodness. Him were it not better to worship than thy gods of many evil passions, of shameful names and shameful lives? Woe unto you that are more stony-hearted than the stones, and more senseless than the senseless, sons of perdition, inheritors of darkness! But blessed am I, and all Christian folk, having a good God and a lover of mankind! They that serve him, though, for a season in this life they endure evil, yet shall they reap the immortal harvest of recompense in the kingdom of unending and divine felicity.”

===Chinese===
「既然这些受造而必朽之物，接触比自己卑贱的东西尚且不受损伤，你这愚妄而心如铁石的人哪，还有什么道理，胆敢讥诮我这话呢？我说：圣子、神的道，从未离开父的荣耀，仍是一样的神，却为救人的缘故取了人的肉身，为要使人得与他那神性而属理智的性情有份，将我们的本体从阴间的低下处领出来，用天上的荣耀尊荣它；为要借着取我们的肉身，诱陷并击败那管辖这幽暗世界的执政者，救我们的族类脱离他的暴虐。所以我告诉你，他是在不受苦之中受了十字架的苦，在其中将自己的二性一并显明。因为作为人，他被钉十字架；但作为神，他叫日头变黑，震动大地，叫许多已睡了的身体从坟墓里起来。又作为人，他死了；但作为神，他掳掠了阴间之后就复活了。所以先知也呼喊说：『阴间在下面遇见你，就陷于苦楚：因为它受了苦涩的讥诮，本以为所接收的不过是一个人，却发现是神，就蓦然被倒空，反遭掳掠。』所以，他作为神复活了，又升上高天——他从未离开过那里。我们这本性，卑贱麻木超乎万有、无恩无荣，他却使它高过一切，安置在尊荣的宝座上，不朽的尊荣四围发光。这样，神的道究竟受了什么损害，叫你毫无羞惭地亵渎他呢？来吧！宁可作这样的认信、敬拜这样一位神，倒好——他是良善的，是爱世人的；他吩咐公义，命定节制，立定贞洁，教训怜悯，赐下信心，传扬和平；他被称为、也实在就是真理本身、爱本身、良善本身。敬拜他，岂不胜过敬拜你们那些满怀邪恶情欲、名字可羞、生活可耻的神么？你们这些比石头更硬心、比无知之物更无知的人有祸了，你们这些灭亡之子、黑暗的承受者！但我是有福的，众基督徒也是有福的，因为我们有一位良善而爱世人的神！事奉他的人，虽然在今生一时忍受恶事，却必在那无穷而属神之福的国度里，收割不朽的报偿。」

## XXXII / 三十二

===Original===
Theudas said unto him, "Behold, it is evident that our religion was instituted by many mighty wise men, and interpreters, marvellous in virtue and learning; and all the kings and rulers of the earth have received it as good and sure in every point. But that of the Galileans was preached by some country peasants, poor and common men, a mere handful, not exceeding twelve in number. How then should one prefer the preaching of these few obscure countrymen to the ordinance of the many that are mighty and brilliantly wise? What is the proof that your teachers be right and the others wrong?"

===Chinese===
特乌达斯对他说：「看哪，我们的宗教显然是由许多有权能的智士和诠释者所创立的，他们在德行和学问上都奇妙非凡；地上的君王和掌权者也无不领受了它，认它每一方面都良善而稳固。而加利利人的宗教，却是由几个乡野农夫传讲的——贫穷的平民，不过寥寥一小群，数目不超过十二人。这样，人怎能宁取这几个无闻乡人的传讲，而舍弃那许多有权能、有辉煌智慧之人的典章呢？你们的师傅对、别人错，凭何证明呢？」

===Original===
Again the king's son made answer, "Belike, Theudas, thou art the ass of the proverb, that heard but heeded not the harp; or rather the adder that stoppeth her ears, that she may not hear the voice of the charmers. Well, therefore, spake the prophet concerning thee, If the Ethiopian can change his skin, or the leopard his spots, then mayest thou also do good, that hast been taught to do evil. Thou fool and blind, why doth not the force of truth bring thee to thy senses? The very fact that your foul idols are commended by many men of marvellous wisdom, and established by kings, while the Gospel is preached by a few men of no mark, showeth the might of our religion and the weakness and deadliness of your wicked doctrines. Because your side, despite its having wise advocates and mighty champions, is dying down, and waxing weak, whilst our religion, though possessed of no human help, shineth from afar brighter than the sun, and hath won the fullness of the world. If it had been set up by orators and philosophers, and had had kings for its succour, thou that art evil wouldst have found occasion to declare that it was wholly of human power. But now, seeing, as thou dost, that the holy Gospel, though composed but by common fishermen, and persecuted by every tyrant, hath after this won the whole world—for its sound hath gone out into all lands, and its words into the ends of the world—what canst thou say but that it is a divine and unconquerable power establishing its own cause for the salvation of mankind? But what proof seekest thou, O fool, that thy prophets are liars and ours true, better than the truths I have told thee? Except thy cause had been vain talk and falsehood, it could not, possessing such human support as it did, have suffered loss and decline. For he saith, 'I have seen the ungodly in great power, and exalted like the cedars of Libanus: and I went by and lo, he was gone: and I sought him but his place could nowhere be found.'

===Chinese===
王子又回答说：「特乌达斯啊，恐怕你就是俗语所说的那头驴，听见了琴声，却不领会；或更像是那塞住耳朵的虺蛇，免得听见行法术者的声音。所以先知论到你的话，说得实在好：『埃塞俄比亚人岂能改变皮肤呢？豹岂能改变斑点呢？若能，你这受教行恶的也能行善了。』你这无知瞎眼的人哪，真理的力量为何不能叫你醒悟过来呢？你们污秽的偶像既被许多智慧非凡的人所称许，又被君王所坚立，而福音却由几个无名之辈传讲——这事实本身，正显明我们宗教的大能，并你们那邪恶教理的软弱与致命。因为你们这一方虽拥有智士为辩护人、勇士为斗士，却正渐渐消沉、日益衰微；我们的宗教虽无任何人的帮助，却自远方照耀，光明胜过太阳，并且赢得了普天之下。这福音若是由演说家和哲学家所建立，又有君王作它的援助，你这行恶的人原会寻得借口，宣称它全然是出于人的权能。但如今，你既亲眼看见：圣福音虽不过由寻常渔夫写成，又被各样暴君所逼迫，至终却赢得了全世界——『它的声音传遍天下，它的言语传到地极』——除了承认那是一种属神而不可征服的大能、为世人的救恩坚立它自己的事业以外，你还能说什么呢？愚妄人哪，你要寻求什么凭据，证明你们的先知是说谎的、我们的是真实的，胜过我所告诉你的这些真理呢？你们的事业若非虚谈与谎言，既有这样的人助，就不至于遭受亏损与衰微了。因为他说：『我见过恶人大有势力，高耸如利巴嫩的香柏树；我从那里经过，看哪，他已经不在了；我寻找他，却寻不见他的所在。』

===Original===
"Concerning you, the defenders of idolatry, were these words spoken by the prophet. For a very, very little while and your place shall not be found: but, like as the smoke vanisheth, and like as wax melteth in face of the fire, so shall ye fail. But, as touching the divine wisdom of the Gospel, thus saith the Lord, 'Heaven and earth shall pass away, but my words shall not pass away.' And again the Psalmist saith, 'Thou, Lord, in the beginning hast laid the foundation of the earth; and the heavens are the work of thy hands. They shall perish, but thou endurest; and they all shall wax old as doth a garment, and as a vesture shalt thou fold them up, and they shall be changed, but thou art the same, and thy years shall not fail!' And those divine preachers of the coming of Christ, those wise fishers of the world, whose nets drew all men from the depths of deceit, whom thou, in thy vileness and bondage to sin, dost vilify, did by signs and wonders and manifold powers shine as the sun in the world, giving sight to the blind, hearing to the deaf, motion to the lame, and life to the dead. Their shadows alone healed all the ailments of men. The devils, whom ye dread as gods, they not only cast forth from men's bodies, but even drave out of the world itself by the sign of the cross, whereby they destroyed all sorcery, and rendered witchcraft powerless. And these men, by curing every disease of man by the power of Christ, and renewing all creation, are rightly admired as preachers of truth by all men of sound mind. But what hast thou thyself to say of thy wise men and orators, whose wisdom God hath made foolish, the advocates of the devil? What worthy memorial have they bequeathed to the world? Tell me. And what canst thou tell of them but unreason and shamefulness, and vain craft that with glosing words concealeth the mire of their unsavoury worship?

===Chinese===
「先知这些话，正是论到你们这些偶像崇拜的护卫者说的。再过片时片时，你们的所在就必寻不见；但如烟之消散、如蜡之在火面前熔化，你们也必这样灭没。至于福音那属神的智慧，主如此说：『天地要废去，我的话却不能废去。』诗篇作者又说：『主啊，你起初立了地的根基，天也是你手所造的。天地都要灭没，你却要长存；天地都要像衣服渐渐旧了，你要将天地卷起来，像一件外衣，天地就都改变了；惟有你永不改变，你的年数没有穷尽！』还有那些传讲基督降临的神圣传道者、那些智慧的渔人——他们的网曾将众人从诡诈的深处拉上来——你这卑贱而受罪捆绑的人竟加以诋毁；他们曾借着神迹、奇事并各样的异能，如太阳一般在世间照耀，叫瞎子看见、聋子听见、瘸子行走、死人复活。单凭他们的影子，就医好了人的一切疾病。你们当作神敬畏的鬼魔，他们不仅从人的身体里赶出去，更借十字架的记号把它们从这世界本身驱逐出去，借此除灭了一切的邪术，使巫术归于无能为力。这些人既凭基督的大能医好了人的各样疾病，又更新了一切受造之物，就理当受一切神志清明的人尊崇，奉为真理的传道者。至于你那些智士和演说家——他们的智慧已蒙神使之变为愚拙，是魔鬼的辩护人——你自己还有什么可说的呢？他们给世界留下了什么值得记念的东西？你说说看。除了悖谬与可耻，除了那用谄媚巧言掩盖其污秽敬拜之泥沼的虚妄诡计，你还能论他们什么呢？

===Original===
"Moreover such of your poets as have been able to soar a little above this great madness have said, with more truth, that they, which are called gods, were men; and because certain of them had been rulers of regions and cities, and others had done something of no great account in their lifetime, men were so deceived as to call them gods. It standeth on record that the man Seruch12 was the first to bring in the use of images. For it is said that in the old times he honoured those who had achieved some memorable deed of courage, friendship, or any other such virtue with statues and pillars. But after generations forgat the intention of their ancestors: and, whereas it was only for remembrance sake that they had set up statues and pillars to the doers of noble deeds, now they were, little by little, led astray through the working of the prince of evil, the devil, and treated as immortal gods men of like passions and corruptible as themselves and further devised sacrifices and drink-offerings for them—the devils, thou mayest know, taking up their abode in these images and diverting to themselves these honours and sacrifices. Accordingly these devils persuade men, who refuse to have God in their knowledge, to consider them as gods for two reasons: first, that they may be glorified by this title (for they are puffed up with arrogance, and delight to be honoured as gods) next, that they may drag their poor dupes into the unquenchable fire prepared for themselves. Hence they teach men all iniquity and filthiness, seeing that they have once subjected themselves to their deceit. So when men had arrived at this pinnacle of evil, they, being darkened, set up every man an idol of his own vice and his own lust, and call it a god. They were abominable in their error, more abominable in the absurdity of the objects that they chose to worship, until the Lord came, and of his tender mercy redeemed us that trust in him from this wicked and deadly error, and taught men the true knowledge of God. For there is no salvation except in him, and there is none other God, neither in heaven, nor in earth, except him only, the Maker of all, who moveth all things by the word of his power: for he saith, 'By the word of the Lord were the heavens made steadfast, and all the power of them by the breath of his mouth,' and, 'All things were made by him, and without him was not anything made that was made.'"

===Chinese===
「再者，你们诗人中凡能稍为飞腾于这大癫狂之上的，都曾以更真的话说过：那些称为神的，原是人；因其中有人曾作地区与城邑的掌权者，又有人在世时行过一些无足轻重的事，世人竟受骗到称他们为神的地步。史有明载：西鹿（Seruch）12 其人首开立像之用。因为据说古时，他以雕像和柱碑尊荣那些成就了某种英勇、友谊或别样德行之可记念事迹的人。但后来的世代忘记了他们列祖的本意：先人给高贵作为者立像立碑，原只是为记念的缘故；后人却因那恶君——魔鬼——的运行，渐渐被引入迷途，把和他们自己一样有血气情欲、一样必朽的人奉为不朽的神明，更为他们制定了献祭与奠祭——那些鬼魔，你可以知道，就住进这些像里，把这些尊荣和祭物转归自己。于是这些鬼魔劝诱那些故意不认识神的人看它们为神，理由有二：第一，好叫它们可以因这名号得荣耀（它们因狂傲而自高，喜悦被人当作神尊荣）；其次，好叫它们能把那些可怜的受骗者拖进为它们自己所预备的不灭之火里。因此，人既一经服在它们的欺骗之下，它们就教训人行种种的不义与污秽。及至人达到了这邪恶的绝顶，他们既心里昏暗，便各人为自己的恶德和私欲立一个偶像，称它为神。他们的错谬是可憎的，他们所拣选敬拜之物的荒谬更是可憎——直到主来临，以他温柔的怜悯，把我们这些信靠他的人从这邪恶致命的错谬里救赎出来，并将神的真知识教训世人。因为除他以外，别无拯救；除了那造化万有、以他权能的命令托住万有的独一者以外，无论在天上、在地上，都再没有别的神：因为经上说：『诸天借耶和华的命而造，万象借他口中的气而成』；又说：『万物是借着他造的；凡被造的，没有一样不是借着他造的。』」

===Original===
When Theudas had heard these sayings, and seen that the word was full of divine wisdom, like one thunderstruck, he was smitten dumb. Now late in time, and with difficulty, came he to understand his own misery, for the word of salvation had touched the darkened vision of his heart, and there fell upon him deep remorse for his past sins. He renounced the error of his idols, and ran towards the light of godliness, and from henceforth departed from his miserable life, and made himself as bitter an enemy of vile affections and sorceries as he before had pledged himself their devoted friend, For at this season he stood up in the midst of the assembly,13 and cried with a loud voice, saying, "Verily, O king, the Spirit of God dwelleth in thy son. Verily, we are defeated, and have no further apology, and have no strength to face the words that he hath uttered. Mighty therefore, in sooth, is the God of the Christians: mighty is their faith: mighty are their mysteries."

Then he turned him round toward the king's son and said, "Tell me now, thou man, whose soul is enlightened, will Christ accept me, if I forsake my evil deeds and turn to him?" "Yea," said that preacher of truth; "Yea, he receiveth thee and all that turn to him. And he not only receiveth thee, but he goeth out to meet thee returning out of the way of iniquity, as though it were a son returning from a far country. And he falleth on his neck and kisseth him, and he strippeth him of the shameful robe of sin, and putteth on him a cloak of brightest glory, making mystic gladness for the powers on high, keeping feast for the return of the lost sheep. The Lord himself saith, 'There is exceeding great joy in heaven over one sinner that repenteth': and again, 'I am not come to call the righteous but sinners to repentance.' And he saith also by the Prophet, 'As I live, saith the Lord, I have no pleasure in the death of the sinner, and the ungodly, but that he should turn from his way and live. Turn ye, turn ye from your evil way. And why will ye die, O house of Israel?' For the wickedness of the wicked shall not hurt him in the day that he turneth from his wickedness, if he do righteousness and walk in the statutes of life, he shall surely live; he shall not die. None of his sins which he hath committed shall be remembered against him. Because he hath done the decree of righteousness, he shall live thereby. And again he crieth by the mouth of another prophet, 'Wash you, make you clean; put away the evil of your doings from before mine eyes; cease to do evil: learn to do well. Come now, and let us reason together: though your sins be as scarlet, I will make them white as snow; though they be red like crimson, I will make them white as wool.' Such therefore being the promises made by God to them that turn to him, tarry not, O thou man, nor make delay: but draw nigh to Christ, our loving God, and be enlightened, and thy face shall not be ashamed. For as soon as thou goest down into the laver of Holy Baptism, all the defilement of the old man, and all the burden of thy many sins, is buried in the water, and passeth into nothingness, and thou comest up from thence a new man, pure from all pollution, with no spot or wrinkle of sin upon thee; and thenceforward it is in thy power ever to keep for thyself the purity that thou gainest hereby through the tender mercy of our God."

When Theudas had been thus instructed, he went out immediately and gat him to his evil den, and took his magical books, and, because they were the beginnings of all evil, and the storehouses of devilish mysteries, burnt them with fire. And he betook himself to the cave of that same holy man, to whom Nachor also had resorted, and told him that which had befallen him, casting dust upon his head, and groaning deeply, and watering himself with his tears, and telling the aged man the full tale of his loathly deeds. He, well skilled in the saving of a soul and the snatching it from the jaw of the wily serpent, charmed away his sorrow with words of salvation, and pledged him forgiveness and promised him a merciful Judge. Then, after he had instructed and charged him to fast many days, he cleansed him in Holy Baptism. And all the days of his life Theudas heartily repented him of his misdeeds, with tears and sighs seeking the favour of God.

===Chinese===
特乌达斯听了这些话，见这话满有属神的智慧，便如遭雷击，哑口无言。只是为时已晚，又历尽艰难，他才明白了自己的悲惨，因为救恩的道触及了他心中昏昧的眼目，他便为已往的罪深自痛悔。他弃绝了他诸偶像的错谬，奔向敬虔之光，从此离开他那悲惨的生活，使自己成为邪恶情欲与邪术的死敌，正如他先前曾立誓作它们的挚友一般。因为就在这时节，他当着会众站立起来，13 放声呼喊说：「王啊，实在地，神的灵住在你儿子里面。实在地，我们败了，再无辩词，也没有力量面对他口中所出的话。所以诚然，基督徒的神是大有能力的：他们的信仰是大有能力的：他们的奥迹也是大有能力的。」

随后他转身向着王子，说：「现在请告诉我，你这灵魂已经蒙光照的人，我若离弃我的恶行、归向他，基督肯收纳我么？」那真理的传道者说：「肯，他收纳你，也收纳一切归向他的人。他不但收纳你，还要出来迎接你这从不义之途上转回的人，如同儿子从远方归来一样；他伏在你的颈项上，连连与你亲嘴，给你脱去那羞辱的罪袍，给你披上至荣耀的衣裳，为在高处的众权能者成就奥秘的喜乐，为那失丧之羊的归回守节设筵。主亲自说：『一个罪人悔改，在天上要为他有极大的喜乐』；又说：『我来本不是召义人，乃是召罪人悔改。』他又借先知说：『主说：我指着我的永生起誓，我断不喜悦罪人死，也不喜悦不虔的人死，惟喜悦他转离自己的道而活。你们转回吧，转回离开你们的恶道。以色列家啊，你们为何要死呢？』恶人的恶，在他转离恶行的那日，必不能害他；他若行公义、遵行生命的律例，就必存活，必不至死。他所犯的一切罪必不被记念在他身上；他既行了公义的定例，就必因此存活。他又借另一位先知的口呼喊说：『你们要洗濯、自洁，从我眼前除掉你们的恶行；要止住作恶，学习行善。你们来，我们彼此辩论：你们的罪虽像朱红，必变成雪白；虽红如丹颜，必白如羊毛。』神既向归向他的人应许了这些，你这人哪，就不要踌躇，不要耽延；只要亲近基督——我们那满有爱心的神——就必蒙光照，你的脸必不蒙羞。因为你一走下圣洗礼的洗濯盆，旧人一切的玷污、你众多罪孽的一切重担，就都埋葬在水里，归于无有；你从那里上来，成了新人，洁净而无染，身上没有罪的玷污、斑点或皱纹；从此以后，你借着我们神温柔的怜悯所取得的这洁净，就常在你的能力之内，可以为自己永远持守。」

特乌达斯既受了这样的教导，立刻出去，回到他罪恶的巢穴，取出他的巫术书——这些书既是一切邪恶的根由，又是鬼魔奥秘的库藏——就用火焚烧了。他又去到那位圣者所住的洞窟，就是纳霍尔先前也曾投奔的那一位，将所遭遇的事告诉他：把尘土撒在自己头上，深深地呻吟，以泪洗面，把那老人所当听的他一切可憎的作为，原原本本向他述说。那位圣者深谙拯救灵魂、从狡诈之蛇的颚中将它夺回之道，就用救恩的言语驱散他的忧伤，向他担保必蒙赦免，又应许他有一位施行怜悯的审判者。然后他教导他、嘱咐他禁食多日，就在圣洗礼中洁净了他。特乌达斯一生的年日，都为自己的恶行诚心悔改，以眼泪叹息寻求神的恩惠。

## XXXIII / 三十三

===Original===
As for the king, when things fortuned thus, he was completely bewildered, and plainly showed his sore vexation and tumult of soul. So again he called all his senators together, and considered what means were still his to deal with his son. Many men put forward many counsels, but that Araches, of whom we have spoken, the most famous in his office, and first of his councillors, spake unto the king, saying, "What was there to be done with thy son, O king, that we have not done, to induce him to follow our doctrines and serve our gods? But, as I perceive, we aim at the impossible. By nature, or, it may be, by chance, he is contentious and implacable. Now, if it be thy purpose to deliver him to torture and punishment, thou shalt do contrary to nature, and be no more called a father; and thou shalt lose thy son, willing, as he is, to lay down his life for Christ his sake. This, then, alone remaineth: to divide thy kingdom with him, and entrust him with the dominion of that part which falleth to his lot; and if the course of events, and the care of the business of life, draw him to embrace our aim and way, then the thing shall be according to our purpose; for habits, firmly established in the soul, are difficult to obliterate, and yield quicker to persuasion than to violence. But if he shall continue in the Christian religion, yet shall it be some solace to thee in thy distress, that thou hast not lost thy son." Thus spake Araches, and all bare witness that they welcomed his proposal. Therefore also the king agreed that this matter should thus be settled.

So at daybreak he called his son, and said unto him, "This is now my latest word with thee, my son. Unless thou be obedient thereto, and in this way heal my heart, know thou well, that I shall no longer spare thee." When his son enquired the meaning of his word, he said, "Since, after all my labours, I find thee in all points unyielding to the persuasion of my words, come now; I will divide with thee my kingdom, and make thee king over the half-part thereof; and thou shalt be free, from now, to go whatsoever way thou wilt without fear." He, though his saintly soul perceived that the king was casting yet another snare to trip his purpose, resolved to obey, in order that he might escape his hands, and take the journey that he desired. So he answered and said, "I have indeed been longing to go in quest of that man of God that pointed out to me the way of salvation, and, bidding farewell to everything, to pass the rest of my life in his company. But, father, since thou sufferest me not to fulfil my heart's desire, I will obey thee herein: for where there is no clear danger of perdition and estrangement from God, it is right to obey one's father."

The king was filled with exceeding great joy, and divided all the country under his sovranty into two parts, and appointed his son king, and adorned him with the diadem, and arrayed him in all the splendour of kingship, and sent him forth with a magnificent bodyguard into the kingdom set apart for him. And he bade his rulers and governors and satraps, everyone that would, to depart together with his son the king. And he set apart a mighty and populous city for his kingdom, and gave him everything that befitted a king. Thus then did Ioasaph receive the power of kingship; and when he had reached that city, where royal state had been prepared for him, on every tower of his city he set up the sign of his Lord's passion, the venerable Cross of Christ. And in person he besieged the idolatrous temples and altars, and razed them to the ground, and uncovered their foundations, leaving no trace of their ungodliness.

===Chinese===
至于王，事情既到了这个地步，他就全然惶惑失措，把自己深重的苦恼与心魂的纷乱明白显露出来。于是他又召集众元老，商议自己还有什么办法可以对付儿子。许多人提出了许多计谋，但我们先前说过的那位阿拉凯斯——职任中最负盛名、位居众谋臣之首的——对王进言说：「王啊，要诱导你的儿子遵从我们的教义、事奉我们的神，还有什么手段是我们没有用过的呢？但据我看来，我们所图的乃是不可能的事。他或出于天性，或出于际遇，生性好争、桀骜难驯。你若定意把他交与酷刑和刑罚，你就是行了违反天性的事，必不再称为父亲；你也必失去你的儿子，何况他是甘愿为基督的缘故舍命的。这样，剩下的惟有此策：把你的国与他分，把那分派给他一份的统治权交在他手里；若世事的推移、生计事务的操劳吸引他接受我们的目标与道路，事情就必照我们的心意成就；因为牢固植根于灵魂的习惯难以磨灭，它们顺服劝化，比顺服强暴更快。但他若仍旧持守基督徒的信仰，那么你虽在忧困之中，也总能得着一点安慰，就是你没有失去你的儿子。」阿拉凯斯说了这话，众人都作见证，表示乐从他的提议。于是王也赞同，这事就照这样定局。

于是到了破晓时分，他召了儿子来，对他说：「我儿，如今这是我对你的末后一言。你若不顺从，不借此医治我的心，你要清楚地知道，我必不再宽容你了。」儿子问他这话是什么意思，他就说：「我既经这一切劳苦，仍见你在凡事上不为我的言语所劝服，来吧，我要与你分我的国，立你作其中一半的王；从今以后，你愿意走哪条路，就可以走哪条路，毫无惧怕。」他圣洁的灵魂虽察觉王又张设另一个网罗，要绊跌他的志向，却定意顺服，好脱离父亲的手，成全他所切愿的行程。于是他回答说：「我实在一直渴望去寻找那位神人——就是他曾向我指明救恩之路的——好向万事道别，在他身旁度尽我余生。但父亲啊，你既不容我成全心中的愿望，这事我就顺服你：因为在没有明显的灭亡危险、也不致与神疏离的事上，顺服父亲乃是正当的。」

王充满了极大无比的喜乐，就把自己主权之下的全境分为两半，立他的儿子为王，为他戴上王冠，给他穿上君王威仪的一切华服，又派一支雄壮的卫队，送他前往那拨分给他的国。他又吩咐他的众统领、众官长与众总督，凡愿意的，都可以随他儿子王一同离去。他又划出一座强盛繁庶的城邑归他的国，把凡与君王相称的一切都赐给了他。约阿萨弗就这样领受了王权；他到了那座城——王家的仪制已在那里为他预备齐整——就在他城中每一座城楼上，竖起他主受难的记号，就是基督可敬的十字架。他又亲身围攻那些拜偶像的庙宇和祭坛，把它们夷为平地，刨出它们的根基，不留下它们不敬虔的一点痕迹。

===Original===
And in the middle of the city he upreared for Christ, his Lord, a temple mighty and passing fair, and he bade the people there often to resort thither, and offer their worship to God by the veneration of the Cross, himself standing in the midst in the presence of all, and earnestly giving himself unto prayer. And as many as were under his hand he admonished and exhorted, and did everything to tear them away from superstitious error, and to unite them to Christ; and he pointed out the deceits of idolatry, and proclaimed the preaching of the Gospel, and recounted the things concerning the condescension of God, the Word, and preached the marvels of his coming, and made known his sufferings on the Cross whereby we were saved, and the power of his Resurrection, and his Ascension into heaven. Moreover he declared the terrible day of his dreadful second coming, and the bliss laid up for the righteous, and the punishments awaiting sinners. All these truths he expounded with kindly mien and gentle words. For he was not minded to be reverenced and feared for the grandeur of his power and kingly magnificence, but rather for his humility and meekness. Hereby also he more easily drew all men unto himself, being verily marvellous in his acts, and equitable and modest in spirit. Wherefore his power, being strongly reinforced by his gentleness and equity, caused all men to yield themselves to his words.

What wonder, then, if, in a little while, all his subjects, in city or country, were so well initiated into his inspired teachings, that they renounced the errors of their many gods, and broke away from idolatrous drink-offerings and abominations, and were joined to the true faith and were created anew by his doctrine, and added to the household of Christ? And all, who for fear of Ioasaph's father had been shut up in mountains and dens, priests and monks, and some few bishops, came forth from their hiding places and resorted to him gladly. He himself would meet and receive with honour those who had fallen upon such tribulation and distress, for Christ his sake, and bring them to his own palace, washing their feet, and cleansing their matted hair, and ministering to them in every way. Then he dedicated his newly built church, and therein appointed for chief-priest one of the bishops that had suffered much, and had lost his own see, on account of his faith in Christ, an holy man, and learned in the canons of the Church, whose heart was fulfilled with heavenly zeal. And forthwith, when he had made ready a rude font,14 he bade baptize them that were turning to Christ. And so they were baptized, first the rulers and the men in authority; next, the soldiers on service and the rest of the multitude. And they that were baptized not only received health in their souls, but indeed as many as were afflicted with bodily ailments and imperfections cast off all their trouble, and came up from the holy font pure in soul, and sound in body, reaping an harvest of health for soul and body alike.

===Chinese===
他又在城的中央为他的主基督兴建了一座宏伟绝伦的圣殿，嘱咐百姓常到那里聚集，借着敬礼十字架向神献上敬拜，他自己则当着众人站在中间，恳切地专心祷告。凡在他手下的人，他都劝戒勉励，尽一切所能把他们从迷信的错谬里夺回，连于基督；他指明拜偶像的种种欺诈，宣扬福音的传讲，讲说神之道俯就下临的事迹，传讲他降世的种种奇事，又使人认识他十字架上的受难——我们正是借此得救——并他复活的大能和他升入高天。此外，他又宣告他那可畏的二次降临之恐怖大日，为义人存留的福乐，以及等候罪人的刑罚。这一切真理，他都以慈和的容色、温柔的言语来讲解。因为他不愿自己因权能的显赫和王威的壮丽受人敬畏，宁愿因自己的谦卑温柔受人敬畏。因此他也更容易把众人吸引到自己身边，因为他在作为上实在奇妙，在心性上公平而谦抑。所以他的权能既因他的温柔与公平大得巩固，就使众人都甘心顺服他的话。

这样，过了不多时，他的众属民，无论城中乡野，都如此完满地受教于他那受感的教训，以致弃绝了他们多神的错谬，脱离了拜偶像的奠祭和可憎之事，连于真信仰，借他的教训重作新造，加添归入基督的家——这又何足为奇呢？而一切因惧怕约阿萨弗的父亲而被禁闭在山岭洞窟中的人——众司祭与修士，还有少数几位主教——都从藏身之处出来，欢然投奔他。那些曾为主基督的缘故落在这样患难困苦中的人，他必亲自迎接，以尊荣接待，领他们进自己的宫中，为他们洗脚，为他们梳理结毡的头发，凡事服事他们。随后他祝圣了新建的教堂，在其中立一位主教为首席司祭——这人因信基督曾多受苦难，失去自己的主教座位，是位圣洁的人，精通教会的法规，心中满盈天上的热心。他又随即预备了一座粗糙的洗礼池 14，吩咐给凡归向基督的人施洗。于是他们都受了洗：先是众统领和掌权的人，其次是当值的兵丁和其余的群众。受洗的人不但灵魂得着了健康，而且凡身罹疾病残缺的，也都脱去了一切的病痛，从圣洗礼池中上来时，灵魂纯洁，身体康健，为灵魂和身体同样收获了健康的庄稼。

===Original===
Wherefore also from all quarters multitudes flocked to King Ioasaph, desirous to be instructed by him in godliness. And all idolatrous images were utterly demolished, and all their wealth and temple treasure was taken from them, and in their stead holy courts were built for God. For these King Ioasaph dedicated the riches and costly vestments and treasures of the idolatrous temples, thereby making this worthless and superfluous material fit for service, and profitable. And the foul fiends that dwelt in their altars and temples were rigorously chased away and put to flight; and these, in the hearing of many, loudly lamented the misfortune that had overtaken them. And all the region round about was freed from their dark deceit, and illuminated with the light of the blameless Christian faith.

And, soothly, the king was a good example to all; and he inflamed and kindled the hearts of many to be of the same mind with himself. For such is the nature of authority. Its subjects alway conform to its likeness, and are wont to love the same objects, and to practise the pursuits which they perceive to be pleasing to their governor. Hence, God helping, religion grew and increased amongst them. The king was wholly dependent on the commandments of Christ and on his love, being a steward of the word of grace, and pilot to the souls of many, bringing them to safe anchorage in the haven of God. For he knew that this, afore all things, is the work of a king, to teach men to fear God and keep righteousness. Thus did he, training himself to be king over his own passions, and, like a good pilot, keeping a firm hold of the helm of good government for his subjects. For this is the end of good kingship, to be king and lord over pleasure—which end also he achieved. Of the nobility of his ancestors, or the royal splendour around him, he was in no wise proud, knowing that we all have one common forefather, made of clay, and that, whether rich or poor, we are all of the same moulding. He ever abased his soul in deepest humility, and thought on the blessedness of the world to come, and considered himself a stranger and pilgrim in this world, but realised that that was his real treasure which he should win after his departure hence. Now, since all went well with him, and since he had delivered all the people from their ancient and ancestral error, and made them servants of him who redeemed us from evil servitude by his own precious blood, he turned his thoughts to his next task, the virtue of almsgiving. Temperance and righteousness he had already attained; he wore on his brow the crown of temperance, and wrapped about him the purple of righteousness. He called to mind the uncertainty of earthly riches, how they resemble the running of river waters. Therefore made he haste to lay up his treasure where neither "moth nor rust doth corrupt and where thieves do not break through nor steal." So he began to distribute all his money to the poor, sparing naught thereof. He knew that the possessor of great authority is bound to imitate the giver of that authority, according to his ability; and herein he shall best imitate God, if he hold nothing in higher honour than mercy. Before all gold and precious stone he stored up for himself the treasure of almsgiving; treasure, which here gladdeneth the heart by the hope of enjoyment to come, and there delighteth it with the taste of the hoped-for bliss. After this he searched the prisons, and sought out the captives in mines, or debtors in the grip of their creditors; and by generous largesses to all he proved a father to all, orphans, and widows, and beggars, a loving and good father, for he deemed that by bestowing blessings on these he won a blessing for himself. Being endowed with spiritual riches, and, in sooth, a perfect king, he gave liberally to all that were in need, for he hoped to receive infinitely more, when the time should come for the recompense of his works.

===Chinese===
因此，群众又从四面八方蜂拥来投约阿萨弗王，渴望在他手下受教，学习敬虔。一切供奉偶像的神像尽都被拆毁，它们一切的财富和庙中之宝都被夺去，在原处为神建造了圣洁的殿庭。为建造这些殿宇，约阿萨弗王把拜偶像庙宇中的资财、华贵的祭衣和珍宝都奉献了出来，借此使这无用而多余的材料变为合用、变为有益。那些住在祭坛和庙宇中的污秽鬼魔，都被严厉地驱逐赶散；它们在许多人的耳中大声哀恸自己遭逢的不幸。于是四围全境都脱离了它们黑暗的欺骗，蒙无瑕疵的基督信仰之光照亮。

实在，这位王是众人极好的榜样；他点燃、挑旺了许多人的心，使他们与他存同样的心意。因为权柄的性质正是如此：属下总变得与其形像相似，惯于爱慕相同的对象，并操练那些他们察觉为治理者所喜悦的事务。这样，在神的帮助下，信仰就在他们中间增长、繁盛。王一心依循基督的诫命和他的爱，作恩典之道的管家，作许多人灵魂的舵手，领他们平安下锚，泊于神的港湾。因为他知道，先于万事，这就是王的职分：教训人敬畏神，谨守公义。他就是这样操练自己作自己情欲的王，又像一位好舵手，为属民牢牢握住善治之舵。因为善王之道的目的，乃在作快乐的王和主——这目的他也达到了。对于祖先的贵胄门第，或环绕身边的王室华彩，他绝不因此骄傲，因为他知道我们众人同有一位始祖，是泥土作的；无论贫富，我们都是同一模子里的塑造。他总在最深的谦卑里自卑己魂，思念来世的福乐，看自己在这世上是客旅、是寄居的，却明白自己真正的财宝，是他离开此地之后所要赢取的。如今他既诸事顺遂，又已拯救众民脱离他们古远祖传的错谬，使他们作了那用自己的宝血救赎我们脱离邪恶奴役之主的仆人，就将心思转向下一件要工——施舍之德。节制与公义，他既已达成；他额上戴着节制的冠冕，身上披着公义的紫袍。他想起地上财富的无定，想起它们何等像河水的奔流，因此急忙把财宝积攒在那里——在那里，「没有虫子咬，不能锈坏，也没有贼挖窟窿来偷」。于是他开始把他一切的钱财分给穷人，无所吝惜。他知道，大权柄的持有者，理当按自己的力量效法那赐权柄的主；而他若不把任何事物看得比怜悯更尊贵，就在这事上最完美地效法了神。他在一切黄金宝石之先，为自己积存了施舍的财宝——这财宝，今世以将来享受的盼望使心得乐，来世以所望福祉的滋味使心得悦。此后他巡查各监狱，寻出矿坑中的囚徒，或落在债主手中的欠债人；又以慷慨的厚赐向众人证实自己是众人的父亲——孤儿、寡妇、乞丐的慈爱良善之父，因为他认为，把福分赐给这些人，就是为自己赢得了福分。他既富有属灵的财宝，实在是一位完全的王，就慷慨施给一切有缺乏的人，因为他盼望到了他的作为得着报偿之时，必要领受无限更多。

===Original===
Now, in little while, the fame of Ioasaph was blazoned abroad; and led, as it were by the scent of sweet ointment, all men flocked to him daily, casting off their poverty of soul and body: and his name was on every man's lips. It was not fear and oppression that drew the people to him, but desire and heartfelt love, which by God's blessing and the king's fair life had been planted in their hearts. Then, too, did his father's subjects begin to come to him, and, laying aside all error, received the Gospel of truth. And the house of Ioasaph grew and waxed strong, but the house of Abenner waned and grew weak, even as the Book of the Kings declareth concerning David and Saul.

===Chinese===
不久，约阿萨弗的名声就传扬开来；众人仿佛被香膏的芬芳所引，天天成群来投奔他，脱去灵魂与身体的贫穷；众人的口上都提说他的名。吸引众人归向他的，不是恐惧与压迫，乃是切慕与发自肺腑的爱——这爱是借着神的赐福和这王美好的生命，栽种在他们心里的。那时，他父亲的属民也开始来归向他，放下一切的错谬，领受了真理的福音。约阿萨弗的家日见增长强盛，阿本内尔的家却日见衰微软弱，正如《列王纪》论到大卫与扫罗所宣告的。

## XXXIV / 三十四

===Original===
When king Abenner saw this, though late and loth, he came to his senses, and renounced his false gods with all their impotence and vain deceit. Again he called an assembly of his chief counsellors, and brought to light the thoughts of his heart. As they confirmed his words (for the dayspring from on high had visited them, the Saviour who had heard the prayer of his servant Ioasaph), it pleased the king to signify the same to his son. Therefore on the morrow he wrote a letter to Ioasaph, running thus:

> “King Abenner to his well-beloved son Ioasaph, greeting. Dearest son, many thoughts have been stealing into my soul, and rule it with a rod of iron. I see our state vanishing, like as smoke vanisheth, but thy religion shining brighter than the sun; and I have come to my senses, and know that the words which thou hast ever spoken unto me are true, and that a thick cloud of sin and wickedness did then cover us, so that we were unable to discern the truth, and recognize the Creator of all. Nay, but we shut our eyes, and would not behold the light which thou didst enkindle more brightly for us. Much evil did we do unto thee, and many of the Christians, alas! did we destroy; who, strengthened by the power that aided them, finally triumphed over our cruelty. But now we have removed that dense mist from our eyes, and see some small ray of truth, and there cometh on us repentance of our misdeeds. But a new cloud of despair would overshadow it; despair at the multitude of mine offences, because I am now abominable and unacceptable to Christ, being a rebel and a foeman unto him. What, then, sayest thou, dearest son, hereto? Make known to me thine answer, and teach me that am thy father what I should do, and lead me to the knowledge of my true weal.”

===Chinese===
阿本内尔王看见这事，虽已迟晚，虽出于不甘，终究醒悟过来，弃绝了他那些假神，并它们一切的无能与虚妄的欺哄。他又召集众首席谋臣开会议事，将心中的意念陈明出来。他们既都附和他的话（因为清晨的日光从高天临到了他们，就是那位已垂听他仆人约阿萨弗祷告的救主），王便乐意将这心意晓谕他的儿子。于是次日他写了一封信给约阿萨弗，信上说：

> 「阿本内尔王致至爱的儿子约阿萨弗，问安。极亲爱的儿子啊，许多意念悄悄潜入我的灵魂，用铁杖辖管它。我看见我们的景况如烟消散，你的信仰却照耀得比太阳更亮；我已醒悟过来，知道你历来对我所说的话都是真实的；又知道那时有一层罪恶的厚云遮盖我们，使我们不能分辨真理，不能认识万有的创造主。非但如此，我们还闭上了眼睛，不肯看你为我们点得更亮的那光。我们向你行了许多恶，又毁灭了许多基督徒——哀哉！他们却蒙那帮助他们的能力所坚固，终久胜过了我们的残暴。但如今我们已把那浓厚的迷雾从眼前除去，得见真理的一线微光，痛悔己行的恶事之心也临到我们。但有一朵绝望的新云又要把这悔悟遮蔽；是为着我过犯的众多而绝望，因为我如今在基督面前可憎可弃，我作了他的叛徒，是他的仇敌。极亲爱的儿子啊，对此你有何话说？求你把你的答复指示我，教训我这作你父亲的当怎样行，领我认识我真正的福祉。」

===Original===
When Ioasaph had received this letter, and read the words therein, his soul was filled with mingled joy and amazement. Forthwith he entered his closet, and falling on his face before the image of his Master, watered the ground with his tears, giving thanks to his Lord and confessing him, and tuning lips of exultation to sing an hymn of praise, saying:

“I will magnify thee, O God, my King, and I will praise thy name forever and ever. Great art thou O Lord, and marvellous-worthy to be praised, and of thy greatness there is no end. Who can express thy noble acts, or show forth all thy praise, who hast turned the hard rock into a standing water and the flint-stone into a springing well? For behold this my father’s flinty and more than granite heart is at thy will melted as wax; because thou art able of these stones to raise up children unto Abraham. I thank thee, Lord, thou lover of men, and God of pity, that thou hast been, and art, long-suffering towards our offences, and hast suffered us until now to go unpunished. Long have we deserved to be cast away from thy face, and made a byword on earth, as were the sinful inhabiters of the five cities, consumed with fire and brimstone; but thy marvellous long-suffering hath dealt graciously with us. I give thanks unto thee, vile and unworthy though I be, and insufficient of myself to glorify thy greatness. And, by thine infinite compassions, I pray thee, Lord Jesu Christ, Son and Word of the invisible Father, who madest all things by thy word, and sustainest them by thy will; who hast delivered us thine unworthy servants from the bondage of the archfiend our foe: thou that wast stretched upon the Rood, and didst bind the strong man, and award everlasting freedom to them that lay bound in his fetters: do thou now also stretch forth thine invisible and almighty hand, and, at the last, free thy servant my father from that cruel bondage of the devil. Show him full clearly that thou art the ever living true God, and only King, eternal and immortal. Behold, O Lord, with favourable and kindly eye, the contrition of my heart; and, according to thine unerring promise, be with me that acknowledge and confess thee the Maker and protector of all creation. Let there be a well of water within me springing up, and let utterance be given unto me that I may open my mouth, and a mind well fixed in thee, the chief cornerstone, that I, thine unprofitable servant, may be enabled to preach to my father, as is right, the mystery of thine Incarnation, and by thy power deliver him from the vain deceit of wicked devils, and bring him unto thee his God and Lord, who willest not the death of us sinners, but waitest for us to return and repent, because thou art glorified forever and ever. Amen.”

===Chinese===
约阿萨弗接了这信，读了其中的言语，他的魂就充满喜乐与惊奇交织之情。他随即进了内室，在他主的圣像面前俯伏于地，用眼泪浇湿了地面，向他主献上感谢、认他称颂他，又调正欢呼的嘴唇，唱出赞美的诗歌，说：

「神我的王啊，我要尊崇你，我要永永远远称颂你的名。主啊，你本为大，配受奇妙的赞美，你的伟大没有穷尽。你既使坚硬的磐石变为水池，使火石变为泉源，谁能传说你尊贵的作为？谁能述尽你的赞美呢？看哪，我父亲那火石般、比花岗岩更坚的心，已照你的旨意如蜡熔化，因为你能从这些石头中给亚伯拉罕兴起子孙来。主啊，爱世人的主、怜悯人的神，我感谢你，因你曾经、如今仍是长久忍耐我们的过犯，又容让我们直到今日未受刑罚。我们久已配得从你面前被弃绝，在地上成为笑谈，如那五城犯罪的居民被火与硫磺所灭一般；但你奇妙的恒久忍耐，却恩待了我们。我虽卑贱不配，靠自己也不足以荣耀你的伟大，我仍要感谢你。主耶稣基督啊，无形之父的儿子与道，你曾凭你的话创造万有，凭你的旨意托住万有；你曾救我们这些不配的仆人，脱离我们仇敌那魔王的奴役；你曾受钉伸展于十字架上，捆绑了那壮士，又将永远的自由判给那些躺卧在他锁链中的人：求你如今也伸出你无形而全能的手，至终救你的仆人我父亲，脱离魔鬼那残忍的奴役。向他显明得全然清楚：你是那永远活着的真神，是独一的王，永在不朽。主啊，求你以恩惠慈爱的眼目，垂顾我心中的痛悔；照你无误的应许，与我这承认你、认你作万有的创造者与保护者的人同在。愿我里面有泉源涌出水来，赐我口才，使我得以开口；赐我心志坚定于你——那房角的头块石头——使我这无用的仆人，得以照所当行的向我父亲传讲你道成肉身的奥秘，靠你的能力救他脱离邪恶魔鬼的虚妄欺哄，领他到你——他的神、他的主——面前；你不愿我们罪人死亡，只等候我们回转悔改，因为你要世世无穷、永远受荣耀。阿们。」

===Original===
When he had thus prayed, and received fullness of assurance that he should not miscarry in his desire, he took courage by the tender mercy of Christ, and arose thence, with his royal bodyguard, and arrived at his father’s palace. When it was told unto his father, “Thy son is come,” he went forth straightway for to meet him, and embraced and kissed him lovingly, and made exceeding great joy, and held a general feast in honour of the coming of his son. And afterward, they two were closeted together.

But how tell of all that the son spake with his father, and of all the wisdom of his speech? And what was that speech but the words put into his mouth by the Holy Ghost, by whom the fishermen enclosed the whole world in their nets for Christ and the unlearned are found wiser than the wise. This Holy Spirit’s grace and wisdom taught Ioasaph to speak with the king his father, enlightening him with the light of knowledge. Before now he had bestowed much labour to drag his father from superstitious error, leaving nothing unsaid and nothing undone to win him over, but he seemed to be twanging on a broken string, and speaking to deaf ears. But when the Lord looked upon the lowliness of his servant Ioasaph, and, in answer to his prayer, opened the closed gates of his father’s heart (for it is said, he will fulfil the desire of them that fear him, and will hear their cry), then the king easily understood the things that were spoken; so that, when a convenient season came, through the grace of Christ, this son triumphed over those evil spirits that had lorded it over the soul of his father, and clean freed him from their error, and made the word of salvation clearly known unto him, and joined him to the living God on high.

===Chinese===
他这样祷告了，又得了满有确据的把握，知道他的心愿必不至落空，就靠基督的温柔怜悯壮起胆来，从那里起身，带着王的卫队，来到他父亲的宫殿。有人禀报他父亲说：「你儿子来了。」他便立刻出去迎接，与他亲嘴拥抱，满怀深情，欢喜极大，又设摆盛大的筵席，庆贺儿子的来临。此后，他们二人便密室独处，共相谈论。

然而，儿子与父亲所谈的一切，他言谈中的一切智慧，怎能述说得尽呢？他的话语，岂不正是圣灵放在他口中的言语么——渔夫们正是靠着这位圣灵，为基督把全世界圈在他们的网里，叫没有学问的显为比有智慧的更有智慧。正是这圣灵的恩典和智慧，教导约阿萨弗与王他父亲说话，用知识的光光照他。在此以前，他曾下过许多劳苦，要把父亲从迷信的错谬中拉出来；为要得着他，凡当说的没有一句不说，凡当行的没有一样不行；然而他宛如拨动一根断弦，宛如对聋耳说话。及至主垂顾他仆人约阿萨弗的卑微，照他所求的应允他，打开了他父亲心中关闭的门（因为经上说：他必成就敬畏他之人的心愿，也必听他们的呼求），王就容易明白所讲论的事；及至合宜的时机来到，这儿子就借着基督的恩典，胜过了那些曾在他父亲灵魂上称王管辖的恶灵，把他从他们的错谬中全然释放，使救恩之道清楚显明于他，并将他连于在高天永活的神。

===Original===
Ioasaph took up his tale from the beginning, and expounded to his father great and marvellous things which he knew not, which he had never heard with the ears of his heart; and he told him many weighty sayings concerning God, and showed him righteousness: to wit that there is no other God in heaven above, nor in the earth beneath, except the one God, revealed in the Father, the Son, and the Holy Ghost. And he made known unto him many mysteries of divine knowledge; and amongst them he told him the history of creation, visible and invisible, how the Creator brought everything out of nothing, and how he formed man after his own image and likeness and endowed him with power of free will, and gave him Paradise to his enjoyment, charging him only to abstain from one thing, the tree of knowledge; and how, when man had broken his commandment, he banished him out of Paradise; and how man, fallen from union with God, stumbled into these manifold errors, becoming the slave of sins, and subject unto death through the tyranny of the devil, who, having once taken men captive, hath made them utterly forget their Lord and God, and hath persuaded them to serve him instead, by the abominable worshipping of idols. So our Maker, moved with compassion, through the goodwill of the Father, and the cooperation of the Holy Ghost, was pleased, for our sakes, to be born of an holy Virgin, Mary, the mother of God, and he, that cannot suffer, was acquainted with sufferings. On the third day he rose again from the dead, and redeemed us from our first penalty, and restored to us our first glory. When he ascended into the heavens, from whence he had descended, he raised us up together with him; and thence, we believe that he shall come again, to raise up his own handiwork; and he will recompense every man according to his works. Moreover Ioasaph instructed his father concerning the kingdom of heaven that awaiteth them that are worthy thereof, and the joy unspeakable. Thereto he added the torment in store for the wicked, the unquenchable fire, the outer darkness, the undying worm and whatsoever other punishment the servants of sin have laid up in store for themselves.

All these things set he forth in many words, which bore witness that the grace of the Spirit was dwelling richly within him. Then he described the uncharted sea of the love of God towards mankind, and how he is ready to accept the repentance of them that turn to him; and how there is no sin too great for his tender mercy, if we will but repent. And when he had confirmed these truths by many an example, and testimony of Scripture, he made an end of speaking.

===Chinese===
约阿萨弗便从头讲起，向父亲讲解了许多又大又奇妙的事，是他所不知道、心中的耳朵从未听过的；又将许多郑重关乎神的话告诉他，把公义指示他，就是说：在天上，在地上，除了在父、子、圣灵里显明的那独一的神以外，再无别神。他又使他知道神圣知识的许多奥秘；其中，他讲述了看得见与看不见之万有的受造历史：创造主怎样从无有中造出一切；怎样照自己的形像、按自己的样式造人，赋他自由意志的能力，将乐园赐给他享受，只吩咐他一件事须禁戒，就是分别善恶树；人违了诫命之后，他怎样将他逐出乐园；人既从与神的联合中坠落，又怎样失脚陷入这诸般的错谬，作了罪的奴仆，在魔鬼的暴虐辖制下服于死亡——那曾一次掳掠了人的，就叫他们全然忘记他们的主、他们的神，又劝服他们转去事奉他，就是借着那可憎的敬拜偶像之礼。于是我们的造物主动了怜悯，借着父的美意与圣灵的同工，乐意为我们从圣童女、神之母马利亚而生；那不能受苦的，也亲历了苦难。第三天，他从死里复活，救赎我们脱离那起初的刑罚，恢复了我们起初的荣耀。他升上诸天——他原是从那里降下的——又叫我们与他一同升上高天；我们信他必再从那里来，叫他自己手中的工作复活过来；他要照各人所行的报应各人。此外，约阿萨弗又教训他的父亲，论到那为配得之人存留的天国，和那说不出来的喜乐；又加上那为恶人存留的刑罚：不灭的火，外边的黑暗，不死的虫，并罪的仆人们为自己积存的一切别的刑罚。

这一切事，他用许多话一一陈明；这些话见证圣灵的恩典丰盛地住在他里面。随后他描绘神对世人的爱——那未经测绘过的大海；描绘他怎样随时乐于接纳归向他之人的悔改；只要我们肯悔改，在他温柔的怜悯面前，就没有甚么罪大到不能赦免。他用许多例证和圣经的见证坚立了这些真理之后，就结束了讲论。

## XXXV / 三十五

===Original===
King Abenner was pricked to the heart by this inspired wisdom and with loud voice and fervent heart confessed Christ his Saviour, and forthwith forsook all superstitious error. He venerated the sign of the life-giving Cross in the sight of all and, in the hearing of all, proclaimed our Lord Jesus Christ to be God. By telling in full the tale of his former ungodliness, and of his own cruelty and bloodthirstiness toward the Christians, he proved himself a great power for religion. So here was proved in fact, the saying of Paul; that where sin abounded, there did grace much more abound.

While then the learned Ioasaph was speaking of God, and of piety towards him, to the dukes and satraps and all the people there assembled, and was as it were with a tongue of fire piping unto them a goodly ode, the grace of the Holy Spirit descended upon them, and moved them to give glory to God, so that all the multitude cried aloud with one voice, “Great is the God of the Christians, and there is none other God but our Lord Jesus Christ, who, together with the Father and Holy Ghost, is glorified.”

Waxen full of heavenly zeal, King Abenner made a sturdy assault on the idols, wrought of silver and gold, that were within his palace, and tore them down to the ground. Then he brake them into small pieces, and distributed them to the poor, thus making that which had been useless useful. Furthermore he and his son besieged the idols’ temples and altars and levelled them even to the ground, and in their stead, and to the honour of God, built holy courts. And not only in the city but throughout all the country also, thus did they in their zeal. And the evil spirits that dwelt in those altars were driven forth with shrieks, and cried out in terror at the invincible power of our God. And all the region round about, and the greater part of the neighbour nations, were led, as by the hand, to the true Faith. Then came the holy Bishop, of whom we have spoken, and King Abenner was instructed, and made perfect with Holy Baptism, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost. And Ioasaph received him as he came up from the Holy Font, in this strange way appearing as the begetter of his own father, and proving the spiritual father to him that begat him in the flesh: for he was the son of his heavenly Father, and verily divine fruit of that divine Branch, which saith, “I am the vine, ye are the branches.”

===Chinese===
阿本内尔王因这受感的智慧觉得扎心，就高声、以火热的心承认基督是他的救主，随即离弃了一切迷信的错谬。他当着众人的面敬礼那赐生命的十字架的记号，又在众人耳中宣扬我们的主耶稣基督是神。他将自己从前的不敬虔，并自己向基督徒所行的残暴与嗜血，原原本本述说出来，就此证明自己是信仰的一大助力。这样，保罗的话便在此得了事实的印证：「只是罪在哪里显多，恩典就更显多了。」

当时，有学问的约阿萨弗正向聚集在那里的众公卿、众总督和全体百姓讲论神，并讲论向神所当存的敬虔，仿佛以火舌向他们吹奏一首美妙的乐歌；圣灵的恩典就降在他们身上，感动他们归荣耀给神，以致全体会众同声高呼：「大哉，基督徒的神！除我们的主耶稣基督以外，再无别神；他与父与圣灵同得荣耀。」

阿本内尔王满心属天的热心，向宫中那些金银铸成的偶像发起猛攻，将它们推倒在地，又打成碎片，分给穷人，使那本来无用的变为有用。此外，他和儿子又围攻偶像的庙宇和祭坛，把它们夷为平地，并在原处、为荣耀神的缘故建造圣洁的殿庭。不但在城中，就是通国之内，他们都因这热心如此而行。那些住在这些祭坛里的邪灵，都尖啸着被驱逐出去，因我们神无敌的大能而惊恐呼号。于是四围全境，并邻邦的大半，都好像被手牵引着一般，被领进真信仰。随后，我们先前说过的那位圣洁主教来了，阿本内尔王受了教训，奉父、子、圣灵的名，借圣洗礼得以完全。约阿萨弗在他从圣洗礼池中上来的时候迎接他，以这奇特的方式作了自己父亲的生父，又向那按肉身生他的人显为属灵之父：因为他是他天上之父的儿子，实在是那神圣枝子所结的神圣果子——那枝子曾说：「我是葡萄树，你们是枝子。」

===Original===
Thus King Abenner, being born again of water and of the spirit, rejoiced with joy unspeakable, and with him all the city and the region round about received Holy Baptism, and they that were before darkness now became children of light. And every disease, and every assault of evil spirits was driven far from the believers, and all were sane and sound in body and in soul. And many other miracles were wrought for the confirmation of the Faith. Churches too were built, and the bishops, that had been hiding for fear, discovered themselves, and received again their own churches, whilst others were chosen from the priests and monks, to shepherd the flock of Christ. But King Abenner, having thus forsaken his former disgraceful life, and repented of his evil deeds, handed over to his son the rule of all his kingdom. He himself dwelt in solitude, continually casting dust on his head, and groaning for very heaviness, and watering his face with his tears, being alone, communing with him who is everywhere present and imploring him to forgive his sins. And he abased himself to such a depth of contrition and humility, that he refused to name the name of God with his own lips, and was scarce brought by his son’s admonitions to make so bold. Thus the king passed through the good change and entered the road that leadeth to virtue, so that his righteousness now surpassed his former sins of ignorance. For four years did he live thus in repentance and tears and virtuous acts, and then fell into the sickness whereof he died. But when the end drew nigh, he began to fear and to be dismayed, calling to remembrance the evil that he had wrought. But with comfortable words Ioasaph sought to ease the distress that had fallen on him, saying, “Why art thou so full of heaviness, O my father, and why art thou so disquieted within thee? Set thy hope on God, and give him thanks, who is the hope of all the ends of the earth, and of them that remain in the sea afar, who crieth by the mouth of his prophet, ‘Wash you, make you clean: put away from before mine eyes the wickedness of your souls; learn to do well’; and ‘Though your sins be as scarlet, I will make them white as snow; though they be red like crimson, I will make them as wool.’ Fear not, therefore, O my father, neither be of doubtful mind: for the sins of them that turn to God prevail not against his infinite goodness. For these, however many, are subject to measure and number: but measure and number cannot limit his goodness. It is impossible then for that which is subject to measure to exceed the unmeasurable.”

With such comfortable words did Ioasaph cheer his soul, and bring him to a good courage. Then his father stretched out his hands, and gave him thanks and prayed for him, blessing the day whereon Ioasaph was born, and said “Dearest child, yet not child of me, but of mine heavenly Father, with what gratitude can I repay thee? With what words of blessings may I bless thee? What thanks shall I offer God for thee? I was lost, and was found through thee: I was dead in sin and am alive again: an enemy, and rebel against God, and am reconciled with him. What reward therefore shall I give thee for all these benefits? God is he that shall make the due recompense.” Thus saying, he pressed many kisses on his beloved son; then, when he had prayed, and said, “Into thy hands, O God, thou lover of men, do I commit my spirit,” he committed his soul unto the Lord in penitence and peace.

===Chinese===
这样，阿本内尔王既从水和圣灵重生，就满了说不出来的喜乐；与他一同，全城并四围全境的人都领受了圣洗礼，那从前是黑暗的，如今成了光明之子。各样的疾病、各样邪灵的攻击，都远远离开信的人，众人在身体和灵魂上都健全康泰。为坚立信仰，又行了许多别的神迹。教堂也建造起来，那些曾因惧怕而藏匿的主教都显露出身，收回了自己的教堂；又有人从众司祭和修士中间被选立，牧养基督的羊群。但阿本内尔王既这样丢弃了他从前可耻的生活，为他的恶行悔改，就把通国的王权交给儿子执掌。他自己独居幽处，不住地将尘土撒在头上，因深重的忧闷而呻吟，以眼泪浇洗自己的脸，独自与那无所不在者相交，恳求他赦免自己的罪。他自卑到如此深重的痛悔与谦卑，甚至不肯亲口提说神的名，直到经儿子劝勉，才勉强放胆如此。这样，王经过了这美好的转变，进入了那引向美德的路，以致他如今的义，超过了他从前因无知所犯的罪。他就这样在悔改、眼泪和善行中度过了四年，随后患上了那致死之病。及至末期临近，他开始惧怕、惊惶，想起自己所行的恶。约阿萨弗便用安慰的话，设法舒缓落在他身上的忧苦，说：「我父啊，你为何这般忧闷？为何在你里面烦躁？应当仰望神，称谢他；他是一切地极之人、并远在海上之人所倚靠的；他曾借他先知的口呼喊说：『你们要洗濯、自洁，从我眼前除掉你们灵魂的恶行，学习行善』；又说：『你们的罪虽像朱红，必变成雪白；虽红如丹颜，必白如羊毛。』所以我父啊，不要惧怕，也不要存疑惑的心：因为凡转向神之人的罪，都胜不过他那无限的良善。这些罪无论多少，总服在度量与数目之下；度量与数目却不能限制他的良善。这样，服在度量之下的，断不能胜过那无法度量的。」

约阿萨弗用这样的安慰话使他的心畅快，使他刚强壮胆。于是他父亲伸出双手，为他感谢，为他祷告，又为约阿萨弗出生的那日祝福，说：「最亲爱的孩子啊——实在说，你不是我的孩子，乃是我天上之父的孩子——我能以怎样的感谢报答你呢？我能用怎样的祝福话为你祝福呢？为你的缘故，我又该向神献上怎样的感谢呢？我失丧了，却借着你得以寻回；我死在罪中，如今又活了过来；我本是神的仇敌和悖逆者，如今与他复和了。这一切厚恩，我要拿什么报答你呢？惟有神必按所当得的报偿。」说罢，他与他亲爱的儿子连连亲嘴；随后他祷告了，说：「神啊，爱世人的主，我将我的灵魂交在你手里」，就在悔改与平安中，将灵魂交付了主。

===Original===
Now, when Ioasaph had honoured with his tears his father that was dead, and had reverently cared for his body, he buried him in a sepulchre wherein devout men lay; not indeed clad in royal raiment, but robed in the garment of penitence. Standing on the sepulchre, and lifting up his hands to heaven, the tears streaming in floods from his eyes, he cried aloud unto God saying,

“O God, I thank thee, King of glory, alone mighty and immortal, that thou hast not despised my petition, and hast not held thy peace at my tears, but hast been pleased to turn this thy servant, my father, from the way of wickedness, and to draw him to thyself, the Saviour of all, departing him from the deceitfulness of idolatry, and granting him to acknowledge thee, who art the very God and lover of souls. And now, O my Lord and God, whose ocean of goodness is uncharted, set him in that place where much grass is, in a place of refreshment, where shineth the light of thy countenance. Remember not his old offences; but, according to the multitude of thy mercies, blot out the handwriting of his sins, and destroy the tablets of his debts, and set him at peace with thy Saints whom he slew with fire and sword. Charge them not to be bitter against him. For all things are possible with thee, the Lord of all, save only to withhold pity from them that turn not unto thee; this is impossible. For thy pity is poured out upon all men, and thou savest them that call upon thee, Lord Jesu Christ, because glory becometh thee forever and ever. Amen.”

Such were the prayers and intercessions that he made unto God, by the space of seven full days, never leaving the grave, and never thinking of meat or drink, and taking no refreshment of sleep: but he watered the ground with his tears, and continued praying and moaning unceasingly. But, on the eighth day, he went back to his palace and distributed amongst the poor all his wealth and riches, so that not one person was left in want.

===Chinese===
约阿萨弗以眼泪敬奉他亡故的父亲，又恭敬地照料他的遗体，把他安葬在敬虔人安息的坟墓里；他下葬时并非身穿王服，乃是披着悔改的衣袍。约阿萨弗站在坟墓上，向天举起双手，热泪从眼中如洪水奔流，大声向神呼喊说：

「神啊，我感谢你，荣耀的王，独有权能、独一不死的主，因你没有藐视我的恳求，没有对我的眼泪缄默；你竟乐意使你这仆人、我的父亲，从邪恶的路上转回，领他归向你自己——万人的救主，救他脱离拜偶像的欺瞒，又施恩使他认识你，就是认识你这位真神、眷爱灵魂的主。主我的神啊，你的良善是未经测绘的大洋：求你把他安置在青草丰盛之处，安置在可安歇之地，就是你面上的光所照耀之处。求你不记念他旧日的过犯，乃要照你丰盛的慈悲，涂抹他罪债的字据，毁掉他债项的契版，使他与你的众圣徒——就是他曾用火与刀杀戮的——安然和好；又嘱咐他们，不要向他怀怨。因为万有之主啊，在你凡事都能，惟独向那不转向你的人收回怜恤，这是你所不能的。因为你的怜恤倾倒在众人身上，你拯救凡求告你的人，主耶稣基督啊，因为荣耀理当归你，直到永永远远。阿们。」

他这样向神献上祷告与代求，有整整七日之久：他从不离开坟墓，绝不想念饮食，也不取睡眠的舒畅，只以眼泪浇灌地面，不住地祷告呻吟。到了第八日，他回到自己的宫中，将他一切的财富资财分给穷人，以致没有一个人仍有缺乏。

## XXXVI / 三十六

===Original===
In a few days, after he had ended this ministry, and emptied all his coffers, in order that the burden of his money might not hinder him from entering in at the narrow gate, on the fortieth day after his father’s decease, and in remembrance of him, he called together all his officers, and those who wore soldiers’ attire, and of the citizens not a few. Sitting in the front, according to custom, in the audience of all he said, “Lo, as ye see, Abenner, my father the king, hath died like any beggar. Neither wealth nor kingly glory, nor I his loving son, nor any of his kith and kindred, has availed to help him, or to save him from the sentence without reprieve. But he is gone to yonder judgement seat, to give account of his life in this world, carrying with him no advocate whatsoever, except his deeds, good or bad. And the same law is ordained by nature for every man born of woman, and there is no escape. Now, therefore, hearken unto me, friends and brethren, people and holy heritage of the Lord, whom Christ our God hath purchased with his own precious blood, and delivered from the ancient error, and bondage of the adversary. Ye yourselves know my manner of life among you; that ever since I knew Christ, and was counted worthy to become his servant, I have hated all things, and loved him only, and how this was my desire, to escape from the tempest and vain tumult of the world, and commune alone with him, and in undisturbed peace of soul serve my God and Master. But my father’s opposition held me back, and the command that biddeth us to honour our fathers. So, by the grace and help of God, I have not laboured in vain, nor spent these days for naught, I have brought my father nigh to Christ, and have taught you all to know the one true God, the Lord of all; and yet not I, but the grace of God which was with me, which rescued me also from superstitious error, and from the worship of idols, and freed you, O my people, from cruel captivity. So now it is high time to fulfil the service that I promised to God; high time to depart thitherward, where he himself shall lead me, where I may perform my vows which I made unto him. Now, therefore, look you out a man whom ye will, to be your leader and king; for by this time ye have been conformed to the will of the Lord, and of his commandments nothing hath been hidden from you. Walk ye therein; turn not aside, neither to the right hand, nor to the left, and the God of peace be with you all!”

When all that company and the common people heard thereof, anon there arose a clamour, an uproar, and a mighty cry and confusion, all weeping like orphans and bewailing their loss. Lamenting bitterly, they protested with oaths and with tears, that they would never let him go, but would restrain him and not suffer in any wise his departure. While the common people, and they in authority, were thus crying aloud, the king broke in, and beckoned with his hand to the multitude and charged them to keep silence. He declared that he gave in to their instancy, and dismissed them still grieving, and bearing on their cheeks the signs of sorrow. And Ioasaph did thus. There was one of the senators first in favour with Ioasaph, a man honoured for his godliness and dignity, Barachias by name, who, as hath been already told, when Nachor, feigning to be Barlaam, was disputing with the philosophers, alone was ready to stand by Nachor and fight for him, for his heart was fired with heavenly love. Him the king took apart, and spake gently with him, and earnestly besought him to receive the kingdom, and, in the fear of God, to shepherd his people; in order that he himself might take the journey that he desired.

===Chinese===
过了不多的日子，他结束了这项职事，又倾尽一切的府库，免得钱财的重担拦阻他进入窄门；在父亲去世后第四十日，为着记念他的缘故，他召集了众官员、一切身穿军装的人，并不少城中的百姓。他照惯例坐在前面，当着众人开口说：「看哪，如你们所见，我父阿本内尔王死了，竟像任何一个乞丐一样死了。财富没有能帮助他，王者的荣耀没有，我这爱他的儿子没有，他的一切亲族密友也没有一个能，没有一个能救他脱离那无可缓赦的判决。他已往那边的审判座去了，要交代他在这世上的生平，随身没有带任何代辩者，只带着他的行为，或善或恶。本性为凡妇人所生的人，都定了这同样的律，无人能逃脱。所以现在，我的朋友们、我的弟兄们、主的子民、主圣洁的产业哪——基督我们的神已经用他自己的宝血把你们买了来，救你们脱离了那古久的错谬与仇敌的捆绑——你们要听我说。你们自己知道我在你们中间为人怎样：自从我认识基督、得蒙配作他的仆人以来，我就恨恶万事，惟独爱他；你们也知道，我的心愿本是逃避世上的风暴与虚妄的喧嚷，独自与他相交，在灵魂不受搅扰的平安中，事奉我的神、我的主。但父亲的拦阻留住了我，还有那吩咐我们当孝敬父母的诫命。这样，靠着神的恩典与帮助，我没有徒然劳力，这些日子也没有虚度：我已领我父亲亲近了基督，又教训你们众人认识那独一的真神、万有的主；然而这不是我，乃是那与我同在的神的恩典——这恩典也曾把我从迷信的错谬和敬拜偶像中救出来，又拯救你们，我的民哪，脱离了那残酷的奴役。所以现在，正是成全我向神所许之事奉的时候了；正是动身往那边去的时候了，往他自己要引领我去的地方，在那里我可以偿还我向他所许的愿。所以，你们要为自己拣选一个你们所愿意的人，作你们的领袖和君王；因为到如今你们已经顺从了主的旨意，他的诫命没有一样向你们隐藏。你们要行在其中，不可偏离左右；愿赐平安的神常与你们众人同在！」

那全会众和百姓听见了，立时起了喧嚷与骚动，有大哭喊与混乱；众人都像孤儿一样哭泣，哀悼他们所受的损失。他们苦苦哀哭，起誓流泪，坚称决不放他走，必要拦住他，断不容他离去。百姓和有权位的人正这样大声喊叫的时候，王打断他们，向群众摆手，吩咐他们肃静。他声明自己依从了他们的恳求，就叫他们散去；他们仍怀着忧伤，两颊带着愁苦的痕迹。约阿萨弗就这样行了。众元老中有一位，最得约阿萨弗的恩宠，是个因敬虔与尊贵而受人敬重的人，名叫巴拉加。如前文所述，当纳霍尔假扮巴拉阿姆与哲学家们辩论的时候，惟独他一人愿意站在纳霍尔身旁为他争战，因为他的心被天上的爱所点燃。王把他领到一边，温和地与他说话，恳切地求他接受王位，存敬畏神的心牧养他的百姓，好让他自己得以踏上他所渴望的旅程。

===Original===
But Barachias would put aside and reject his offer, saying, “O king, how wrongful is thy judgement, and thy word contrary to divine command! If thou hast learned to love thy neighbour as thyself, with what right art thou eager to shift the burden off thy back and lay it upon mine? If it be good to be king, keep the good to thy self: but, if it be a stone of stumbling and rock of offence to thy soul, why put it in my pathway and seek to trip me up?” When Ioasaph perceived that he spake thus, and that his purpose was fixed, he ceased from communing with him. And now, at about the dead of night, he wrote his people a letter, full of much wisdom, expounding to them all godliness; telling them what they should think concerning God, what life, what hymns and what thanksgiving they should offer unto him. Next, he charged them to receive none other than Barachias to be ruler of the kingdom. Then left he in his bedchamber the roll containing his letter, and, unobserved of all, went forth from his palace. But he might not win through undetected, for, early on the morrow, the tidings, that he was departed, anon made commotion and mourning among the people, and, in much haste, forth went every man for to seek him; they being minded by all means to cut off his flight. And their zeal was not spent in vain; for, when they had occupied all the highways, and encompassed all the mountains, and surrounded the pathless ravines, they discovered him in a watercourse, his hands uplifted to heaven, saying the prayer proper of the Sixth Hour.

When they beheld him, they surrounded him, and besought him with tears, upbraiding him for departing from them. “But,” said he, “why labour ye in vain? No longer hope to have me to your king.” Yet gave he way to their much opposition, and turned again to his palace. And, when he had assembled all the folk, he signified his will. Then with oath he confirmed his word, that he would dwell with them not one day more. “For,” said he, “I have fulfilled my ministry toward you, and have omitted naught, neither have I kept back anything that was profitable unto you, in failing to show or teach you, testifying to all the faith in our Lord Jesus Christ, and pointing out the paths of repentance. And now behold I go the road that I have long time desired, and all ye shall see my face no more. Wherefore I take you to record this day, as saith the holy Apostle, that I am pure from the blood of you all, for I have not shunned to declare unto you all the counsel of God.”

===Chinese===
巴拉加却推辞拒绝他的请求，说：「王啊，你的判断何其不义，你的话何其违背神的诫命！你既学得了要爱邻舍如同自己，凭甚么急着把这担子从你背上卸下，搁在我身上呢？作王若是好的，你就把这好处留给自己；王位若于你的灵魂是绊脚的石头、跌人的磐石，你为何把它放在我的路上，想要绊倒我呢？」约阿萨弗见他这样说，又看出他的心意已定，就不再与他谈论。到了夜阑人静的时候，他给百姓写了一封信，满有智慧，向他们讲明一切的敬虔之道，告诉他们对于神当存怎样的思想，当度怎样的生活，当向他献上怎样的颂诗与感恩。接着，他嘱咐他们，除了巴拉加以外，不可接纳别人作王国的掌权者。然后他把那写着信的书卷留在卧房里，避过众人的耳目，出了王宫。但他终究未能不被察觉地走脱：次日一早，他已出走的消息立时在民间激起骚动与哀恸，人人都急忙出去寻找他，一心要无论如何截住他的逃亡。他们的热心也没有白费：他们占尽了所有大道，绕遍了群山，围住了无路可通的沟壑，终于在一道水沟里发现了他——他正举手向天，诵念第六时辰当诵的祷告。

众人一见他，就围住他，流着泪恳求他，责怪他要离开他们。他说：「但是，你们为甚么徒然劳碌呢？不要再指望我作你们的王了。」然而他仍屈从了他们多方的拦阻，回转自己的王宫去了。他召集了全体民众，表明了自己的心意，又起誓坚定他的话，说他必不再与他们同住一天。他说：「因为我向你们的职事已经完成了，没有一样省略；凡与你们有益的，我没有一样避讳不说的，无不向你们显示、教训你们，又向众人见证了对我们主耶稣基督的信仰，指出了悔改的诸般道路。看哪，如今我要走那条我久已渴望的路了，你们众人必不得再见我的面。所以今日，我照圣使徒的话请你们作见证：你们众人中间，罪不在我身上，因为神的旨意，我并没有一样避讳不传给你们的。」

===Original===
When they heard this, and perceived the steadfastness of his purpose, that nothing could hinder him from his resolve, they wept like orphans over their bereavement, but could in no wise over-persuade him. Then did the king take that Barachias, of whom we have already spoken, saying, “This is he, brethren, whom I appoint to be your king.” And though Barachias stoutly resisted, yet he established him, unwilling and reluctant, upon the royal throne, and placed the diadem on his head, and gave the kingly ring into his hand. Then he stood facing the cast and made prayer for King Barachias, that his faith toward God might be preserved unwavering, and that he might keep without faltering the path of Christ’s commandments. Therewith he prayed for the clergy and all the flock, asking of God succour for them and salvation, and all that might fitly be asked for their welfare.

Thus he prayed, and then turning said unto Barachias, “Behold, brother, I charge thee, as the Apostle once adjured his people, ‘Take heed unto thyself, and to all the flock, over the which the Holy Ghost hath made thee king, to feed the Lord’s people, whom he hath purchased with his own blood.’ And even as thou wast before me in the knowledge of God, and didst serve him with a pure conscience, so now also show the more zeal in pleasing him. For, as thou hast received of God a mighty sovereignty, thou owest him the greater repayment. Render therefore to thy Benefactor the debt of thanksgiving, by the keeping of his holy commandments and by turning aside from every path whose end is destruction. For it is with kingdoms as with ships. If one of the sailors blunder it bringeth but small damage to the crew. But if the steersman err, he causeth the whole ship to perish. Even so it is with sovranty: if a subject err, he harmeth himself more than the state. But if the king err, he causeth injury to the whole realm. Therefore, as one that shall render strict account, if thou neglect aught of thy duty, guard thyself with all diligence in that which is good. Hate all pleasure that draweth into sin: for, saith the Apostle, ‘Follow peace with all men, and holiness, without which no man shall see the Lord.’ Consider the wheel of men’s affairs, how it runneth round and round, turning and whirling them now up, now down: and amid all its sudden changes, keep thou unchanged a pious mind. To change with every change of affairs betokeneth an unstable heart. But be thou steadfast, wholly established upon that which is good. Be not lifted and vainly puffed up because of temporal honour; but, with purified reason, understand the nothingness of thine own nature, and the span-length and swift flight of life here, and death the yoke-fellow of the flesh. If thou consider these things, thou shalt not be cast into the pit of arrogance, but shalt fear God, the true and heavenly King, and verily thou shalt be blessed. For he saith, ‘Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, and walk in his ways,’ and ‘Blessed is the man that feareth the Lord: he shall have great delight in his commandments.’ And which commandments above all shouldest thou observe? ‘Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy,’ and ‘Be ye merciful, as your heavenly Father is merciful.’ For the fulfilment of this commandment, above all, is required of them that are in high authority. And, soothly, the holder of great authority ought to imitate the giver of that authority to the best of his ability. And herein shall he best imitate God, by considering that nothing is to be preferred before showing mercy. Nay, further, nothing so surely draweth the subject to loyalty toward his Sovereign as the grace of charity bestowed on such as need it. For the service that cometh from fear is flattery in disguise, with the pretence of respect cozening them that pay heed to it; and the unwilling subject rebelleth when he findeth occasion. Whereas he that is held by the ties of loyalty is steadfast in his obedience to the ruling power. Wherefore be thou easy of access to all and open thine ears unto the poor, that thou mayest find the ear of God open unto thee. For as we are to our fellow-servants, such shall we find our Master to us-ward. And, like as we do hear others, so shall we be heard ourselves: and, as we see, so shall we be seen by the divine all-seeing eye. Therefore pay we first mercy for mercy, that we may obtain like for like.

===Chinese===
他们听见了，又看出他心志坚定，没有甚么能拦阻他的决定，就为他们所受的丧别像孤儿一样哭泣，却无论如何也劝他不转。于是王带着我们上文提过的那位巴拉加，说：「弟兄们，这就是我所立作你们王的那位。」巴拉加虽然极力抗拒，他还是把他——不甘不愿地——坚立在王家的宝座上，把王冠戴在他头上，将王的印戒交在他手里。随后他面向东方站立，为巴拉加王祷告，愿他对神的信心得蒙保守、毫不动摇，愿他毫不迟疑地谨守基督诫命之道。接着又为圣职人员并全体羊群祷告，向神为他们求取救助与救恩，并一切合宜为他们福祉祈求的事。

他这样祷告完了，就转过身来对巴拉加说：「看哪，弟兄，我嘱咐你，正如使徒当日郑重嘱咐他的百姓：『圣灵立你作王，你就当为自己谨慎，也为全群谨慎，牧养主的民，就是他用自己血所买来的。』你既在我以先认识神，又曾以清洁的良心事奉他，如今更当显出加倍的热心讨他喜悦。因为你从神领受了浩大的王权，所欠他的报答也当越发重大。所以，你要借着谨守他圣洁的诫命、转离一切结局是灭亡的道路，向你的恩主偿还感恩的债。因为国正如船：水手中若有一人失误，带给众船员的损害还小；掌舵的若错了，就使全船沉灭。王权也是这样：臣仆若犯错，为害自己多于邦国；君王若犯错，就使全国受害。所以，你若在本分上有丝毫疏忽，都必严格交账；故当在善上以万般的殷勤看守自己。要恨恶一切引人犯罪的享乐，因为使徒说：『你们要追求与众人和睦，并要追求圣洁；非圣洁没有人能见主。』你要思想人事之轮，看它怎样团团运转，将人忽而转上、忽而转下；在这一切突兀的变迁之中，你却要持守敬虔的心志，永不改变。随世事每一变迁而改变，正表记着心怀不坚；你却要坚定不移，全然立定在善上。不要因暂时的尊荣而自高、虚妄地膨胀；倒要凭着洁净了的理性，明悟你自己本性的虚无，明悟此生不过一掌之长、疾飞而逝，又明悟死亡——那与肉体同轭相伴的。你若思想这些事，就不致坠入傲慢的坑中，倒要敬畏神——那真实而属天的君王——你也实在必蒙福。因为经上说：『凡敬畏耶和华、遵行他道的人便为有福』；又说：『敬畏耶和华、甚喜爱他命令的，这人便为有福。』那么，你当遵守的诫命，哪一条在一切之先呢？『怜恤人的人有福了！因为他们必蒙怜恤』；『你们要慈悲，像你们的天父慈悲一样。』因为这诫命的成全，在一切之中，尤其要求于身居高位的人。诚然，执掌大权的人，当尽力效法那赐下权柄的。他在这事上最能效法神，就是在凡事上认定，没有一样该摆在施怜悯之前。不但如此，再没有甚么比把仁爱的恩惠施给缺乏之人，更能确实地引动臣民忠于他们的君王了。因为出于惧怕的事奉，不过是改了装束的谄媚，用尊敬的假象哄骗那留意于它的人；不甘愿的臣仆一找到机会，就必反叛。惟有被忠爱之索系住的人，对掌权的恒久坚定顺服。所以你当易于亲近万人，向穷人敞开你的耳朵，好叫你发现神的耳朵也向你敞开。因为我们怎样待同作仆人的，也必见主人怎样待我们；我们怎样垂听别人，自己也必怎样蒙垂听；我们怎样观看，也必怎样在那神圣无所不见的眼中被观看。所以，我们当先以怜悯偿付怜悯，好叫我们怎样施与，也怎样得着。

===Original===
“But hear yet another commandment, the fellow of the former; ‘Forgive, and it shall be forgiven unto you;’ and ‘If ye forgive not men their trespasses, neither will your heavenly father forgive you your trespasses.’ Wherefore bear no malice against them that offend against thee; but, when thou askest forgiveness of thy sins, forgive thyself also them that injure thee, because forgiveness is repaid by forgiveness, and by making peace with our fellow-servants we are ourselves delivered from the wrath of our Master. Again, a lack of compassion towards them that trespass against us maketh our own trespasses unpardonable, even as thou hast heard what befell the man that owed ten thousand talents, how, through his want of pity on his fellow-servant, he was again required to pay all that mighty debt. So we must take good heed lest a like fate betide us. But let us forgive every debt, and cast all anger out of our hearts, in order that our many debts, too, may be forgiven. Beside this, and before all things, keep thou that good thing which is committed to thy trust, the holy Word of faith wherein thou hast been taught and instructed. And let no tare of heresy grow up amongst you, but preserve the heavenly seed pure and sincere, that it may yield a manifold harvest to the master, when he cometh to demand account of our lives, and to reward us according to our deeds, when the righteous shall shine forth as the sun, but darkness and everlasting shame shall cover the sinners. And now, brethren, I commend you to God, and to the word of his grace, which is able to build you up, and to give you an inheritance among all them which are sanctified.”

And when he had thus spoken, he kneeled down, as it is written, and prayed again in tears. And he turned him round, and kissed Barachias, whom he had chosen to their king, and all the officers. Then came a scene fit, belike, to make one weep. They all crowded around him, as though his presence meant life to them, and his departure would reave them of their very souls; and what piteous pleading, what extravagance of grief did they omit? They kissed him; they hung about him; they were beside themselves for anguish of heart. “Woe is us,” cried they, “for this grievous calamity!” They called him, Master, Father, Saviour, Benefactor. “Through thee,” said they, “we learned to know God, and were redeemed from error, and found rest from every ill. What remaineth us after thou art gone? What evils shall not befall us?” Thus saying, they smote upon their breasts, and bewailed the misfortune that had overtaken them. But he with words of comfort hushed their sobs, and promised to be with them still in the spirit though he might no longer abide with them in the body. And when he had thus spoken, in the sight of all he went forth from the palace. And immediately all the people followed him. They despaired of his return; they ran from the city, as from a sight that they could no longer endure. But when they were outside the city, Ioasaph addressed them with sharp words, and chode with them harshly; and so they were parted from him, and unwillingly went home, often turning round to look on him, and stumbling on their road. And some of the hotter spirits also followed afar off weeping, until the shades of night parted them one from another.

===Chinese===
「但你还要听另一条诫命，是与前一条作伴的：『你们要饶恕人，就必蒙饶恕』；『你们不饶恕人的过犯，你们的天父也必不饶恕你们的过犯。』所以，对得罪你的人不可怀恨；当你求自己罪得赦免时，你自己也要饶恕那些伤害你的人，因为饶恕必以饶恕得偿还；我们与同作仆人的和好，自己就脱离了主的忿怒。再者，向那些得罪我们的人缺乏怜恤，就使我们自己的过犯不得赦免——正如你曾听见的，那欠一万他连得的人遭遇了甚么：他因对同伴毫无怜恤，又被追讨偿还那一笔巨债。所以我们必须小心提防，免得同样的命运临到我们。但我们总要饶恕一切的债，把一切忿怒从心里赶除，好叫我们这许多的债也得蒙赦免。此外，在一切之先，你要牢牢守着那交托你的善道，就是你素来在其中受教受训的信仰圣言。不可容异端的稗子在你们中间长起来，总要保守那天上的种子纯洁真诚，好在主人来的时候——就是他来清算我们一生、照我们所行的报应我们，那时义人要发出光来如同太阳，黑暗和永远的羞耻要遮盖罪人——为他结出多倍的收成。如今，弟兄们，我把你们交托神和他恩惠的道；这道能建立你们，叫你们和一切成圣的人同得基业。」

他说完了这些话，就照经上所记的跪下，又流着泪祷告。他转过身来，与他所拣选作他们王的巴拉加亲嘴，又与众官员亲嘴。随后出现的场面，只怕真能叫人落泪。众人都拥挤围着他，仿佛他的同在就是他们的生命，他的离去要把他们的灵魂都夺了去；一切哀怜的恳求、一切过度的悲伤，他们没有一样不行出来的。他们与他亲嘴，缠附在他身旁，因心里的剧痛而神魂失守。他们喊着说：「我们有祸了！遭了这样沉重的灾殃！」他们称他为主、为父、为救主、为恩主。他们说：「是借着你，我们得以认识神，蒙救赎脱离错谬，又从一切的凶恶中得了安息。你去了以后，我们还有甚么可留的呢？有甚么灾祸不会临到我们呢？」他们这样说着，捶胸哀哭，悲叹那临到他们的不幸。他却用安慰的话平息他们的呜咽，又应许他们：他虽不能再以身体与他们同住，却仍要在灵里与他们同在。他说了这话，当着众人出了王宫。众民随即都跟了出去。他们已对他的归回绝望，奔出城去，仿佛逃离一幕再也不能忍受的景象。但到了城外，约阿萨弗用严厉的话对他们说话，严词斥责他们；他们就与他分离了，不情愿地回家去，一路频频回头望他，在路上踉跄跌撞。还有些心肠更热的人，也远远跟着哭泣，直到夜的暗影把他们彼此分开。

## XXXVII / 三十七

===Original===

Thus this noble man went forth from his palace rejoicing, as when after long exile a man returneth with joy to his own country. Outwardly he wore the robes that he was wont to wear, but beneath was the hair-shirt which Barlaam had given him. That night he halted at a poor man’s cabin, and stripped himself of his outer raiment, which, as his last alms, he bestowed upon his poor host, and thus by the prayers of that poor man, as well as of so many others, he made God his ally, and put on his grace and help as a garment of salvation; and, clad in a coat of gladness, thus went he off to his hermit-life, carrying with him neither bread, nor water, nor any necessary food, with no garment upon him save the aforesaid rough shirt. For his heart was wounded with a marvellous longing and divine love for Christ the immortal King; he was beside himself with longing, mad for God, possessed by love of him; “For love,” he saith, “is strong as fire.” So drunken was he with this heavenly love, so parched with thirst, according to him that saith, “Like as the hart desireth the water-brooks, so longeth my soul after thee, O God. My soul is athirst for the mighty and living God”; or, as the soul that is sick of love crieth in the Song of Songs, “Thou hast ravished us, ravished us with the desire of thee”; and, “Let me see thy countenance, and let me hear thy voice, for thy voice is a sweet voice, and thy countenance is comely.”

It was the desire for this unspeakable comeliness of Christ that fired the hearts of the Apostolic Choir and of the Martyr folk to despise the things that are seen, and all this temporal life, and the rather to choose ten thousand forms of death and torture, being enamoured of his heavenly beauty, and bearing in mind the charm that the divine Word used for to win our love. Such was the fire that was kindled in the soul of this fair youth also, noble in body, but most noble and kingly in soul, that led him to despise all earthly things alike, to trample on all bodily pleasures, and to contemn riches and glory and the praise of men, to lay aside diadem and purple, as of less worth than cobwebs, and to surrender himself to all the hard and irksome toils of the ascetic life, crying, “O my Christ, my soul is fixed upon thee, and thy right hand hath upholden me.”
