Thus, without looking back, he passed into the depth of the desert; and, laying aside, like a heavy burden and clog, the stress of transitory things, he rejoiced in the Spirit, and looked steadfastly on Christ, whom he longed for, and cried aloud to him, as though he were there present to hear his voice, saying, “Lord, let mine eyes never again see the good things of this present world. Never, from this moment, let my soul be excited by these present vanities, but fill mine eyes with spiritual tears; direct my goings in thy way, and show me thy servant Barlaam. Show me him that was the means of my salvation, that I may learn of him the exact rule of this lonely and austere life, and may not be tripped up through ignorance of the wiles of the enemy. Grant me, O Lord, to discover the way whereby to attain unto thee, for my soul is sick of love for thee, and I am athirst for thee, the well of salvation.”

These were the thoughts of his heart continually, and he communed with God, being made one with him by prayer and sublime meditation. And thus eagerly he pursued the road, hoping to arrive at the place where Barlaam dwelt. His meat was the herbs that grow in the desert; for he carried nothing with him, as I have already said, save his own bones, and the ragged garment that was around him.

===Chinese===

于是这位尊贵的人欢欢喜喜地出了王宫，如同人在长久流放之后，欢然归回本乡一般。他外面穿着素常所穿的袍服，里面却贴身穿着巴拉阿姆所给他的粗毛衣。当夜他投宿在一个穷人的小屋里，脱下自己的外衣，作为最后的施舍，赠与那贫穷的屋主；这样，借着那穷人的祷告，并其余许多人的祷告，他使神作了他的盟友，将神的恩典与帮助当作救恩的衣服穿上；又身披喜乐的衣裳，就这样启程奔赴他的隐修生活，随身的饼没有，水没有，任何必需的食物也没有；身上除上述那件粗砺的衬衣以外，再无别的衣服。因为他的心已为对不朽君王基督的奇妙恋慕与神圣之爱所创伤；他为恋慕而神魂颠倒，为神癫狂，被对他的爱所占有，因为他曾说：「爱坚强如火。」他为这属天的爱如此沉醉，又如此干渴难忍，正如那作诗的所说：「神啊，我的心切慕你，如鹿切慕溪水。我的心渴想神，就是那大能的永生神」；又如那思爱成病的灵魂在《雅歌》中呼喊：「你以切慕之情把我们夺去了，夺去了」；又说：「愿我得见你的面貌，得听你的声音，因为你的声音柔和，你的面貌秀美。」

正是对基督这无可言喻之秀美的恋慕，点燃了使徒的行列与殉道众民的心，使他们轻看所见之物与这整个短暂的人生，宁择万千般死亡与酷刑，因他们醉心于他属天的美丽，又铭记神的道为赢得我们的爱所施展的魅力。在这俊美的青年——论身体高贵，论灵魂却至为高贵、满有王者之风——心里，点燃的也正是这样的火；这火引领他同等轻看一切属地之物，践踏一切肉体的逸乐，蔑视财富、荣耀与人的称赞，将冠冕与紫袍置于一旁，视之还不如蛛网贵重，又把自己交付于苦修生活一切艰苦烦劳的操劳，呼喊说：「我的基督啊，我心紧紧地跟随你，你的右手扶持我。」

就这样，他一去不回头，进入了旷野的深处；又将短暂世事的重压，如同重担与累赘一般卸下，在圣灵里喜乐，定睛仰望他所切慕的基督，仿佛他就立在眼前、听他声音一般，向他大声呼喊说：「主啊，愿我的眼目永不再看见这今世的美好事物。从此刻起，愿我的灵魂永不再被这些眼前的虚妄所激动，只求你使我的双眼满含属灵的眼泪；指引我的脚步行在你的道上，将你的仆人巴拉阿姆指示我。将那作成我救恩之器皿的指示我，使我可以从他学得这孤独而严峻之生活的准确法则，不致因不识仇敌的诡计而被绊跌。主啊，求你赐我得寻那条达到你面前的路，因为我为你思爱成病，我渴想你——那救恩的泉源。」

他心里常常存着的，正是这些思念；他与神相交，借着祷告与崇高的默观，得与神合而为一。他就这样切切赶路，盼望抵达巴拉阿姆所住的地方。他的食物就是旷野里所长出的草蔬；因为他如我上文所说，身边一无携带，除了他自己的骨头，和缠在他身上的那件褴褛衣裳。

===Original===

But whilst he found some food, though scanty and insufficient, from the herbs, of water he was quite destitute in that waterless and dry desert. And so at noontide, as he held on his way under the fierce blaze of the sun, he was parched with thirst in the hot drought of that desert place, and he suffered the extreme of anguish. But desire of Christ conquered nature, and the thirst wherewith he thirsted for God bedewed the heat of thirst for water.

Now the devil, being envious and full of hate for that which is beautiful, unable to endure the sight of such steadfastness of purpose, and glowing love towards God, raised up against Ioasaph many temptations in the wilderness. He called to his remembrance his kingly glory, and his magnificent bodyguard, his friends, kinsfolk and companions, and how the lives of all had depended on his life, and he minded him of the other solaces of life. Then he would confront him with the hardness of virtue, and the many sweats that she requireth, with the weakness of his flesh, with his lack of practice in such rigours, the long years to come, this present distress from thirst, his want of any comfort, and the unendingness of his toils. In a word, he raised a great dust-cloud of reasonings in his mind, exactly, I ween, as it hath been recorded of the mighty Antony.

But, when the enemy saw himself too weak to shake that purpose (for Ioasaph set Christ before his mind, and glowed with love of him, and was well strengthened by hope, and steadfast in faith, and recked nothing of the devil and his suggestions), then was the adversary ashamed of having fallen in the first assault. So he came by another road (for many are his paths of wickedness), and endeavoured to overthrow and terrify Ioasaph by means of divers apparitions. Sometimes he appeared to him in black, and such indeed he is: sometimes with a drawn sword he leapt upon him, and threatened to strike, unless he speedily turned back. At other times he assumed the shapes of all manner of beasts, roaring and making a terrible din and bellowing; or again he became a dragon, adder, or basilisk. But that fair and right noble athlete kept his soul in quietness, for he had made the Most High his refuge: and, being sober in mind, he laughed the evil one to scorn, and said, “I know thee, deceiver, who thou art, which stiffest up this trouble for me; which from the beginning didst devise mischief against mankind, and art ever wicked, and never stintest to do hurt. How becoming and right proper is thy habit, that thou shouldest take the shape of beasts and of creeping things, and thus display thy bestial and crooked nature, and thy venomous and hurtful purpose! Wherefore, wretch, attempt the impossible? For ever since I discovered that these be the contrivances and bugbears of thy malice, I have now no more anxiety concerning thee. The Lord is on my side, and I shall see my desire upon mine enemies. I shall go upon the adder and basilisk, the which thou dost resemble; thee, the lion and dragon I shall tread under my feet; for I am strengthened with the might of Christ. Let mine enemies be ashamed and turned backward: let them be driven and put to shame suddenly.”

Thus speaking, and girding on that invincible weapon, the sign of the Cross, he made vain the devil’s shows. For straightway all the beasts and creeping things disappeared, like as the smoke vanisheth, and like as wax melteth at the fire. And he, strong in the might of Christ, went on his way rejoicing and giving thanks unto the Lord. But there dwelt in that desert many divers beasts, and all kinds of serpents, and dragon-shaped monsters, and these met him, not now as apparitions but in sober sooth, so that his path was beset by fear and toil. But he overcame both, for love, as saith the scripture, cast out fear, and longing made toil light. Thus he wrestled with many sundry misfortunes and hardships until, after many days, he arrived at that desert of the land of Senaar, wherein Barlaam dwelt. There also he found water and quenched the burning of his thirst.

===Chinese===

然而，草蔬虽可供给一点食物，却稀少而不足；在那无水干旱的旷野里，他则全然无水可用。于是在正午，当他在烈日猛焰之下继续赶路时，他因那旷野之地的燥热干旱而渴得焦灼，受尽了极度的痛楚。但对基督的恋慕胜过了本性，他为神而发的干渴，如甘露滋润了他为水而受的燥热。

魔鬼素来嫉妒美善、满怀仇恨，忍受不得看见这般坚定的志向与向神炽燃的爱，就在旷野中向约阿萨弗兴起了许多试探。他叫他回想自己的王者荣耀、他显赫的卫队、他的朋友亲族与同伴，回想众人的性命曾如何系于他一人的性命，又拿人生其余的种种慰藉来提醒他。随后，他又拿德行的严苛、她所要求的多方汗劳，拿他肉体的软弱、他素未惯于这般严酷的操练、未来漫长的岁月、当前干渴的窘迫、安慰的匮乏、劳苦的无休无止，来当面攻逼他。一言以蔽之，他在他心思里掀起了一大团思虑的尘雾——我想，这与关于大能者安东尼的记载丝毫不差。

及至仇敌见自己力薄，摇撼不动这志向（因为约阿萨弗将基督摆在心思眼前，为他炽燃着爱，因盼望大得坚固，在信仰上坚定不移，全不把魔鬼和他的暗示放在心上），那敌对者便因初次进攻败落而羞愧。于是他改从另一条路来（因为他的邪恶路径本就繁多），企图借种种怪异的幻象推倒、恐吓约阿萨弗。有时他向他显现为黑色——他的本相确是如此；有时他持着出鞘的剑向他扑去，威胁要击刺，除非他速速折返。有时他又变作各样野兽的形状，咆哮吼震，发出可怖的喧声；或又变作龙、虺蛇或蛇怪。但那俊美而极尊贵的竞技者保守自己的灵魂于安静之中，因为他已以至高者为避难所；他心思清醒，嗤笑那恶者说：「我知道你是谁，你这骗子，是你在我身上搅起这番风波；你从起初就向人类谋算恶事，永远邪恶，从不收敛你的伤害。你取野兽与爬行之物的形状，以此显露你兽性而乖曲的本性、你恶毒而有害的存心——你这习性何等相称，何等合宜！所以，你这卑劣之徒，何必妄试那不可能的事？自从我发现这些不过是出于你恶毒的诡谋与唬人的伎俩，我从此就不再为你挂虑了。耶和华在我这一边，我必亲眼看见我仇敌遭报。我要行在虺蛇与蛇怪之上——你所酷肖的正是这些；至于你——狮子与龙——我必将你践踏在脚下，因为我是靠着基督的大能得以刚强。愿我的仇敌羞愧退后，愿他们被驱散，忽然蒙羞。」

他这样说着，又束上那无敌的兵器——十字架的记号，便使魔鬼的诸般幻景尽归无效。因为立时间，众野兽与爬行之物尽都消没，如烟消散，如蜡在火前熔化。他靠着基督的大能刚强前行，一路欢欣，称谢主。但那旷野中确实栖居着许多各类的野兽、各样蛇类与龙形的怪物；它们迎着他来，如今不再是幻象，而是千真万确，使他的路途步步布满惧怕与劳苦。但惧怕与劳苦这两样他都胜过了，因为爱，如经上所说，把惧怕除去，切慕也使劳苦轻省。就这样，他与种种不幸和艰苦角力，直到多日之后，来到示拿地的旷野，就是巴拉阿姆所住的地方。在那里，他也寻得了水，止息了灼烧着他的干渴。

## XXXVIII / 三十八

===Original===
Now two full years spent Ioasaph wandering about the ocean of that desert, without finding Barlaam; for here also God was proving the steadfastness of his purpose, and the nobility of his soul. He lived thus in the open air, scorched with heat or frozen with cold, and, as one in search of precious treasure, continually looking everywhere for his treasured friend, the aged Barlaam. Frequent were the temptations and assaults of the evil spirits that he encountered, and many the hardships that he endured through the lack of herbs that he needed for meat, because the desert, being dry, yielded even these in but scant supply. But, being kindled by love of her Master, this adamantine and indomitable soul bore these annoyances more easily than other men bear their pleasures. Wherefore he failed not of the succour that is from above, but, many as were the sorrows and toils that he endured, comfort came to him from Christ, and, asleep or awake, refreshed his soul. By the space of those two years Ioasaph went about continually, seeking him for whom he yearned, and rivers of waters ran from his eyes, as he implored God, crying aloud and saying, “Show me, O Lord, show me the man that was the means of my knowledge of thee, and the cause of my many blessings. Because of the multitude of mine offences, deprive me not of this good thing; but grant me to see him, and fight with him the ascetic fight.”

By the grace of God, he found a cave, by following footsteps that led thither. There he met a monk pursuing a hermit life. Him he embraced and saluted tenderly. He asked where to find Barlaam’s dwelling, and told him his own tale, laying all bare. Of him then he learned the abode of the man whom he sought, and thither went foot-hot, as when a cunning hunter happeneth on the tracks of his game. And when he had met with certain signs, pointed out to him by this other old hermit, he went on rejoicing, strong in hope, like a child hoping after long absence to see his father. For when divine love hath broken into a soul, it proveth hotter and stronger than the natural.

So he stood before the door of the cave, and knocked, saying “*Benedicite*, father, *benedicite*!” When Barlaam heard his voice, he came forth from the cave, and by the spirit knew him, who by outward appearance could not easily be known, because of the marvellous change and alteration that had changed and altered his face from its former bloom of youth; for Ioasaph was black with the sun’s heat, and overgrown with hair, and his cheeks were fallen in, and his eyes deep sunken, and his eyelids seared with floods of tears, and much distress of hunger. And Ioasaph recognised his spiritual father, for his features were, for the more part, the same. So the old man stood, and, facing the East, offered up to God a prayer of thanksgiving; and, after the prayer, when they had said the Amen, they embraced and kissed each other affectionately, taking their full fill of long deferred desire.

===Chinese===
此后整整两年，约阿萨弗在那旷野的洋海中漂流游走，却寻不见巴拉阿姆；因为在这里，神也正在试验他心志的坚定与他灵魂的高贵。他就这样露宿于天地之间，或受炎日灼烤，或被严寒冻僵；又如同搜寻珍宝的人，到处不住寻找他视为至宝的朋友——年迈的巴拉阿姆。他所遭遇的，是邪灵频频的试探与攻击；他所忍受的，是因缺少可作食物的草本而生的许多艰辛，因为这旷野干旱，连这些出产也极为稀少。但这如金刚石般不可屈挠的灵魂，因着爱她的主而燃灼，忍受这些烦扰，比世人忍受他们的逸乐还觉容易。因此，从上头来的扶助没有离开他；他忍受的忧伤与劳苦尽管众多，安慰却从基督那里临到他，或睡或醒，都使他心灵畅快。在那两年之间，约阿萨弗不住地四处奔走，寻找他所切慕的人；泪水从他眼中如江河涌流，他恳求神，大声呼喊说：「主啊，求你将那人指示我，将那使我得以认识你的器皿、我诸多福分的缘由指示我。因我过犯众多，求你不要叫我失去这美善的事；惟求你使我能见到他，与他一同打那苦修的仗。」

靠着神的恩典，他循着引到那里的脚印，寻见了一个洞窟。在那里，他遇见一位过隐修生活的修士。约阿萨弗拥抱他，亲切地问安，又询问在哪里可以寻见巴拉阿姆的住处，并把自己的经历一五一十地述说出来，毫无隐瞒。于是他从那人那里得知了他所寻之人的居所，便急忙赶去，如同精明的猎人偶然撞见猎物的踪迹。及至他遇上那位老隐修士向他指明的种种标记，就欢欢喜喜地继续前行，满有盼望的力量，如同孩子久别之后盼望见到父亲一样。因为当属神的爱闯入一个灵魂，便显为比天然之情更加炽热、更加强猛。

于是他站在洞窟门前叩门，说：「*Benedicite*（请祝福），父啊，*Benedicite*！」巴拉阿姆听见他的声音，就从洞窟里出来，凭着灵认出他来——若凭外貌，这人已不易辨认了，因为奇妙的改变与变化，已将他的脸从昔日青春的丰采改变了：约阿萨弗被烈日晒得黝黑，须发蓬生，双颊凹陷，两眼深陷，眼皮被泪水的洪流烙得焦灼，还带着多日饥馑的煎熬。约阿萨弗也认出了他的属灵之父，因为他的容貌大抵依旧。于是老人站起来，面向东方，向神献上感恩的祷告；祷告完毕，两人同说阿们之后，就彼此拥抱，亲亲切切地互相亲嘴，充分饱享那久悬未偿的渴望。

===Original===
But, when they had done with embracing and greeting, they sat them down and conversed. Barlaam began, saying, “Welcome art thou, son well beloved son of God, and inheritor of the heavenly kingdom through Jesus Christ our Lord, whom thou lovest, whom thou rightly desirest above the things that are temporal and corruptible! Like a prudent and wise merchant, thou hast sold all, and bought the pearl that is beyond price, and hast found the treasure that cannot be stolen, hidden in the field of the commandments of the Lord; thou hast parted with all, and spared naught of the things that so soon pass away, that thou mightest purchase that field for thyself. The Lord give thee the eternal for the temporal, the things that are incorruptible and wax not old for the corruptible!

“But tell me, dearly beloved, how thou camest hither? How did thy matters speed after my departure? And hath thy father learned to know God, or is he still carried away with his former foolishness, still under the bondage of devilish deceits?”

Thus questioned Barlaam, and Ioasaph answered, telling him piece by piece all that had befallen him since he went away; and in how many ways the Lord had prospered him, until they were come together again.

===Chinese===
拥抱问安既毕，两人便坐下交谈。巴拉阿姆先开口说：「欢迎你，神所深爱的儿子，靠我们的主耶稣基督承受天国为业的嗣子——这位主，你爱他，你正当地渴慕他过于那暂时而朽坏的事物！你好像那聪明审慎的买卖人，变卖了一切，买了那无价的珠子；又寻得了那不能被偷夺的宝藏，就是藏在主诫命之田里的；你舍弃了一切，在那转瞬即逝的事物上一无顾惜，为要为自己买下那块田。愿主以永恒报答你所舍的暂时，以那不能朽坏、不能衰残的，替换你所舍的朽坏！

「只是告诉我，亲爱的孩子：你是怎样到这里来的？我离开以后，你的事情进展如何？你父亲认识神了么？还是仍旧被他从前的愚妄冲去，仍旧在魔鬼欺哄的捆绑之下？」

巴拉阿姆这样询问，约阿萨弗便一一回答，把巴拉阿姆离去以后临到自己身上的一切事逐件述说给他听，又讲述主怎样多方面地使他顺利亨通，直到他们再次相聚。

===Original===
The old man listened with pleasure and amazement, and with hot tears said, “Glory to thee, our God, that ever standest by and succourest them that love thee! Glory to thee, O Christ, King of all and God all-good, that it was thy pleasure that the seed, which I sowed in the heart of Ioasaph, thy servant, should thus bring forth fruit an hundredfold worthy of the husbandman and Master of our souls! Glory to thee, good Paraclete, the all-holy Spirit, because thou didst vouchsafe unto this man to partake of that grace which thou gavest thine holy Apostles, and by his hand hast delivered multitudes of people from superstitious error, and enlightened them with the true knowledge of God!”

Thus was God blessed by both, and thus were they conversing and rejoicing in the grace of God until evenfall. Then stood they up for to pray and to perform the sacred services. Then also remembered they that it was mealtime, and Barlaam spread his lavish table, laden with spiritual dainties, but with little to attract the palate of sense. These were uncooked worts, and a few dates, planted and tended by Barlaam’s own hands, such as are found in the same desert, and wild herbs. So they gave thanks and partook of the victuals set before them, and drank water from the neighbour springing well, and again gave thanks to God, who openeth his hand and filleth all things living. Then they arose again, and, when they had ended their Night Hours, after prayer, they joined in spiritual converse again, discoursing wholesome words, and full of heavenly wisdom, all the night long until daybreak bade them once more remember the hour of prayer.

So Ioasaph abode with Barlaam for some many years, pursuing this marvellous and more than human life, dwelling with him as with a father and tutor, in all obedience and lowliness, exercising himself in every kind of virtue, and learning well from practice how to wrestle with the invisible spirits of evil. From that time forward he mortified all his sinful passions, and made the will of the flesh as subject to the spirit as slave is to his master. He was altogether forgetful of comforts or repose, and tyrannized over sleep as over a wicked servant. And, in brief, such was his practice of the religious life, that Barlaam, who had spent many years therein, marvelled at him, and failed to equal the earnestness of his life. For he took only so much of that coarse and cheerless food as would keep him alive; else had he died afore his time, and forfeited the reward of his well-doing. He subdued himself to watchings, as though he were without flesh and body. In prayer and mental exercise his work was unceasing, and all the time of his life was spent in spiritual and heavenly contemplation, so that not an hour, nor even a single moment was wasted, from the day that he came to dwell in the desert. For this is the end of monastic life, never to be found idle in spiritual employment: and well herein did this noble and active runner of the heavenly race order his way. And he kept his ardour unquenched from beginning to end, ever ascending in his heart, and going from strength to strength, and continually adding desire to desire, and zeal to zeal, until he arrived at the bliss that he had hoped and longed for.

===Chinese===
老人怀着欣喜与惊异聆听，流着热泪说：「愿荣耀归于你，我们的神，你永远护佑在旁，扶助那爱你的人！愿荣耀归于你，基督啊，万有之王、全善的神，你竟乐意叫我所撒在你仆人约阿萨弗心里的种子，这样结出百倍的果子，配得上我们灵魂的农夫与主人！愿荣耀归于你，良善的保惠师、至圣的灵，因为你曾施恩给这人，使他有分于你赐给你圣洁众使徒的那恩典，又借他的手拯救了万千民众脱离迷信的错谬，以对神的真认识光照他们！」

两人就这样称颂神，又这样交谈，在神的恩典中欢喜快乐，直到黄昏。于是他们站起来祷告，行那神圣的礼拜。这时他们才想起是用饭的时候了，巴拉阿姆便摆开他丰盛的筵席，满载属灵的美馔，却少有能满足感官口味的东西：那是些未经烹煮的菜蔬，还有几颗椰枣——是巴拉阿姆亲手栽种照管的，正如同一旷野中所出产的——并一些野草。他们祝谢了，享用摆在他们面前的食物，又喝邻近涌流之井里的水，再为那位张开手、使凡有生气的都随愿饱足的神献上感谢。然后他们又起来，祷告之后，行完了夜间的时辰课颂，便再度进入属灵的交谈，整夜讲述有益于人、满属天智慧的话，直到破晓提醒他们再次记起祷告的时辰。

此后约阿萨弗与巴拉阿姆同住了许多年，过着这奇妙而超乎人性的生活，如同与父亲和师傅同住一般，凡事顺服、凡事谦卑，操练各样的德行，并在实行中熟习如何与那些看不见的邪恶之灵角力。从那时起，他治死了一切犯罪的情欲，使肉体的意志顺服于灵，如同奴仆顺服于主人。他全然忘了安逸与歇息，辖制睡眠，如同辖制一个恶仆。简而言之，他敬虔生活的操练如此，以致在其中度过多年的巴拉阿姆也为之惊叹，及不上他生活的恳切。因为那粗粝乏味的食物，他只取仅足以维生的分量；否则他必未到期而死，丧失了他善行的赏赐。他使自己屈从于守夜，仿佛没有肉体、没有身子一般。在祷告与心神的操练上，他的工夫永不止息；他一生的时光都消磨在属灵与属天的默观之中，以致自他来住在旷野的那日起，没有一时、乃至没有一刻被虚掷浪费。因为修道生活的宗旨正是如此：总不被人发现在属灵的事工上闲懒。这位在那属天赛程中高贵而勤勉的奔跑者，在这一件事上安排他的道路，何其美好。他保守自己的热心自始至终不灭，心中不住上升，力上加力，不断地愿上加愿、热上加热，直到他达到了他所盼望、所切慕的福乐。

## XXXIX / 三十九

===Original===
Thus did Barlaam and Ioasaph dwell together, rivals in the good rivalry, apart from all anxious care and all the turmoils of life, possessing their minds undisturbed and clear of all confusion. After their many labours after godliness, one day Barlaam called to him his spiritual son, whom he had begotten through the Gospel, and opened his mouth to discourse of spiritual things, saying, “Long ago, dearly beloved Ioasaph, was it destined that thou shouldest dwell in this wilderness; and, in answer to my prayer for thee, Christ promised me that I should see it before the ending of my life. I have seen my desire: I have seen thee severed from the world and the concerns of the world, united to Christ, thy mind never wavering, and come to the measure of the perfection of his fullness. Now therefore as the time of my departure is at the door, and seeing that my desire, that hath grown with my growth and aged with my years, to be forever with Christ, is even now being fulfilled, thou must bury my body in the earth and restore dust to dust, but thyself abide for the time to come in this place, holding fast to thy spiritual life, and making remembrance of me, poor as I am. For I fear lest perchance the darksome army of fiends may stand in the way of my soul, by reason of the multitude of mine ignorances.

“So do thou, my son, think no scorn of the laboriousness of thy religious life, neither dread the length of the time, nor the tricks of devils. But, strong in the grace of Christ, confidently laugh at the weakness of these thy foes; and, as for the hardness of thy toils, and the long duration of the time, be as one that daily expecteth his departure hence, and as if the same day were the beginning and the end of thy religious life. Thus, always forgetting the things which are behind, and reaching forth unto those things which are before, press toward the mark for the prize of the high calling of God in Christ Jesus, according to the exhortation of the holy Apostle, who saith, ‘Let us not faint; but though our outward man perish, yet the inward man is renewed day by day. For our light affliction, which is but for a moment, worketh for us a far more exceeding eternal weight of glory; while we look not at the things which are seen, but at the things which are not seen: for the things which are seen are temporal; but the things which are not seen are eternal.’

“Ponder thou over these things, beloved: quit thee like a man; yea, be strong; and, as a good soldier, do thy diligence to please him who hath called thee to be a soldier. And, even if the evil one stir in thee thoughts of neglecting duty, and thou art minded to slacken the string of thy purpose, fear not his devices, but remember the Lord’s command, which saith, ‘In the world ye shall have tribulation, but be of good cheer; I have overcome the world.’ Wherefore, rejoice in the Lord alway; for he hath chosen and separated thee out of the world, and set thee, as it were before his countenance. The Master, who hath called thee with a holy calling, is alway near. Be careful for nothing, but in everything, by prayer and supplication with thanksgiving let thy requests be made known unto God. For he himself hath said, ‘I will never leave thee, nor forsake thee.’ So, by the hardness of thy life, and by scorn of its rigours, win such thoughts as these, and rejoice, remembering our Lord God, for he saith, ‘I remembered God and was glad.’”

===Chinese===
巴拉阿姆与约阿萨弗就这样一同居住，在这场善美的竞赛中彼此争先，远离一切焦虑的挂怀与人生的纷扰，持守心思不受搅扰、清朗而无混乱。在他们多年敬虔的劳苦之后，一日，巴拉阿姆把他借着福音所生的属灵之子叫到跟前，开口讲论属灵的事，说：「亲爱的约阿萨弗啊，你住在这旷野，原是早已注定的；我曾为你祈求，基督便应许我，必使我在性命未终以先看见这事。我所心愿的已经看见了：我看见你已与世界并世界的事隔绝，与基督联合，心思毫不动摇，达到了满有基督长成身量的完全地步。如今我离世的时候已在门口；我那随着我的成长而长大、随着我的年岁而变老的心愿——永远与基督同在的心愿——如今正要成全。所以你必须将我的身体埋葬在地里，使尘土归于尘土；你自己却要在这地方继续住下去，持定你的属灵生命，并记念我这可怜的人。因为我惧怕，唯恐那幽暗的鬼魔军旅，会因着我诸多无知的罪，拦阻我的灵魂。

「所以，我儿，你不要轻看你修道生活的劳苦，也不要惧怕时日的漫长，或魔鬼的诡计。你要在基督的恩典上刚强，放胆嗤笑你这些仇敌的软弱；至于你劳苦的艰辛、时日的久长，你要看自己如同天天期待离世的人，把这一天当作你修道生活的起头，又当作它的终结。这样，你要总是忘记背后，努力面前的，向着标竿直跑，要得神在基督耶稣里从上面召你来得的奖赏，正如圣使徒所劝勉的；他说：『我们不丧胆；外体虽然毁坏，内心却一天新似一天。我们这至暂至轻的苦楚，要为我们成就极重无比、永远的荣耀；原来我们不是顾念所见的，乃是顾念所不见的；因为所见的是暂时的，所不见的是永远的。』

「亲爱的啊，你要思想这些事，作大丈夫，要刚强；像好士兵一样，殷勤讨那召你当兵之主的喜悦。即使那恶者在你里面搅起怠忽职分的意念，你想要松开志向之弦，也不要怕他的诡计，只要记念主的命令；他说：『在世上你们有苦难，但你们可以放心，我已经胜了世界。』所以你要靠主常常喜乐；因为他已经拣选你、把你从世界中分别出来，仿佛将你安置在他面前。那以圣召召你的主，时刻相近。你要一无挂虑，只要凡事借着祷告、祈求和感谢，将你所要的告诉神。因为他亲自说过：『我总不撇下你，也不丢弃你。』所以，你要借着生活的严厉，借着对其艰苦的轻看，赢得这样的意念，并且欢喜，记念我们的主神，因为他说：『我记念神，就欢喜。』」

===Original===
“But when the adversary, seeking another fashion of war, proposeth high and arrogant thoughts, and suggesteth the glory of the kingdom of this world, which thou hast forsaken, and all its lures, hold out, as a shield before thee, the saving word that saith, ‘When ye shall have done all those things which are commanded you, say, “We are unprofitable servants, for we have done that which was our duty to do.”’ And, indeed, which of us is able to repay the debt that we owe our Master, for that he, though he was rich, yet for our sakes became poor, that we through his poverty might become rich, and, being without suffering, yet suffered, that we might be delivered from suffering? What thanks hath the servant if he suffer like as his Master? But we fall far short of his sufferings. Meditate upon these things, casting down imaginations, and every high thing that exalteth itself against the knowledge of God, and bringing into captivity every thought to the obedience of Christ. And the peace of God which passeth all understanding shall keep thy heart and thoughts in Christ Jesus.”

When blessed Barlaam had so said, Ioasaph’s tears knew no measure, but, like water from the brimming fountain, bedewed him and the ground whereon he sat. He mourned over the parting, and earnestly implored that he might be his companion on his last journey, and might remain no longer in this world after Barlaam’s decease, saying, “Wherefore, father, seekest thou only thine own, and not thy neighbour’s welfare? How fulfillest thou perfect love in this, according to him that said, ‘Thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself,’ in departing thyself to rest and life, and leaving me to tribulation and distress? And, before I have been well exercised in the conflicts of the religious life, before I have learned the wily attacks of the enemy, why expose me to fight singlehanded against their marshalled host? And for what purpose but to see me overthrown by their mischievous machinations, and to see me die, alas! the true spiritual and eternal death? That is the fate which must befall inexperienced and cowardly monks. But, I beseech thee, pray the Lord to take me also together with thee from life. Yea, by the very hope that thou hast of receiving the reward of thy labour, pray that, after thy departure, I may not live one day more in the world, nor wander into the ocean depths of this desert.”

While Ioasaph spake thus in tears, the old man checked him gently and calmly, saying, “Son, we ought not to resist the judgements of God, which are beyond our reach. For though I have oftentimes prayed concerning this matter, and constrained the Master, that cannot be constrained, not to part us one from the other, yet have I been taught by his goodness that it is not expedient for thee now to lay aside the burden of the flesh: but thou must remain behind in the practice of virtue, until the crown, which thou art weaving, be more glorious. As yet, thou hast not striven enough after the recompense in store for thee, but must toil yet a little longer, that thou mayest joyfully enter into the joy of thy Lord. For myself, I am, as I reckon, well-nigh an hundred winters old, and have now spent seventy and five years in this desert place. But for thee, even if thy days be not so far lengthened as mine, yet must thou approach thereto, as the Lord ordereth, that thou mayest prove no unworthy match for them that have borne the burden and heat of the day. Therefore, beloved, gladly accept the decrees of God. What God hath ordered, who, of men, can scatter? Endure, then, under the protection of his grace.

===Chinese===
「但当那仇敌改换另一种战争的方式，提出高傲狂妄的意念，又暗示你所撇下的这世界的国及其荣耀，并它一切的诱饵时，你要举起那救恩之道，如同盾牌挡在身前；那道说：『你们作完了一切所吩咐你们的事，只当说：「我们是无用的仆人，所作的本是我们应分作的。」』再说，我们谁能偿还我们所欠主的债呢？他本来富足，却为我们的缘故成了贫穷，叫我们因他的贫穷可以成为富足；他本无受苦，却受了苦，为要救我们脱离苦难。仆人若与主人同样受苦，还有什么可酬谢的呢？何况我们远不及他的受苦。你要思想这些事，将各样的计谋，各样拦阻人认识神的那些自高之事，一概攻破了，又将人所有的心意夺回，使他都顺服基督。这样，神所赐出人意外的平安，必在基督耶稣里保守你的心怀意念。」

可称颂的巴拉阿姆说了这些话，约阿萨弗的眼泪便再无节制，如同满溢的泉水，洒湿了老人和他所坐的地上。他为这分离哀恸，恳切祈求让自己陪着老人走最后的旅程，不要在巴拉阿姆去世之后仍留在世上，说：「父亲啊，你为何只求自己的益处，而不求邻人的益处呢？那吩咐『要爱人如己』的主既然说过这话，你却自己去安息、去得生命，把我留在患难困苦之中，这怎能算成全了完全的爱呢？况且我尚未在修道生活的争战中受足操练，尚未识得仇敌狡诈的攻击，你为何就把我交出去，叫我孤身一人迎战他们列阵的大军呢？这除了叫我被他们恶毒的机谋所倾覆，叫我死——哀哉，那真正属灵而永远的死——之外，还有什么目的呢？这正是无经历而怯懦的修士必遭的结局。所以我求你，祷告主，叫我也与你一同从今生被接去。是的，凭着你所怀那必得劳苦赏赐的盼望，我求你祷告，叫我在你离世之后，在这世上一天也不再存活，也不漂流在这旷野的大洋深处。」

约阿萨弗流泪这样说的时候，老人温柔而平静地劝止他，说：「我儿，神的判断非我们所能及，我们不该抗拒。这事我虽屡次祷告，勉强那不能受勉强的主，不要叫我们彼此分开，却已借他的良善受教，知道你现在卸下肉体的担子还不合宜；你必须留在后面操练德行，直到你所编织的冠冕更为荣耀。你追求那为你存留的赏赐，至今尚不够竭力，还须再劳苦片时，好使你得以欢欢喜喜进入你主的快乐。至于我自己，我算起来已将近一百个寒冬，如今在这旷野之地已度过了七十五年。至于你，即使你的年日不似我这样长久，也必须照主的安排逐渐接近这数目，使你不致配不上那些曾背负白日重担与炎热的人。所以，亲爱的，要甘心领受神的定旨。神所安排的，世人谁能废掉呢？所以你要在他恩典的护庇之下坚忍。

===Original===
“But be thou ever sober against thoughts other than these; and, like a right precious treasure, keep safely from robbers thy purity of heart, stepping up day by day to higher work and contemplation, that that may be fulfilled in thee, which the Saviour promised to his friends, when he said, ‘If any man love me, he will keep my word: and my father will love him, and we will come unto him, and make our abode with him.’”

With these words, and many others, full worthy of that sanctified soul and inspired tongue, did the old man comfort Ioasaph’s anguished soul. Then he sent him unto certain brethren, which abode a long way off, for to fetch the things fitting for the Holy Sacrifice. And Ioasaph girded up his loins, and with all speed fulfilled his errand: for he dreaded lest peradventure, in his absence, Barlaam might pay the debt of nature, and, yielding up the ghost to God, might inflict on him the loss of missing his departing words and utterances, his last orisons and blessings.

So when Ioasaph had manfully finished his long journey, and had brought the things required for the Holy Sacrifice, saintly Barlaam offered up to God the unbloody Sacrifice. When he had communicated himself, and also given to Ioasaph of the undefiled Mysteries of Christ, he rejoiced in the Spirit. And when they had taken together of their ordinary food, Barlaam again fed Ioasaph’s soul with edifying words, saying, “Well-beloved son, no longer in this world shall we share one common hearth and board; for now I go my last journey, even the way of my fathers. Needs must thou, therefore, prove thy loving affection for me by thy keeping of God’s commandments, and by thy continuance in this place even to the end, living as thou hast learned and been instructed, and alway remembering my poor and slothful soul. Rejoice, therefore, with great joy, and make merry with the gladness that is in Christ, because thou hast exchanged the earthly and corruptible for the eternal and incorruptible; and because there draweth nigh the reward of thy works, and thy rewarder is already at hand, who shall come to see the vineyard which thou hast dressed, and shall richly pay thee the wages of thine husbandry. ‘Faithful is the saying, and worthy of all acceptation,’ as proclaimed by Paul the divine, ‘For if we be dead with him, we shall also live with him; if we endure, we shall also reign with him in his eternal and everlasting kingdom, being illuminated with the light unapproachable, and guerdoned with the effulgence of the blessed and life-giving Trinity.’”

===Chinese===
「但你对于不出于这些的意念，却要时刻清醒警觉；要看守你心中的纯洁，如同看守极宝贵的珍宝，谨防盗贼，日日登上更高的工夫与默观，好使救主应许他朋友的话在你身上成全；他说：『人若爱我，就必遵守我的道，我父也必爱他，并且我们要到他那里去，与他同住。』」

老人就用这些话，并许多配得上那成圣灵魂与受感口舌的话，安慰了约阿萨弗伤痛的魂。随后他差他往住在远方的几位弟兄那里，去取行圣祭所需用的物品。约阿萨弗就束上腰，火速办完了差事：因为他惧怕，唯恐在他不在的时候，巴拉阿姆会偿清天赋的本债，将魂交托给神，叫他失去临别的话语与叮嘱、末后的祷告与祝福。

约阿萨弗就这样刚强壮胆地走完了漫长的路，带回圣祭所需的物品，圣洁的巴拉阿姆便向神献上不流血的祭。他自己领受了，又将基督无玷的圣奥迹分给约阿萨弗，就在灵里欢喜。他们一同用了平常的饮食以后，巴拉阿姆又用造就人的话喂养约阿萨弗的魂，说：「深爱的儿子啊，在这世上，我们不再同炉共桌了；因为如今我要走最后的旅程，就是走我列祖所走的路。所以你必须借遵守神的诫命、借在这地方坚持到底，显明你对我的爱心；要照你所学习和受教的样式生活，常常记念我这贫穷而怠惰的灵魂。所以你当满怀大喜乐，以基督里的欢乐欢庆，因为你已把属地而朽坏的换成那永恒而不朽坏的；因为你工作的赏赐临近了，那报偿你的主已经到来，他要来看你所修理的葡萄园，并丰厚地偿付你园中劳作的工价。『这话是可信的，是十分可佩服的』，如属神的保罗所宣示的：『我们若与基督同死，也必与他同活；我们若能忍耐，也必和他一同作王』，在他那永远常存的国里，受不能靠近之光的照耀，得那可称颂、赐生命的三一神荣辉的赏报。」

===Original===
Thus until eventide and all night long did Barlaam converse with Ioasaph, who wept tears that could not be stayed, and could not bear the parting. But just as day began to dawn, Barlaam ended his discourse, lifted up his hands and eyes to heaven, and offered his thanks to God, thus saying, “O Lord, my God, who art everywhere present, and fillest all things, I thank thee, for that thou hast looked upon my lowliness, and hast granted me to fulfil the course of this mine earthly pilgrimage in thy true Faith, and in the way of thy commandments. And now, thou lover of good, all-merciful Master, receive me into thine everlasting habitations; and remember not all the sins that I have committed against thee, in knowledge or in ignorance. Defend also this thy faithful servant, before whom thou hast granted to me, thine unprofitable servant, to stand. Deliver him from all vanity, and all despiteful treatment of the adversary, and set him clear of the many-meshed nets which the wicked one spreadeth abroad for to trip all them that would full fain be saved. Destroy, Almighty Lord, all the might of the deceiver from before the face of thy servant, and grant him authority to trample on the baneful head of the enemy of our souls. Send down from on high the grace of thy Holy Spirit; and strengthen him against the invisible hosts, that he may receive at thy hands the crown of victory, and that in him thy name may be glorified, the Father, the Son, and the Holy Ghost, for to thee belongeth glory and praise forever and ever. Amen.”

Thus prayed he, and in fatherly wise embraced Ioasaph, and saluted him with an holy kiss. Then he sealed himself with the sign of the Cross, and gathered up his feet, and, with exceeding great joy, as at the homecoming of friends, departed on that blessed journey, to receive his reward yonder, an old man and full of days in the Spirit.

===Chinese===
巴拉阿姆就这样与约阿萨弗交谈，直到黄昏，又终夜不止；约阿萨弗的眼泪无法止住，受不住这分离。但天色将曙的时候，巴拉阿姆结束了讲论，举起双手和双眼望天，向神献上感谢，这样说：「主我的神啊，你无所不在，充满万有，我感谢你，因你垂顾了我的卑微，又赐我在你的真信仰里、在你诫命的道路上，走完了我在世寄居的行程。如今，好善的主、满有怜悯的主啊，求你接纳我进入你永存的帐幕；我在知情或无知中所犯的一切罪，求你不要记念。也求你护佑你这忠心的仆人——你曾施恩，容我这无用的仆人站立在他面前。搭救他脱离一切的虚空，脱离仇敌一切的凌辱，救他脱出那恶者为绊倒一切切愿得救之人而四散张开的密网。全能的主啊，求你将那欺骗者的一切权势，从你仆人面前毁灭净尽，又赐他权柄，践踏我们灵魂之仇敌那有毒的头颅。求你从高天差遣你圣灵的恩典下来，坚固他抵挡那看不见的军旅，使他得以从你手中领受得胜的冠冕，并使你的名在他里面得荣耀，就是父、子、圣灵的名；因为荣耀颂赞都归于你，世世无尽。阿们。」

他这样祷告了，就以慈父的心肠拥抱约阿萨弗，以圣洁的亲吻与他问安。随后他以十字架的记号印封自己，把脚收拢，怀着极大的喜乐——如同友人还乡一般——踏上那蒙福的旅程，往那边去领受他的赏赐：一位在灵里寿高年迈、日子满足的老人。

## XL / 四十

===Original===
Then did Ioasaph embrace the good father, with all the devotion and sorrow that can be told, and washed his corpse with his tears. Then he wrapped it in the hair-shirt, which Barlaam had given him in his palace; and over him he recited the proper psalms, chanting all the day long, and throughout the night, and watering the venerable body of the Saint with his tears. On the morrow, he made a grave hard by the cave, and thither reverently bore the sacred body, and there, like a good and honourable son, laid his spiritual father in his sepulchre. And then, the fire of grief kindling all the hotter within his soul, he set himself to pray the more earnestly, saying:

“O Lord my God, hearken unto my voice, when I cry unto thee. Have mercy upon me, and hear me, for I seek thee with all my heart. My soul hath sought for thee: O hide not thy face from me, and turn not away in anger from thy servant. Be thou my helper; cast me not utterly away, and forsake me not, O God my Saviour, because my father and mother forsake me; but do thou, O Lord, take me up. Teach me thy way, O Lord, and lead me in the right way because of mine enemies. Deliver me not over unto the souls of them that afflict me; for I have been cast upon thee ever since I was born; thou art my God even from my mother’s womb. O go not from me, because, except thee, there is none to help me. For lo, I set the hope of my soul upon the ocean of thy mercies. Be thou the pilot of my soul, thou that steerest all creation with the unspeakable forethought of thy wisdom; and show thou me the way that I should walk in; and, as thou art a good God and a lover of men, save me by the prayers and intercessions of Barlaam thy servant, for thou art my God, and thee I glorify, the Father, the Son, and the Holy Ghost, world without end. Amen.”

Thus prayed he, and sat him down nigh the sepulchre, a-weeping. And as he sat, he fell asleep, and saw those dread men, whom he had seen before, coming to him, and carrying him away to the great and marvellous plain, and bringing him to that glorious and exceeding bright city. When he had passed within the gate, there met him others, gloriously apparelled with much light, having in their hands crowns radiant with unspeakable beauty, such as mortal eye hath never seen. And, when Ioasaph enquired, “Whose are these exceeding bright crowns of glory, which I see?” “Thine,” said they, “is the one, prepared for thee, because of the many souls which thou hast saved, and now made still more beautiful because of the religious life that thou leadest, if thou continue therein bravely until the end. And this other crown is thine also; but it must thou give unto thy father, who, by thy means, turned from his evil way unto the Lord, and was truly penitent.” But Ioasaph was as one sore vexed, and said, “How is it possible that, for his repentance alone, my father should receive reward equal to mine, that have laboured so much?” Thus spake he, and straightway thought that he saw Barlaam, as it were, chiding him and saying, “These are my words, Ioasaph, which I once spake unto thee, saying, ‘When thou waxest passing rich, thou wilt not be glad to distribute,’ and thou understoodest not my saying. But now, why art thou displeased at thy father’s equality with thee in honour, and art not rather glad at heart that thine orisons in his behalf have been heard?” Then Ioasaph said unto him, as he was ever wont to say, “Pardon! father, pardon! But show me where thou dwellest?” Barlaam answered, “In this mighty and exceeding fair city. It is my lot to dwell in the midmost street of the city, a street that flasheth with light supernal.” Again Ioasaph thought he asked Barlaam to bring him to his own habitation, and, in friendly wise, to show him the sights thereof. But Barlaam said that his time was not yet come to win those habitations, while he was under the burden of the flesh. “But,” said he, “if thou persevere bravely, even as I charged thee, in a little while thou shalt come hither, and gain the same habitations, and obtain the same joy and glory, and be my companion forever.” Hereupon Ioasaph awoke out of sleep, but his soul was still full of that light and ineffable glory; and greatly wondering, he raised to his Lord a song of thanksgiving.

===Chinese===
于是约阿萨弗以言语所能述说的一切敬爱与哀恸，抱住那良善的父，用眼泪洗濯他的遗体；随后用那件粗毛衣将遗体裹好——就是巴拉阿姆从前在王宫里赠给他的那件；又在他身旁诵念合宜的诗篇，终日唱诵，彻夜方休，用泪水不住浇灌圣徒那可敬的身体。次日，他在洞窟近旁掘了一座坟墓，恭恭敬敬地把圣体抬到那里，如同良善而孝敬的儿子，将他的属灵之父安放在坟墓里。此后，忧伤的火在他魂中燃得愈发炽烈，他就愈发恳切地专心祷告说：

「主我的神啊，我呼吁你的时候，求你垂听我的声音。求你怜恤我，应允我，因为我一心寻求你。我的灵魂寻求了你：求你不要向我掩面，也不要在怒中转脸远离你的仆人。求你作我的帮助；不要把我全然丢弃，不要离弃我，我救恩的神啊，因为我父母离弃我，惟愿你收留我。耶和华啊，求你将你的道指教我，因我仇敌的缘故，引导我走正直的路。不要把我交给那些苦害我的人；因为我自出母胎就被交在你手里，从我母亲生我，你就是我的神。求你不要远离我，因为除你以外，无人帮助我。看哪，我已将我灵魂的盼望，安放在你怜悯的洋海之上。求你作我灵魂的舵手——你正以你智慧那不可言喻的先见，为一切受造之物掌舵；求你将我当行的路指示我；你是良善的神、爱世人的主，求你借你仆人巴拉阿姆的祷告与代求拯救我；因为你是我的神，我荣耀你——圣父、圣子、圣灵，直到世世代代，永永远远。阿们。」

他这样祷告完了，就在坟墓旁边坐下哭泣。他坐着的时候睡着了，看见那些可畏的人——就是他从前见过的——来到他那里，把他带到那广大奇妙的平原，又领他进那座荣耀而极其明亮的城。他进了城门以后，另有几位迎着他来，身披大光，极其荣耀，手中拿着冠冕，闪耀着说不出的华美，是必死之人的眼目从未见过的。约阿萨弗问说：「我所看见的这些极其明亮的荣耀冠冕，是谁的呢？」他们说：「其中一顶是你的，是为你预备的，因你拯救了许多灵魂；如今又因你所行的修道生活而愈加美丽——只要你奋勇坚持到底。这另一顶也是你的；但你必须把它交给你的父亲，因他借着你从恶道上归向主，真心悔改了。」约阿萨弗却像被大大触恼了一般，说：「怎么可能呢——单凭悔改，我父亲所得的赏赐，竟与我这劳苦繁多的人相等？」他这样说着，立时仿佛看见巴拉阿姆在责备他，说：「约阿萨弗啊，这正是我从前对你说过的话——我说，『当你富足无比的时候，你就不再甘心施舍了』——那时你不明白我的话。如今你为何因父亲与你同得尊荣而不悦，反不为你为他献上的祷告已蒙垂听而心中欢喜呢？」约阿萨弗便照他素来惯常的口吻对他说：「恕罪，父啊，恕罪！但请你告诉我，你住在哪里？」巴拉阿姆回答说：「就在这座宏大而极其华美的城里。我所得的分，是住在城中最当中的那条街——那街闪耀着属天之光。」约阿萨弗又仿佛求巴拉阿姆带他到自己的居所，好亲亲热热地把那里的种种景象指给他看。但巴拉阿姆说，他还在肉体的重担之下，得着那些居所的时候尚未来到。他说：「但你若照我所嘱咐你的奋勇坚持，过不多时你必来到这里，得着同样的居所，获致同样的喜乐与荣耀，永远作我的同伴。」于是约阿萨弗从睡眠中醒来，他的魂仍然满载着那光亮而不可言喻的荣耀；他大为惊奇，向他的主高唱了一首感恩的颂歌。

===Original===
And he continued to the end, verily leading on earth the life of an angel, and after the death of his aged friend using himself to severer austerity. Twenty and five years old was he when he left his earthly kingdom, and adopted the monastic life; and thirty and five years in this vast desert did he, like one dis-fleshed, endure rigours above the endurance of man, but not before he had delivered the souls of many men from the soul-devouring dragon, and presented them to God, saved for aye; winning herewith the Apostolic grace. In will he had proved a martyr, and had with boldness confessed Christ before kings and tyrants, and had proved himself the mighty-voiced preacher of his greatness, and had overthrown many spirits of wickedness in the desert, and had overcome all in the strength of Christ. Partaking richly of the gift of grace from above, he kept his mind’s eye purified from every earthborn cloud, and looked forward to the things that are to come, as though they were already come. Christ was his recompense for all: Christ was his desire: Christ he ever saw as present with him: Christ and his fair beauty everywhere met his sight, according to the saying of the prophet, “I have set God always before me; for he is on my right hand, therefore I shall not fall.” And again, “My soul cleaveth to thee; thy right hand hath upholden me.” For verily Ioasaph’s soul clave to Christ, being knit to him in indissoluble union. From this marvellous work he never swerved, never altered the rule of his ascetic life, from beginning to end, but maintained his zeal from his youth even until old age; or rather, he daily advanced higher in virtue, and daily gained purer power of vision.

Thus did Ioasaph spend his days, and render unto him that called him labour worthy of his calling, having crucified the world to himself, and himself unto the world, and, at the last, departed in peace unto the God of peace, and passed to that Master whom he had alway longed for. There he appeared in the immediate presence of the Lord, and was crowned with the crown of glory already prepared for him: there it is granted to him to behold Christ, to be with Christ, to rejoice forever in the fair beauty of Christ, into whose hands he commended his spirit, when he departed to walk in the land of the living, where is the song of them that feast, the dwelling-place of them that rejoice.

As for his venerable body, it befell thus; about the very hour of Ioasaph’s death, there came by divine revelation, from one of the neighbouring cells, a certain holy man. It was the same that once pointed out to Ioasaph his way to Barlaam. This man honoured the corpse with sacred hymns, and shed tears, the token of affection, over him, and performed all the last Christian rites, and laid him in the sepulchre of his father Barlaam; for it was only meet that their bodies should rest side by side, since their souls were to dwell through eternity together.

===Chinese===
此后他持守到底，在地上实在度着天使般的生活；自他年迈的朋友去世以后，又使自己惯于更严厉的苦行。他离开地上王国、投身修道生活的时候，年二十五岁；此后三十五年，他在那广大的旷野里，如同脱去肉身之人一般，忍受了超乎人力所能忍受的严酷；只是他未走完这路程以先，已先将许多人的灵魂从那吞吃灵魂的龙口中救出，献与神，使他们永远得救，并借此赢得了使徒的恩典。他在心志上已作了殉道者，曾在君王和暴君面前放胆认信基督，证明自己是传扬他威严的大声传道者，在旷野中倾覆了许多邪恶之灵，又靠着基督的大能胜过了一切。他丰盛地有分于从上头来的恩赐，保守心思之眼洁净，脱尽一切属地的云翳，前瞻那将要来的事，如同它们已经来到。基督是他一切的报偿：基督是他的切慕：他常看基督如与自己同在：基督并他的荣美处处映入他的眼目，正如先知所说：「我将神常摆在我面前，因他在我右边，我便不至摇动。」又说：「我的灵魂紧贴于你；你的右手扶持了我。」约阿萨弗的魂实在紧贴着基督，与他结成了不能拆散的联合。这奇妙的事他从未偏离，从始至终从未更改他苦修生活的规矩，乃是从青年直到老年持守他的热心；更可以说，他天天在德行上攀升更高，天天得着更纯净的属灵视力。

约阿萨弗就这样度尽了他的岁月，向那选召他的主，献上了与蒙召相称的劳苦；他既已把世界钉在十字架上，又把自己钉在世界的十字架上，末了便在平安中离世，往那赐平安的神那里去，归到他素来长久切慕的主那里。在那里，他得以显现在主面前，戴上那早已为他预备的荣耀冠冕；在那里，他蒙恩得见基督，与基督同在，又永远欢欣于基督的荣美——他离世往活人之地去的时候，就是把自己的灵魂交在这位基督手里；那地有赴筵者的歌声，是欢乐者的居所。

至于他那可敬的遗体，事情经过是这样：约在约阿萨弗去世的那时辰，有一位圣洁之人，因着神的启示，从邻近的一间修士小室来到他那里。他正是从前向约阿萨弗指明往巴拉阿姆去的那条路的人。这人用神圣的颂歌尊荣遗体，又在他身上流下热泪，作为情爱的印证，行完了基督徒殡葬的一切礼节，把他安放在他父亲巴拉阿姆的坟墓里；因为他们二人的身体理当并排安息，他们的灵魂也必同住直到永世。

===Original===
In obedience to the strict command of a dread Angel that appeared to him in a dream, this hermit, who had performed the last rites, journeyed to the kingdom of India, and, entering in to King Barachias, made known unto him all that had befallen Barlaam, and this blessed Ioasaph. Barachias, making no delay, set forth with a mighty host, and arrived at the cave, and beheld their sepulchre, and wept bitterly over it, and raised the gravestone. There he descried Barlaam and Ioasaph lying, as they had been in life. Their bodies had not lost their former hue, but were whole and uncorrupt, together with their garments. These, the consecrated tabernacles of two holy souls, that sent forth full sweet savour, and showed naught distressful, were placed by King Barachias in costly tombs and conveyed by him into his own country.

Now when the people heard tell of that which had come to pass, there assembled a countless multitude out of all the cities and regions round about, to venerate and view the bodies of these Saints. Thereupon, sooth to say, they chanted the sacred hymns over them, and vied one with another to light lamps lavishly, and rightly and fitly, might one say, in honour of these children and inheritors of light. And with splendour and much solemnity they laid their bodies in the Church which Ioasaph had built from the very foundation. And many miracles and cures, during the translation and deposition of their relics, as also in later times, did the Lord work by his holy servants. And King Barachias and all the people beheld the mighty virtues that were shown by them; and many of the nations round about, that were sick of unbelief and ignorance of God, believed through the miracles that were wrought at their sepulchre. And all they that saw and heard of the Angelic life of Ioasaph, and of his love of God from his childhood upward, marvelled, and in all things glorified God that alway worketh together with them that love him, and granteth them exceeding great reward.

Here endeth this history, which I have written, to the best of my ability, even as I heard it from the truthful lips of worthy men who delivered it unto me. And may God grant that all we that read or hear this edifying story may obtain the heritage of such as have pleased the Lord, by the prayers and intercessions of blessed Barlaam and Ioasaph, of whom this story telleth, in Christ Jesu our Lord; to whom belongeth worship, might, majesty and glory, with the Father and the Holy Ghost, now and forevermore, world without end. Amen.

===Chinese===
这位行完殡葬之礼的隐修士，因着一位在梦中向他显现的可畏天使的严命，动身前往印度王国，进见巴拉加王，把临到巴拉阿姆和这位可称颂的约阿萨弗身上的一切事，都一一奏知他。巴拉加毫不迟延，率领一支大军启程，到了那洞窟，看见他们的坟墓，伏在上面痛哭，随后揭起墓石。他在那里看见巴拉阿姆和约阿萨弗躺着，如同生前一样。他们的身体没有失去本来的颜色，完好不朽，连衣裳也一并如此。这两具成圣的帐幕——两个圣洁的灵魂曾居于其中——发出浓郁的馨香，毫无朽坏凄惨之相；巴拉加王将他们安放在贵重的墓冢里，运回自己的本国。

百姓听见所成就的这事，就有数不尽的群众从四围各城各方聚集而来，要敬礼、瞻仰这二圣的遗体。于是，说实在的，他们向二圣唱诵神圣的颂歌，又彼此争竞，不惜大量地点燃灯火——为着尊荣这些光明之子、光明之后嗣，若容我说，这实在是合宜又恰当的。他们又以盛大隆重的威仪，将二圣的身体安放在那座教堂里，就是约阿萨弗从根基上亲手建造的。在奉迁与安放他们圣髑的时候，以及后来的年日里，主借他这两位圣仆行了许多神迹与医治。巴拉加王和众百姓都看见了借他们显出的大能作为；四围许多民族中，那些患着不信与不认识神之病的人，都因在他们坟墓那里所行的神迹归信了。凡看见或听见约阿萨弗这天使般的生活、并他自幼年以来对神之爱的人，都无不惊奇，就在凡事上荣耀神——这位神常与爱他的人同工，又赐给他们极其浩大的赏赐。

本史传至此终结。我已竭尽所能，照着从那些将此书传给我的可敬之人诚实的口中所听见的，把它写述出来。愿神赐恩，叫我们一切阅读或听闻这造就人之故事的人，都靠着我们主基督耶稣，借本故事所讲述的可称颂的巴拉阿姆与约阿萨弗的祷告与代求，得以承受那蒙主喜悦之人的基业；愿敬拜、权能、尊大与荣耀都归于他，与圣父、圣灵同归，从今以后，直到永永远远，世世无尽。阿们。

8. 拿先斯的圣格列高利（Greg. Naz.）《演讲集》（Orat.）xl. 45：*οὐκ ἔτι μὲν σάρκα, οὐκ ἀσώματον δέ, οἷς αὐτὸς οἶδε λόγοις, θεοειδεστέρου σώματος, κ.τ.λ.*（意为：「不再是肉身，却也非无躯体，乃照他自己所晓得的方式，成为更近神形的身体」云云。） ↩︎
